# Changelog

## Unreleased

- **New — `things op-result <op-id>`: recover a write's outcome from the local change history when the command was killed (RSPA1, deliverable 3).** When a harness caps command wall-time and kills a long GUI drive before it prints its result, the caller never learns what happened and is tempted to blind-retry a write that may have landed. Dispatch the write with `--op-id <key>` (single-op verbs; compounds go through `batch` with a per-line `opId`), let it die, then run `things op-result <key>` in a fresh process: it reads the audit trail the killed process already durably wrote and reports `found` (the final result + target uuid + observation), `intent-only` (the op started but no outcome was recorded — still running or the process died mid-flight, outcome UNCERTAIN, with the correlated trace path when determinable), or `unknown`. A read-only history lookup — opens no database, drives nothing, always exits 0. Exposed as a library function (`opResult`, through `src/index.ts`), the `op-result` CLI subcommand (`--json` envelope), and a read-only `op_result` MCP tool. The skill's GUI-ops + timeout guidance now teaches the recovery pattern: if your environment kills long commands, let it die and use `op-result` — never blind-retry a GUI drive. (`src/op-result.ts`, `src/cli/commands/op-result.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`, `src/cli/help.ts`, `skills/things-cli/SKILL.md`, `skills/things-cli/references/repeating.md`. Tests: `test/unit/op-result.test.ts`.)
- **Fix — `reschedule-repeat` now DERIVES and DRIVES the calendar anchor from `--when` (RSPA1, deliverable 1).** A `--when`-only reschedule (no `--yearly-month` / `--on-day` / `--weekdays`) used to drive only the deadline-shifted "Next:" field and leave the weekly/monthly/yearly anchor pop-ups untouched — so the reschedule kept the dialog's stale/default anchor while the verify (which derives the anchor from `--when`) expected the derived placement, a coherence gap behind a live yearly-reschedule failure. The reschedule compile now applies the SAME `deriveFixedAnchor` make/add-repeating apply upstream (via a shared `reschedEffParams`): a `--when`-only rule derives the anchor from the deadline-adjusted due date (`when + startDaysEarlier`) and drives the anchor pop-ups, while explicit anchor flags keep priority and a rule-only reschedule (no `--when`) drives no anchor. The verify assertions are built from the same effective params, so drive-vocabulary == assert-vocabulary is now locked by a coherence test. Live-certified on golden-v3 (Things 3.22.14) against a deadlined yearly template with a PENDING materialized instance — the exact live shape now commits in ~7s (was: a 120s zero-movement discard). (`src/write/commands.ts`. Tests: `test/engine/write-ui-vector.test.ts`. Evidence: [docs/lab/rspa1-reschedule-pending.md](docs/lab/rspa1-reschedule-pending.md).)
- **Docs — the repeating-deadline guidance now leads with the two-spellings steer for `add-repeating`.** The skill's deadline-law section, the `--deadline` / `--start-days-earlier` CLI help, and the `repeat` tool's `project_deadline` / `start_days_earlier` descriptions now say plainly: state the two dates you know, use the relative form when the intent is genuinely relative, give ONE spelling or the other (never compute one from the other), with the disagreement-refusal as the safety net. (`skills/things-cli/references/repeating.md`, `src/cli/commands/writes.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`.)
- **Performance — the dialog-class UI-drive session-reachability preflight (SESSGATE) is far cheaper on a busy desktop, with no change to what it refuses/relocates (PERF1).** The preflight enumerates AX windows across every foreground process to tell a locked/full-screen session (refuse) from a wrong-Space window (relocate) from a healthy one (proceed) — near-free on the empty lab golden but seconds-long on a large, busy real desktop, where it ran TWICE per promote composite (pre-seed gate + in-drive gate: ~15s of a ~29s add-repeating on the maintainer's host). Two behavior-preserving cuts: (1) the app-wide window walk now runs ONLY when Things has no AX-visible window — the common healthy case is already "reachable" and skips it — and, when it must run, short-circuits on the first window-bearing app instead of summing every app's windows (the discriminator only tests "any other window?" vs "none"); (2) a promote composite's two gates share a short-TTL memo of a REACHABLE verdict so they don't both probe. Not-reachable verdicts always re-probe fresh (the reveal can turn a wrong-Space window reachable) and the cleanup path keeps its own independent blindness probe, so the refuse-locked / relocate-wrong-Space / disclose semantics are byte-identical. (`src/write/vectors/session-reachability.ts`, `src/write/vectors/ui.ts`. Tests: `test/unit/session-reachability.test.ts`. Evidence: [docs/lab/perf1-drive-overhead.md](docs/lab/perf1-drive-overhead.md).)
- **Performance — a repeating-rule GUI drive fills its date fields far faster, and the post-reveal settle is trimmed, with byte-identical results (PERF2).** Setting the "Next: (first occurrence)" / "Ends: on date" / reminder date fields located the controls by walking Things' ENTIRE Accessibility tree — which, on a large library, descended the main window's whole task list and cost ~4.4s for the first-occurrence field alone. The driver now finds the small Repeat-dialog shell first and searches only inside it (the same attached-sheet-vs-detached-window shapes the rest of the drive already uses), so the search skips the list entirely: on the lab golden the first-occurrence field drops from 619ms to 312ms (−50%), and the removed descent is the multi-second cost on a busy real desktop. The one-time settle after the item is revealed is trimmed 1500→1000ms (the menu bar was measured repopulating in ~92ms; the trim keeps a wide host-headroom margin, and under-margining only ever fails closed to a re-runnable refusal). Behavior is unchanged: the date fields still target deterministically, still read back and fail loudly on a rejected write, and still raise the same named error when a field is absent — full-vocabulary make-repeating and a Next+ends-on drive both land byte-identical database results in the lab. Reduce Motion + disabling window animations roughly halve dialog-present time and are documented as standing configuration for a dedicated automation host (never baked into the certification environment). (`src/write/vectors/ui.ts`. Tests: `test/unit/ui-scripts.test.ts`. Evidence: [docs/lab/perf2-step-latency.md](docs/lab/perf2-step-latency.md).)
- **Added — `todo add-repeating` accepts `--start-days-earlier <n>` as an alternative to a concrete `--deadline <date>`, and refuses the two when they disagree.** The deadline geometry can now be named from either end: `--deadline <date>` gives the first occurrence's due date (the per-occurrence lead is derived as `deadline − --when`), or `--start-days-earlier N` names the lead directly (the deadline is derived as `--when + N`). Both map onto the RULE and keep the seed deadline-free. Giving BOTH is over-determined — there is no per-first-occurrence gap to absorb a mismatch — so they must agree (`deadline − when == N`): an exact match proceeds, a disagreement is refused up front (zero mutation) with a message naming both corrected spellings. `--start-days-earlier` needs a concrete `--when` and does not apply to after-completion series. `project add-repeating` is unchanged (its `--deadline` is the project's own due date). (`src/write/promote-clone.ts`, `src/write/operations.ts`, `src/cli/commands/writes.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`. Tests: `test/engine/write-promote-clone.test.ts`, `test/cli/repeat-flags.test.ts`.)
- **Fixed — `add-repeating --deadline <date>` now gives every occurrence the deadline (and no longer double-books the first occurrence); `make-repeating` on a future deadlined item no longer leaves a duplicate (DBLSPAWN1).** A concrete item-level `--deadline` on `todo add-repeating` used to (1) land on the seed only and get DROPPED from the series — you had to follow up with a separate `reschedule-repeat --deadline --start-days-earlier` to actually deadline the occurrences — and (2), because a deadlined item is preserved by the app when it becomes repeating, leave the future-scheduled seed as a **materialized instance** the series then **duplicated when the date arrived** (DB-verified on golden-v3 / Things 3.22.14: two occurrences for the same day). Now `add-repeating` maps the concrete `--deadline` straight onto the RULE — deriving "start `deadline − when` days earlier" — so every spawned occurrence carries its deadline and the seed stays deadline-free (no preserve, no duplicate). A `--deadline` needs a concrete `--when` (the offset is `deadline − start`) and must be on or after it, else it is refused with a clear message; after-completion series are unchanged. `make-repeating` (and `add-repeating` as a backstop) now also detect when the app kept the promoted item as a future-dated first occurrence and move that redundant occurrence to the Trash — disclosed in `warnings[]` — so the series mints a single occurrence when the date arrives. The underlying app double-spawn (which a pure-GUI user hits too) is documented for Cultured Code in oddities §9ff. (`src/write/promote-clone.ts`, `src/write/vectors/simulator.ts`, `src/write/repeat-anchor.ts`. Tests: `test/engine/{write-promote-clone,write-simulator}.test.ts`. Evidence: [docs/lab/dblspawn1-preserved-instance.md](docs/lab/dblspawn1-preserved-instance.md).)

- **Added — an off-schedule FIRST occurrence is now a first-class, disclosed pattern; the one shape the app can't express (a monthly first off its anchor day) is refused cleanly (DACON1).** A concrete `--when` alongside an explicit calendar anchor can DISAGREE — e.g. `--frequency weekly --weekdays wednesday --when <a Thursday>` asks for a Thursday first occurrence with Wednesdays thereafter. The Repeat dialog's "Next:" field supports exactly this, and in-lab probing (golden-v3 / Things 3.22.14) confirms the app HONORS an off-rule first for **weekly** and **yearly** rules — deadlined or not — landing the typed `--when` verbatim as the first instance and following the anchor from the second occurrence on. `make-repeating` / `add-repeating` / `reschedule-repeat` now ALLOW this and DISCLOSE both halves of the landed pattern in `warnings[]` and `--dry-run` (`off-rule first occurrence — appears <when>[, due <when+N>]; thereafter <rule pattern>[, appearing N days earlier]`), and the reschedule verify asserts the rule anchor without demanding the cursor equal `--when` (an off-rule cursor lands on the next aligned occurrence). In DEADLINE mode `--when` is still the start and the anchor names the DUE date, so the off-rule test is deadline-shift-aware (compares the anchor to `--when + start-days-earlier`). The one shape the app does NOT honor is a **monthly** first occurrence off the anchor day — the month row snaps "Next:" to the anchor's day-of-month (e.g. a day-10 `--when` under a day-20 anchor commits day 20) — so that combination is now **refused at validation** with the two nearest expressible alternatives (put `--when` on the anchor day, or omit the anchor to take it from `--when`), instead of failing mid-drive with a rolled-back seed. (`src/write/repeat-anchor.ts` [`assessOffRuleFirst`], `src/write/{repeat-rule,promote-clone,commands,pipeline}.ts`, `src/cli/commands/writes.ts`. Tests: `test/unit/{repeat-anchor,repeat-rule}.test.ts`. Evidence: [docs/lab/dacon1-deadline-contradiction.md](docs/lab/dacon1-deadline-contradiction.md); oddities §9ee.)
- **Fixed — a GUI-drive verify no longer burns its full timeout budget on a write that settled on a stable wrong value (DACON1 poller early-exit).** When a driven change moved an asserted field but to the WRONG value, and that value then held unchanged, the verify poller waited out the entire budget (the live-host 120s reschedule) before reporting the mismatch. The poller now returns `mismatch` early once the same mismatching observation repeats for ≥3 consecutive polls spanning ≥5s AFTER movement — a settled wrong write cannot become right by waiting. It is deliberately conservative: ANY change to the observed values resets the window (never fires while state is still converging), it never converts a would-converge case, it only ever returns the SAME classification the deadline would have (an asserted-field mismatch), and it is inert for budgets shorter than the window — the tripwire (timeout) and silent-noop paths are untouched. (`src/write/verify/poller.ts`. Tests: `test/unit/verify-poller.test.ts`.)
## 0.17.0 — 2026-08-18

- **Fixed — RRD1: `reschedule-repeat` on an already-deadlined (or already-remindered) series no longer breaks by blind-toggling the dialog's checkboxes.** The Repeat dialog opens PRE-POPULATED on a reschedule — a deadlined rule opens with "Add deadlines" already ticked — but the recipe pressed the checkbox unconditionally, so an already-correct box was flipped the WRONG way: rescheduling a deadlined monthly template with `--deadline --start-days-earlier 21` UNCHECKED "Add deadlines", hid the "start N days earlier" field it had already revealed, and collapsed the drive ("none of its expected element shapes resolved"). The checkboxes now converge through a new deterministic closed-loop `ensure-checkbox` step (read the box's state, press ONLY when it differs from the requested target, re-read to confirm — never a blind toggle), applied to both "Add deadlines" and "Add reminders". The deadline checkbox is converged BEFORE the "Next:" field is driven, since deadline mode changes what "Next:" means (it becomes the deadline date, YANCH1 #493) — the deadline-date shift must be computed against the converged state. **Preserve-unspecified law:** an unspecified `--deadline` / `--reminder` on a reschedule emits NO checkbox step, so the pre-populated state is PRESERVED (requested-fields-only, #492) — a rule-only reschedule leaves a deadline/reminder exactly as it was; only an explicit `--deadline false` un-ticks the box. Certified in-lab on golden-v3 (deadlined-reschedule, deadline-preserve, reminder-preserve, idempotent re-run).

- **Fixed — #493: `make-repeating` / `add-repeating` on a fixed monthly or yearly series no longer silently lands the dialog's default anchor (January 1 / the 1st of the month), and a deadlined series now starts on the requested date with its deadline correctly offset.** When a fixed monthly/yearly rule was requested with a first-occurrence date but no explicit `--on-day`/`--on-weekday`/`--yearly-month` anchor, the recipe drove ONLY the "Next:" field — which fixes the FIRST occurrence but leaves the recurring rule on the dialog's untouched default (yearly → January 1, monthly → the 1st), so a `--when 2027-10-16 --frequency yearly` series recurred every January 1. The drive now DERIVES the calendar anchor from the requested date (weekly → weekday, monthly → day-of-month, yearly → month+day — the same law the read-path decoder uses) and drives the anchor pop-ups explicitly, completing the derive-and-drive family the weekly weekday-derivation began. Three coupled deadline fixes ride along: (1) a rule-level `--deadline` / `--start-days-earlier` on `make-repeating` was **silently dropped** (the shared promote helper copied only the add-repeating field set), landing a NON-deadlined series — now carried through; (2) a deadlined rule anchors on the DEADLINE and each instance STARTS `start-days-earlier` days before it, so `--when` (the scheduled start) is driven into the dialog as `when + start-days-earlier` and the app back-shifts the start to `--when` — a `--when 2027-10-16 --deadline --start-days-earlier 14` series now starts October 16 with a deadline October 30, recurring yearly (was: a January-1 series with no deadline); (3) the post-drive verify asserts the derived-or-explicit anchor (a dropped default anchor is now a `verify-failed:mismatch`, not a silent `ok`) and the first-occurrence START (accounting for the deadline offset). The `set-datetime` primitive is hardened: it can no longer surface a raw `-[__NSArray0 objectAtIndex:]` (-2700) crash when the target date control is absent (it throws a NAMED error reporting the dialog's date-area inventory), and it READS THE CONTROL BACK after each write, failing loudly when the control rejected the value (the macOS error beep) instead of committing a garbled default. The promote ok-result now ECHOES the landed cadence + first-occurrence + deadline offset so a caller can eyeball what committed. **Skill + CLI hardening:** the `--dangerously-drive-gui` guidance now spells out a ≥180s wrapper timeout and the cardinal rule that a timeout / empty output / silent no-op is NOT success — re-read `things show <uuid>` (and the dev trace) before retrying, never fire an identical retry, and stop-and-file-a-bug on wrong/ambiguous state. Certified in-lab on golden-v3 / Things 3.22.14: the exact reported shape now lands correctly (or, before the deadline-shift, fails closed — never a silent wrong series). (`src/write/repeat-anchor.ts` [`deriveFixedAnchor`/`deriveMonthlyAnchor`/`deriveYearlyAnchor`/`deadlineDriveNext`], `src/write/promote-clone.ts` [`ruleParamsFor` deadline carry, deadline-shift, `landedRuleEcho`], `src/write/commands.ts` [`reschedRuleExtras`], `src/write/repeat-asserts.ts`, `src/write/vectors/ui.ts` [`axSetDateTimeScript`], `src/cli/commands/writes.ts`, `skills/things-cli/SKILL.md`. Tests: `test/unit/{repeat-anchor,repeat-asserts,ui-scripts}.test.ts`. Evidence: [docs/lab/yanch1-derived-anchor.md](docs/lab/yanch1-derived-anchor.md); ruling [docs/design/decisions.md](docs/design/decisions.md) 2026-08-17.)
- **Fixed — #491: rescheduling a repeating series that changes ONLY the monthly anchor, weekday set, deadline offset, ends bound, or first-occurrence date no longer reports a false "idempotent no-op" — the change now actually applies.** `reschedule-repeat`'s expected-delta asserted only the rule's unit + interval (the RSIM5-era shallow subset), so the pre-drive idempotency check declared "already in the requested state" whenever unit+interval matched — even when the calendar anchor, `start-days-earlier`, ends bound, or requested `--when` differed — returning `ok` + an "idempotent no-op" warning at exit 0 with NO drive (and the post-drive verify, using the same shallow asserts, would have passed a drive that landed the wrong anchor). The fix builds the expected-rule assertion set from the COMPLETE requested vocabulary (rule type · unit · interval · calendar anchor [weekly weekdays / monthly day-or-nth-weekday / yearly month+anchor, compared order-insensitively against the decoded rule] · ends bound [`ends-after`→occurrence-count total, `ends-on`→end date, per RRX1] · `start-days-earlier` [ts] · deadline flag · and, when `--when` is given, the ANCH2 first-occurrence cursor). The idempotency precheck now skips the drive ONLY when EVERY requested field already holds; a genuine same-command re-run is still a zero-drive no-op. **Requested-fields-only law:** a field the caller did not set is not asserted, so a bare `--frequency weekly --interval 2` reschedule never trips on an untouched ends bound. The same full-fidelity depth extends to `make-repeating`/`add-repeating`'s post-drive verify (previously `repeating.isTemplate` only, with deep checks living only in external campaign certs): a template minted with a dropped anchor / ends / deadline is now a `verify-failed:mismatch` naming the successor uuid for cleanup, not a silent `ok`. **Structural hardening (maintainer-ratified):** the assert set is built from an EXHAUSTIVE map over the rule vocabulary (`Record<keyof …, …>` — every field is either an assert-producer or an explicit skip with a written reason, e.g. `reminder` is skipped because it is not stored in the rule blob), so a field added to the rule params breaks compilation until it is consciously handled; a discrimination property test proves the law the types cannot (for two bags sharing a requested-field footprint but differing in ≥1 value, the asserts from A are UNSATISFIED against the state from B — exactly what #491 lacked), with coherence + requested-fields-only + mapper-completeness companions. **Also closes UIC6-l:** the CLI mapper silently DROPPED a wrong-frequency calendar-anchor flag (`--frequency weekly --on-day 15` ran a plain weekly rule); it now REFUSES it, naming the flag and the frequencies it belongs to, and the flag→param mapping is likewise an exhaustive map so an accepted-but-dropped flag is impossible. (`src/write/repeat-asserts.ts` new, `src/model/recurrence.ts` [`decodeOffsetEntry`/`anchorKeyOfOffsets`], `src/write/{commands,verify/delta}.ts`, `src/write/vectors/simulator.ts` [reschedule honors `--when`], `src/cli/commands/repeat-flags.ts`. Tests: `test/unit/repeat-asserts.test.ts` (discrimination/coherence/requested-fields-only/fuzz), `test/engine/write-ui-vector.test.ts` (precheck drive-vs-no-drive cells), `test/cli/repeat-flags.test.ts` (UIC6-l refusal + mapper-completeness). Evidence: [docs/lab/rrf1-full-rule-asserts.md](docs/lab/rrf1-full-rule-asserts.md); ruling [docs/design/decisions.md](docs/design/decisions.md) 2026-08-17.)
- **Fixed — #487: a GUI-driving write no longer returns EMPTY output when it runs long or is interrupted; it now gives up first, or reports an honest, uncertain-outcome result — plus a dev-mode step timeline for diagnosing hangs (TRACE1).** On the maintainer's large, syncing production database, `make-repeating --dangerously-drive-gui --json` filled the Repeat dialog correctly but the drive ran past the caller's own 30-second timeout; the caller killed the process and got NO json, no error, no exit code (the template still landed moments later). Three fixes address the whole failure chain regardless of which link dominates: (1) **A signal handler** — a SIGTERM/SIGINT mid-write now flushes a final trace entry and emits a structured `interrupted` result that says the outcome is UNCERTAIN (the in-flight UI step may still complete) and to re-check with `things show <ref>` before retrying, instead of the silent empty stdout. (2) **An in-CLI drive watchdog** — the app-driving vector now enforces an overall UI-drive budget (default 90s, `things config set ui-drive-budget-ms <ms>` / `THINGS_API_UI_DRIVE_BUDGET_MS`), so the CLI gives up FIRST — clearing any open dialog via the SESSGATE close+reopen maneuver and returning a structured `verify-failed`/timeout flagged `uncertain` with the trace path — before a caller's own timeout can. Because a production DB commits the dialog several times slower than the lab golden, the default is the lab-measured drive time multiplied by a wide safety factor; callers are told (CLI help + skill) to allow ≥120s so the watchdog always wins. (3) **Dev-mode trace telemetry** — a development checkout (a `-dev` build, or `things config set trace true`) writes a per-invocation step timeline to `~/.local/state/things-api/trace/<stamp>-<pid>.jsonl`: sanitized argv, pipeline stages, every UI-drive osascript hop with duration + outcome, the verify summary, and the final result/exit code — so one file reconstructs an exact hang. Overhead is ≈0 when off; the file is LOCAL-ONLY (it may hold real task titles) and never ships to the repo or an issue. **Orphan policy (deliberate):** the drive already runs one osascript per step, so a kill stops it at a step boundary; Node does not kill its child on its own death, so the single in-flight osascript completes as an orphan (this is why the template still landed) — the trace's last checkpoint plus the interrupt marker record exactly where. No detached processes are created. The maintainer's separately-proposed cause — post-drive polling for a future-dated first occurrence — was FALSIFIED (the discovery tolerates a null instance and the verify budget is hard-capped) and is locked by a regression test. Lab baseline (golden-v3): a full make-repeating drive is ~5.1s osascript / ~7.4s total, so the 90s default is a wide ~12× safety factor over the airgapped golden. (`src/trace/tracer.ts` new, `src/cli/interrupt.ts` new; `src/write/vectors/{ui,types}.ts`, `src/write/pipeline.ts`, `src/{config,contracts,index,paths}.ts`, `src/cli/{main,commands/writes,help}.ts`, `skills/things-cli/SKILL.md`. Tests: `test/unit/trace.test.ts`, `test/cli/interrupt.test.ts`, `test/engine/write-ui-watchdog.test.ts`, `test/unit/repeat-discovery.test.ts`. Evidence: [docs/lab/trace1-drive-timings.md](docs/lab/trace1-drive-timings.md); ruling [docs/design/decisions.md](docs/design/decisions.md) 2026-08-17.)
- **Added — completing or canceling a to-do now reports the remaining OPEN work in its container(s) (HINTS1).** A successful `todo complete` / `todo cancel` (single op AND each applicable line inside `batch`) gains a small `context` object so an agent caller can notice an emptied container without a second read. When the to-do was in a **project** (directly or under a heading), `context.project = { uuid, title, remainingOpen }` reports the OPEN, untrashed, non-template to-dos left in that project (heading children flatten into the project count; heading rows never count) — `remainingOpen: 0` means the project's open work is now empty. When the to-do was a member of **Today** at mutation time (any arm — a scheduled arrival, an evening item, or a due/overdue deadline pull), `context.today = { remainingOpen }` reports the OPEN Today members left. Both can appear; both are absent when neither applies (and on every other op — `reopen`, `delete`, `move`, `undo`, `project.complete` are deliberately out of scope). The hint only INFORMS — an agent seeing `remainingOpen: 0` on a project can decide to complete the project too, but nothing auto-acts. The count is computed from a cheap post-verify re-read via the library's own read paths (the Today count reuses the one Today-view membership law); a to-do in neither a project nor Today costs zero extra reads. The `--json` envelope and the MCP tool result carry `data.context`; the human render adds a dim `project "X": N open remaining` / `today: N open remaining` line under the ok line. Repeating note: a successor instance that materializes into the project/Today after completion is honest present state and IS counted. (`src/write/completion-context.ts` new, `src/write/{pipeline,commands,pre-state}.ts`, `src/cli/commands/writes.ts`; contract [docs/design/contracts.md](docs/design/contracts.md). Maintainer-requested, design ratified.)
- **Fixed — a normal write no longer silently no-ops when Things is not running; a closed app is launched-and-readied first, or cleanly refused (issue #486).** A write dispatched into a not-running Things was accepted but dropped during the app's startup window, surfacing as `verify-failed:silent-noop` (exit 3) — ordinary operational state reading as an unexplained mutation failure. Now a write's preflight detects a closed app (one shared `pgrep -x Things3` shape with `doctor`), and **by default launches Things in the background (`open -g`, no focus steal) and waits CLOSED-LOOP for it to become ready to land a write** before dispatching and verifying normally; the launch is disclosed in `warnings[]`. A new **`auto-launch` config key** (default on; `things config set auto-launch false` / `THINGS_API_AUTO_LAUNCH`) flips this to a preflight **`blocked` environment refusal with ZERO dispatch** ("Things is not running, and auto-launch is turned off") for a host that must never surface the app on its own. Failure attribution gained an **`app-not-running`** likely-cause: any residual silent-noop/timeout while the app had to be launched now reads "the command ran during the app's startup window … retry now that Things is up" instead of the generic app-behavior mystery — the reported scenario can never again be unexplained. **Reads are unaffected** (they hit the database directly and never launch anything). Certified end-to-end on golden-v3 / Things 3.22.14: closed-app write auto-launches and lands with the disclosure; auto-launch-off refuses with zero dispatch and the app stays quit. (The URL-drop window did not reproduce in a clean airgapped clone — first URL landed immediately — so the readiness wait is a defensive floor keyed to the app writing to its DB after launch; the launch uses the least-disruptive `open -g`.) (`src/write/{pipeline,failure-hints,automation-probe}.ts`, `src/config.ts`, `src/cli/{help,commands/writes}.ts`. Tests: `test/engine/write-pipeline.test.ts`, `test/unit/{failure-hints,config}.test.ts`, `test/cli/config-get-cli.test.ts`. Evidence: [docs/lab/apprun1-launch-readiness.md](docs/lab/apprun1-launch-readiness.md); ruling [docs/design/decisions.md](docs/design/decisions.md) 2026-08-17.)
- **Fixed — the TRUE cause of #480: dialog-class GUI ops now check that a Things window is reachable on the current desktop, refusing a locked/full-screen session cleanly and relocating an off-desktop window (SESSGATE).** The root cause was found live: when the Mac's screen is **locked**, or a **full-screen app** covers the desktop, the Repeat / Convert / Move dialog opens on a window the automation layer cannot see — so the drive hangs waiting for the dialog, the stuck dialog then **freezes app-wide writes** (the #480 auto-trash that silently did nothing), and the cleanup falsely reported the dialog "dismissed". This is the SESSION-STATE divergence the earlier "does not reproduce on clean 3.22.14" investigations (#479/#480, golden-v3) could not explain. Every dialog-class op (`make-repeating`/`reschedule-repeat`, `add-repeating`, `convert-to-project`, `promote-heading`, `move-heading-to-project`, to-do AND project) now runs a **session-reachability check** after selecting the target and before any change, with a three-way response: **reachable** → proceed (works with the app in the background too); **the Things window is on another desktop** → automatically bring it to the current desktop, re-check, and proceed, disclosing the move in a warning; **the screen is locked / a full-screen app is covering the desktop** → refuse with `blocked` (exit 4) and a plain-language remediation (unlock the Mac / leave the full-screen app), touching nothing. The `make-repeating`/`add-repeating` compounds run the check **before they seed their copy**, so a locked session never leaves an orphan to-do behind. Pure menu ops that work under lock (`pause-repeat`/`resume-repeat`) are deliberately NOT gated. The post-failure cleanup is now **honest**: when it cannot see the app's windows it never claims a dialog was dismissed — it runs the proven "close the window and reopen it" recovery (which clears a stuck dialog and unfreezes writes without needing the accessibility layer) and says so, which is what makes the seed auto-trash / rollback actually land. In-lab re-certified on golden-v3 / Things 3.22.14: the locked signature refuses with zero mutation; the unlocked drive still creates the series (no regression); the off-desktop recovery restores a reachable window. (`src/write/vectors/session-reachability.ts` new, `src/write/vectors/{ui,ui-recipes,types,registry}.ts`, `src/write/{promote-clone,pipeline,guards}.ts`. Tests: `test/unit/session-reachability.test.ts`, `test/unit/repeat-recipe.test.ts`, `test/engine/{write-ui-vector,write-promote-clone}.test.ts`. Evidence: [docs/lab/sessgate-session-reachability.md](docs/lab/sessgate-session-reachability.md); ruling [docs/design/decisions.md](docs/design/decisions.md) 2026-08-16; oddities §9cc/§9dd.)
- **Certified against Things 3.22.14 (golden advanced to `things-lab-golden-v3`).** The lab golden was advanced 3.22.12 → 3.22.14 by the drift-runbook DRIFT-1 in-place swap (schema + sdef byte-identical throughout — a BEHAVIORAL-only update; fingerprint `sha256:784bd2f6…`, DB v26, no new baseline). `lab:regress` ran GREEN across every suite (u/a/x/o/r/e/p/s) + the write-layer e2e with zero verdict/tier/crash flips vs the 3.22.12 baseline; the schedule-class template crashes (U12/R09) still reproduce, so the `H-REPEAT-SCHEDULE` guards stay; the repeat-rule blob corpus decodes byte-identical with `rrv=4` unchanged; the shipped #476/#480/#483 repeat defenses were re-certified under 3.22.14. **The two reported 3.22.14 field regressions do NOT reproduce in a clean airgapped 3.22.14 lab clone** — #479 (checklist-on-template) works, verifies, and propagates; #480 (add-repeating) passes the full repro matrix with the Repeat dialog appearing and Next/reminder driven — so NO version-gated refusal or reveal fix ships (either would break a working, verified feature); the anomaly (host reports not lab-reproducible under the exact version — candidate differentiators: active Things Cloud sync, production-DB/window state, MAS vs direct build channel) is documented for follow-up. A pre-existing staleness in the e2e guest driver (`lab/guest/e2e-write-smoke.sh` used the removed `todo backdate`/`todo add-logged` commands) was fixed to the current `todo update`/`todo add --created-at/--completed-at` vocabulary. (`docs/lab/golden-v3-metadata.json`, `docs/lab/gv3-certification.md`, `lab/runner/run.ts`, `lab/scripts/{regress,e2e-write-smoke}.sh`; ruling [docs/design/decisions.md](docs/design/decisions.md) 2026-08-16.)
- **Fixed — an exhausted repeating series now reads as `ended`, not `waiting`/`scheduled`.** A fixed repeat that has run out its `--ends-after N` count (or passed its `--ends-on` date) is ended by the app CLEARING its next-occurrence cursor; `templateStatus` now reads exhaustion from that cursor (a fixed template with no next occurrence is `ended`), replacing an unreachable `remainingCount === 0` test. Probed reality (RRX1, golden-v2 / 3.22.12): `--ends-after N` writes an IMMUTABLE total count that never decrements (the app counts spawns separately and stops by clearing the cursor), so the decoded rule field is renamed `RepeatRule.remainingCount` → `occurrenceCount` (the configured "ends after N" total, not a remaining tally; `null` = unlimited). (`src/model/{recurrence,occurrences}.ts`, `src/write/{repeat-rule,promote-clone}.ts`; evidence [docs/lab/rrx1-rc-and-reminder.md](docs/lab/rrx1-rc-and-reminder.md).)
- **Fixed — `todo clear-reminder` on a repeating template now refuses cleanly instead of failing verification.** On Things 3.22.12 a repeating template's rule reminder is a real `reminderTime` column value (RRX1 — contradicting the earlier 3.22.11 finding), but no automation surface can clear it in place (the Shortcuts clear is a silent no-op, the AppleScript de-schedule is refused, the URL bounce crashes). The op now refuses a repeating template outright (`blocked:H-REPEAT-SCHEDULE`, "change it in the app's repeat editor") for every vector, where before the non-null column let it route to the no-op Shortcuts path and verify-fail. A reminderless template is still caught by `H-NO-REMINDER`. (`src/write/clear-reminder.ts`.)
- **Fixed — `add-repeating` fails EARLY + NAMED when the reveal did not land an eligible selection, and AUTO-TRASHES its own seed when the promote fails (issue #480).** Two distinct bugs from the field report (Things 3.22.14): (1) the ui drive died opaquely at "the Repeat dialog never appeared" whenever the promote's `things:///show?id=` reveal navigated without leaving the to-do row selected — an AXPress on the resulting DISABLED `Items ▸ Repeat…` is a silent no-op. The to-do make/add-repeating recipe now runs a positive **eligibility assertion** before the press: it reads `id of selected to dos` (uuid-precise) and requires EXACTLY the target selected, then checks the menu item's `AXEnabled`, aborting with a NAMED diagnostic (`NOTSEL…`/`WRONGSEL…`/`DISABLED…`) instead of a downstream timeout. (2) A failed `add-repeating` left the seeded to-do behind as residue whose reported uuid was not reachable through `todo delete`. The composite now **auto-trashes its own seed** inside the txn when the promote fails (recreatable from the args; the Trash is recoverable) and discloses it with a `restore` remediation; if the auto-trash itself fails, the result carries the seed's REAL, resolvable uuid with a working `things todo delete <uuid>` remediation. Mirrored on `project.add-repeating` (shared code). **The `verify-failed:silent-noop` itself is a suspected Things 3.22.14 behavior change** — the full repro matrix (bare/+area/+tag/+area+tag/+reminder/full) all PASS on golden-v2 / 3.22.12, so no root-cause reveal fix is shippable from the golden (maintainer golden decision; same 3.22.14 split as #479/CHKT1). (`src/write/{promote-clone.ts}`, `src/write/vectors/{ui,ui-recipes,types}.ts`. Tests: `test/engine/write-ui-vector.test.ts`, `test/engine/write-promote-clone.test.ts`, `test/unit/{ui-scripts,repeat-recipe}.test.ts`. Evidence: [docs/lab/adr1-add-repeating-reveal.md](docs/lab/adr1-add-repeating-reveal.md); ruling [docs/design/decisions.md](docs/design/decisions.md) 2026-08-16.)
- **Fixed — `todo add-repeating --reminder HH:mm` now carries the reminder onto the SERIES instead of dropping it (issue #480, requested behavior #3).** The create leg set a one-off `reminderTime` on the seed to-do, but the Repeat-dialog conversion does not preserve it — the dialog OWNS the repeat reminder via its "Add reminders" control — so the base reminder was silently lost from the template (verified empty on golden-v2). `add-repeating` now threads the base `--reminder` into the promote so the dialog's reminder picker is driven, committing it to the template's `reminderTime` and every spawned occurrence (ANCH2: the picker commits deterministically). (`src/write/promote-clone.ts`. Test: `test/engine/write-promote-clone.test.ts` — the promote leg's rule params carry the base reminder.)
- **Fixed — `make-repeating` / `add-repeating` / `reschedule-repeat` now HONOR the requested first occurrence (issue #476).** The Things Repeat dialog has a "Next:" first-occurrence date field (ANCH2, golden-v2 / 3.22.12); its default is the today-anchored next match, but it is editable and honored. The promote verbs now DRIVE it with the requested first occurrence — make-repeating: the item's scheduled date (or an explicit `--when` override); add-repeating: `--when`; reschedule-repeat: the new `--when` — so a fixed series starts on the requested date verbatim (subsequent occurrences follow the rule), across daily/weekly/**monthly/yearly** and to-do AND project. A weekly rule with `--weekdays` omitted also derives the recurring weekday from that date (not the app's Sunday default). After the drive, the landed first occurrence is verified fail-closed. The former fail-closed refusal (`H-REPEAT-ANCHOR`) is DELETED — its premise ("no drivable first-occurrence control") was false. (`src/write/{repeat-anchor,promote-clone,repeat-rule}.ts`, `src/write/vectors/{ui,ui-recipes,simulator}.ts`, `src/cli/commands/repeat-flags.ts`; evidence [docs/lab/anch2-next-field.md](docs/lab/anch2-next-field.md).)
- **Fixed — a recurring `--reminder` time is now set instead of refused (issue #476 item 4).** Things' repeat-dialog reminder-time picker was thought undrivable (UIC6-g), but that was a targeting artifact: the old `set-datetime` primitive wrote into "the first date area by role" — which a fixed rule makes ambiguous (Next / Ends / reminder can all be present). `set-datetime` now targets its control deterministically, so `--reminder HH:mm` commits the requested time exactly (re-cert RC5: 18:00). This also fixes the `--ends-on` "series-collapse" (former app-oddity §8v, now retracted as our defect): the Ends date and the Next field are driven distinctly with no cross-contamination. (`src/write/vectors/{ui,ui-recipes}.ts`, `src/write/repeat-rule.ts`.)
- **Fixed — `--after-completion` with any end bound (`--ends-on` OR `--ends-after`) is refused during validation, before any GUI side effect (issue #476 item 5).** After-completion mode doesn't expose the Ends control the fixed schedule does, so neither an end date nor an occurrence count could be applied. The combination is rejected up front with an actionable message; `--after-completion` with no end (the default) is unaffected. (`src/write/repeat-rule.ts` `assertRepeatRule`.)

## 0.16.0 — 2026-08-14

- **Changed (BREAKING) — `things batch` now performs a STATIC preflight: any statically-invalid line refuses the WHOLE batch before anything runs.** A line checkable with no app state (torn/non-object line, missing/unknown op, a `make-repeating`/`add-repeating` compound, non-object `params`, a malformed `opId`, a `$ref` naming a `tempId` no EARLIER line declares — unknown or forward — or a duplicate `tempId`) rejects the entire submission up front, enumerating EVERY offending line, and dispatches nothing (dry-run runs the same pass). Previously most of these failed only the offending line while independent later lines ran. Only RUNTIME failures (guard block, verify-failed, unsupported vector, a ref to an earlier leg that bound nothing) keep per-line semantics. (`src/write/batch.ts` `preflightBatch`, `src/cli/commands/writes.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`; ruling [docs/design/decisions.md](docs/design/decisions.md) 2026-08-14.)
- **Changed (BREAKING) — stop-on-failure is now the DEFAULT for `things batch`; `--continue-on-error` (MCP `continue_on_error`) opts into the old proceed-past behavior.** A runtime per-line failure HALTS the batch: the failed line is reported and every later line is reported not-run. The summary now carries resume guidance — the not-run count plus whether a verbatim resubmission is safe (safe when every committed line carried an `opId`; otherwise it names the non-idempotent line indices that would re-run). The old opt-in `--fail-fast` / `fail_fast` / library `failFast` flag is DELETED (ALPHA-CONTRACT — no alias). Put an `opId` on every line so a stopped batch can be resubmitted verbatim to resume.
- **Changed (BREAKING) — `undo --last`/`--by` now SKIP already-undone changes, so repeated `undo --last N` walks progressively deeper into history.** Selection excludes records already reversed (derived from the trail's inverse records), so `--last N` means "the N newest not-yet-undone mutations" — running it twice advances into older history instead of re-selecting the same records. IRREVERSIBLE changes are NOT skipped: they still count toward N and are reported every pass, so you see them rather than silently reaching past them. `--txn` is unchanged (surgical; loud already-undone error). (`src/write/undo.ts` `selectUndoTargets`; ruling [docs/design/decisions.md](docs/design/decisions.md) 2026-08-14.)
- **Docs — the batch/undo semantics (above) now read consistently across the consumer covenant and the design contract.** A copy-only alignment pass over the just-shipped batch static-preflight / stop-on-failure-default / undo-skips-already-undone laws: `docs/contract.md`'s partial-failure covenant now states the batch STATIC preflight (a statically-invalid line refuses the whole batch before anything runs) and the stop-on-failure DEFAULT with resume guidance, replacing the pre-change "runs its operations independently" wording that implied a failure never halts later lines; `docs/design/contracts.md` gains an explicit undo-selection paragraph (`undo --last`/`--by` skip already-undone records — `--last N` = the N newest not-yet-undone mutations; irreversible records still count toward N and are reported; the append-only `undoOf` ledger; `--txn` surgical + loud already-undone error). The CLI `--help`, MCP tool descriptions, the skill, and the decisions register already stated these laws consistently. No wire or behavior change.
- **Added — `todo clone` / `project clone` of a repeating TEMPLATE now clones it as a NEW repeating series (template-direct clone via re-promote; ruling 2026-08-13(d), [docs/design/promote-via-clone.md](docs/design/promote-via-clone.md) §6(d)).** Cloning a repeating template previously refused (`H-CLONE-SOURCE` — its `rt1_recurrenceRule` is unreproducible on any write surface). It now runs a compound: clone the template's content as a PLAIN item (title/notes/tags/checklist incl. checked state/container, plus a project's child + heading structure — recurrence and schedule stripped), then native-promote the clone with the SOURCE's decoded rule. The decoded rule maps onto the promote vocabulary through `ruleToInverseParams` (+ the template's deadline flag) — the SAME mapping and expressibility boundary the reschedule-repeat undo rides — so a rule the Repeat dialog cannot express (two end bounds, a multi-anchor month/year rule) or an undecodable rule REFUSES fail-closed (`H-CLONE-SOURCE`, naming the feature) before anything is minted. The result is the **add-repeating contract**: the new template's uuid + `repeating {templateUuid, instanceUuid|null}` (one instance now for after-completion / a future-dated fixed rule spawns the template + cursor with no instance yet) plus a warning noting the NEW series identity (references to the source template do not transfer; no instances are copied). **UI-gated** (`--dangerously-drive-gui` / MCP `dangerously_drive_gui`) because the promote leg drives the app — an ordinary non-template clone stays headless and ungated. A **PAUSED** source template clones UNPAUSED (pause is not part of the rule vocabulary), disclosed — re-pause with `pause-repeat`. `--title`/`--preserve-created` behave as in an ordinary clone (a delete-fate promote may replace the clone row, so `--preserve-created` is best-effort on the surviving series — disclosed). **Undo = trash-both** (removes the minted template + its current instance; there is no original to restore — recorded and undone as `*.add-repeating`). The nested-repeater refusal on a project subtree is UNCHANGED (a template INSIDE the source stays UNCLONABLE — this change is about the template AS the source). A required refinement shipped alongside: the project promote's same-title row-select ambiguity check (`sameTitleRowCount`) now excludes hidden repeating-template rows, since a template clone keeps the source template's title + area and the source template is an invisible, non-selectable row. CI covers the leg sequences + rule-mapping round-trip + undo records (no app); VM end-to-end certification is queued (up-next). (`src/write/promote-clone.ts` new `cloneTemplateViaRepromote`, `src/write/{clone,pipeline,pre-state}.ts`, `src/cli/commands/writes.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`. Tests: `test/engine/write-promote-clone.test.ts` — daily/weekly-weekdays/monthly-ordinal/after-completion mapping, inexpressible + undecodable refusals, ui gating, paused disclosure, trash-both undo, project template + nested-repeater refusal; `test/engine/write-clone.test.ts`, `test/cli/write-cli.test.ts` updated. Docs: `docs/design/{promote-via-clone,decisions}.md`, `docs/capability-matrix.md`, `docs/up-next.md`, `skills/things-cli/`.)
- **Docs — the row-shaping rules are renumbered R6–R13 → RS1–RS8, ending the digit collision with the read-shape doctrine's structural R1–R9.** `docs/contract.md` (the consumer covenant) gains an explicit **Row-shaping rules (RS1–RS8)** section stating each rule's one-line summary (no-redundant-ancestry, detail tiers, search provenance, universal reshapes, `stage`, repeating split, `when`, provisional/deadline-pull); the design contract (`docs/design/contracts.md`) and every living cross-reference (skill references, glossary, source/test comments) move to the RS prefix. A new drift-lock test (`test/unit/rs-rule-drift.test.ts`) keeps the two contract docs' RS rule-set and summaries byte-identical going forward. No wire or behavior change — the rules are unchanged, only their names. (Ruling 2026-08-14, `docs/design/decisions.md`.)
- **Docs — the skill gains a cross-device sync-timing note (docs-recert sweep).** `skills/things-cli/references/contracts.md` now teaches the sync-trigger model an agent forms expectations against: a verified write is committed locally at once (`ok`), starts syncing out within ~2–3 s, and cross-device lag is the RECEIVING device's own pull/wake cadence — not a delay or failure on the writing side. (Internal-only in the same sweep: the read-shape doctrine v1/v2 docs were merged into one living `docs/design/read-shape-doctrine.md`, and `docs/design/{architecture,contracts}.md` directory/source-path claims were re-certified against the tree.)
- **Changed (breaking behavior) — deleting a repeating TEMPLATE is now ALLOWED with disclosure; the delete arm of `H-REPEAT-SCHEDULE` is lifted (ruling 2026-08-13; SERDEL S1–S3, [docs/lab/serdel-series-removal.md](docs/lab/serdel-series-removal.md)).** `things todo delete` / `things project delete` on a repeating template previously REFUSED (`blocked:H-REPEAT-SCHEDULE`). They now succeed: our template-trash is byte-identical to the GUI's own Edit ▸ Delete and is human-recoverable via the app's Trash ▸ Put Back (which restores the template AND resumes the schedule). Like `log-now`, the result is `ok` with **NO undo token** (irreversible headlessly) plus warnings disclosing (a) the series stops generating new occurrences, (b) its live instances are left in place — count + the current instance uuid, (c) restore is unavailable here (Trash ▸ Put Back only). `things todo restore` / `things project restore` on a trashed template keep refusing, but now return a categorical Put-Back message instead of the raw wrapped AppleScript-301 `verify-failed:silent-noop`. `H-REPEAT-SCHEDULE` still refuses the OTHER template hazards (URL schedule writes, move, status/duplicate). The internal `internalSeriesRemoval` exemption flag (#464) is deleted — the promote-undo trash-both legs now use plain deletes (ALPHA-CONTRACT, no compat). (`src/write/{guards,pipeline,undo,commands,reversibility}.ts`, `src/read/queries.ts` (`liveSeriesInstances`). Tests: `test/unit/write-guards.test.ts` — delete-allowed + restore Put-Back refusal; `test/engine/write-promote-clone.test.ts` — undo trash-both round-trip. Docs: `docs/design/{decisions,promote-via-clone}.md`, `docs/capability-matrix.md`, `docs/reference/assumption-register.md` (WG-8/RD-15), `skills/things-cli/`.)
- **Changed (breaking behavior) — `make-repeating` / `add-repeating` are REFUSED as batch legs (ruling 2026-08-13).** A `things batch` line with `todo.make-repeating` / `project.make-repeating` / `todo.add-repeating` / `project.add-repeating` now refuses per-line with a clear error naming the standalone command (`things todo make-repeating …`, etc.) — these are multi-leg promote compounds (clone|add → GUI-promote → trash), not single atomic ops, so they do not compose inside a batch. Previously a batch `make-repeating` line silently ran the OLD destructive native promote; that path is deleted per ALPHA-CONTRACT (the recoverable promote-via-clone form is standalone-only). Independent later lines still run; a `$ref` to the refused line's tempId fails closed. (`src/write/batch.ts`. Tests: `test/engine/write-batch-temp-ids.test.ts` — the make-repeating line refuses, its tempId binds nothing. Docs: `docs/design/promote-via-clone.md` §3, `docs/design/decisions.md`.)
- **Fixed — `make-repeating` / `clone --preserve-created` no longer aborts on a reminder-bearing to-do (UIC8 C1c; [docs/lab/uic8-promote-clone-cert.md](docs/lab/uic8-promote-clone-cert.md)).** When a source to-do carried an item reminder on a dated/`today`/`evening` `when`, the clone leg emitted `reminder` AND a backdated `createdAt` in ONE `todo.add`, which the json import forbids (`--reminder is not available with --created-at`) — so `todo make-repeating`, `project make-repeating` (which always preserve creation), and a standalone `todo clone --preserve-created` aborted at the clone leg (exit 1, X left untouched — fail-safe but unusable on any reminder-carrying to-do). The clone now SPLITS the two: the base add carries `createdAt` only, and the reminder is reproduced in a follow-up `todo.update` leg that re-supplies the source `when` together with the reminder (the fidelity-matrix-sanctioned when+reminder shape), still inside the clone txn (so verify/undo stay coherent — undo trashes the whole minted clone). No split when `--preserve-created` is absent (the base add carries the reminder as before). (`src/write/clone.ts`. Tests: `test/engine/write-clone.test.ts` — reminder+preserve-created reproduces both dimensions via split legs; dry-run discloses the reminder follow-up.)
- **Fixed — `--preserve-modified` is now honored by the promote compounds instead of silently no-opping (UIC8 C6; [docs/lab/uic8-promote-clone-cert.md](docs/lab/uic8-promote-clone-cert.md)).** `make-repeating --preserve-modified` neither preserved the original X's `userModificationDate` forward (the trash-X leg dropped the flag) nor restored it on undo (the summary record carried no `preModDates`). The flag is now threaded onto the trash-X leg — the ONLY leg touching a pre-existing row (the clone/promote legs mint fresh rows, where it is a no-op) — so X's `umd` is kept off the `changes`/watch timeline forward; and X's captured pre-write `umd` now rides the promote SUMMARY record's `preModDates`, so the ratified symmetric-undo restore fires on the revived X. `add-repeating` touches no pre-existing rows, so `--preserve-modified` there remains a clean silent no-op. (`src/write/promote-clone.ts`. Tests: `test/engine/write-promote-clone.test.ts` — forward preservation + summary `preModDates`, undo restores X's `umd`, add-repeating no-op.)
- **Changed — `make-repeating` is now promote-via-clone: recoverable and undoable; NEW `todo add-repeating`; `project add-repeating` gains full vocabulary (promote-via-clone phase 3; rulings 2026-08-13, [docs/design/promote-via-clone.md](docs/design/promote-via-clone.md)).** `things todo make-repeating` / `things project make-repeating` (library `write.makeRepeatingTodo` / `makeRepeatingProject`, MCP `repeat` `{action: start}`) are REIMPLEMENTED as `clone(X, --preserve-created) → trash(X) → native-promote(the clone)`: the ORIGINAL is moved to the Trash (recoverable) and a disposable copy is promoted, so the fate lottery lands on a row we were going to discard (deterministic, CLONE verdict B). The result still carries the minted template uuid (unchanged contract) plus the trashed original's uuid, and — the headline — **`things undo` now REVERSES a promote**: it trashes BOTH the minted template and its current instance (the SERDEL trash-both) and restores the original from the Trash. A project holding a nested repeating template is refused (`H-CLONE-SOURCE`, no `--flatten` — ruling (b)). The old destructive direct-promote is deleted (ALPHA-CONTRACT); the native `*.make-repeating` pipeline ops remain the INTERNAL mechanism the orchestrator drives on the clone. **NEW `things todo add-repeating <title>`** (library `write.addRepeatingTodo`, MCP `repeat` `{scope: todo, action: add}`) — the add→promote composite closing the last make-repeating asymmetry (create with the full to-do add vocabulary, then promote). **`things project add-repeating`** now threads the FULL project add vocabulary (notes/area/`--when`/deadline/child to-dos + the structured `items` field #461) and the full calendar-anchor rule vocabulary, and its undo removes the created series (trash-both). **The undo's series-removal legs are plain template deletes** — deleting a repeating template is now publicly allowed with disclosure (see the "template delete is allowed" entry above), so no internal exemption flag is needed. Undo-of-undo is unsupported headlessly (a trashed template's revival is the app's Trash ▸ Put Back) — disclosed. Placement of the spawned instance is the app-native landing, disclosed in the result (no restore leg — GUI parity, ruling 2026-08-14). A batch `make-repeating` or `add-repeating` line is refused (compound-not-atomic — see the "batch refuses" entry above). (`src/write/promote-clone.ts` new — the four orchestrators; `src/write/make-repeating-project.ts` → `promoteProjectViaGui` (internal project-promote leg); `src/write/{operations,commands,reversibility,undo,guards,clone,batch}.ts`; `src/client.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`, `src/cli/commands/{writes,repeat-flags}.ts`, `src/index.ts`. Tests: `test/engine/write-promote-clone.test.ts` new, `test/unit/reversibility-matrix.test.ts`, `test/engine/write-repeat-response.test.ts`, `test/engine/write-project-make-repeating.test.ts`, `test/mcp/server.test.ts`. Docs: `docs/design/{promote-via-clone,decisions}.md`, `docs/up-next.md`, `README.md`, `skills/things-cli/`.)**
- **Added — symmetric umd-restore on undo (ruling 2026-08-13).** When an audit record carries `preModDates` (an op that ran with `--preserve-modified`, below), `things undo` now appends best-effort AppleScript `set modification date` legs after its mutation legs, restoring the captured pre-write `userModificationDate` values so the UNDO also stays off the `changes`/watch timeline — symmetric with the forward op's silence (disclosed in the result, never fatal). Default operations' undos remain honest umd bumps. This IS the "future symmetric undo" the `--preserve-modified` entry below anticipated. (`src/write/undo.ts`.)
- **Added — the universal `--preserve-modified` write flag: keep a change off the modification-date timeline (TAGMOD T5; maintainer ruling 2026-08-13).** Available on virtually every mutating command — the single-mutation write family, `todo/project move`, and `reorder` (via `addWriteFlags`), plus the MCP write tools (`preserve_modified`) and the library (`WriteOptions.preserveModified`). Tolerated like `--dry-run`: it never errors on an op that does not need it. Mechanics: the pipeline captures each PRE-EXISTING target row's `userModificationDate` (`umd`) before the write (riding the pre-read the verifier already does), runs the mutation + verify normally, then restores each bumped pre-existing row's `umd` to its captured value through the AppleScript `set modification date` property write — the ONLY surface that writes `umd` (novel-paths #44). Rows the op CREATES are untouched (their `umd` is legitimately new), so a create-only op (`add`) is a silent no-op; a `umd`-silent op (reorders, tag rename/delete) restores nothing. A multi-leg compound (the `complete`/`cancel`/`update --completed-at` flip-dance) captures once before the first leg and restores once after the last. **Best-effort:** a failed restore never fails the (already-verified) mutation — it is disclosed per row. **Result shape (ADDITIVE, presence-keyed):** `preservedModified: <n>` (count of rows restored) appears only when the flag did real work, with `preserveFailures: [{uuid, detail}]` when a restore leg could not neutralize a row; the TTY notes it dimly. When the flag is active the captured pre-`umd` values ride the `ok` audit record (`preModDates`), which the symmetric umd-restore on undo now consumes (above). **1-second floor:** the AppleScript `date` type has no sub-second, so a restore lands on `floor(umd0)` — always ≤ the original, the safe direction for a `changes --since` query (a restored row never re-surfaces). **SYNC-SAFE (SYNC2B, golden-v2/3.22.12):** a hand-written past `umd` propagates to peer devices AND survives the sync round-trip in both directions — Things Cloud treats `umd` as ordinary per-attribute synced data, not a monotonic sync clock, and the accompanying content edit rides the sync change-log independent of `umd` — so the flag is safe against a synced Things Cloud store; the earlier "UNSYNCED-only" caveat is **retired** from the `--help`/MCP/skill/doc copy. Only residual: a genuinely concurrent same-row edit on another device re-bumps `umd` via the per-attribute max-merge (fails safe — the edit resurfaces, never silently hidden). (`src/write/preserve-modified.ts` new, `src/write/{pipeline,resolution-timestamps,move}.ts`, `src/audit/schema.ts`, `src/cli/commands/writes.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`. Tests: `test/engine/write-preserve-modified.test.ts` — single-leg capture/restore + AS leg, multi-leg capture-once/restore-once, create no-op, silent-op no-op, non-fatal restore-failure disclosure; `test/cli/universal-preserve-modified.test.ts` — the whole-tree completeness lock. Docs: `docs/lab/sync2b-durable-account.md` (SY-2/SY-2M), `docs/reference/timestamps.md` §4, `docs/capability-matrix.md`, `docs/up-next.md`, `skills/things-cli/`.)

- **Added — first-class `todo clone` / `project clone`: a faithful content COPY of an item through official write surfaces (promote-via-clone phase 2; CLONE verdict-A fidelity matrix, [docs/lab/clone-fidelity-and-template-trash.md](docs/lab/clone-fidelity-and-template-trash.md)).** New ops `todo.clone` / `project.clone` on the library (`client.write.cloneTodo` / `cloneProject`), CLI (`things todo clone <ref>` / `things project clone <ref>`), and MCP (`clone_item`, type-dispatched like `duplicate_item`). Unlike the native `duplicate` (URL-only, shallow), a clone is an ORCHESTRATED compound (`src/write/clone.ts`) that reproduces the source's content field-by-field: title, notes, tags, `when` stage (today/evening/someday/date), reminder time, deadline, checklist items INCLUDING checked state (a post-add `--check`-equivalent leg, A2), the container (project/area/heading), and the completed/canceled terminal state of the item itself with the EXACT original `stopDate` (`complete`/`cancel` + backdate legs, A5/A7). A **project** clone additionally rebuilds area membership, headings, and headed + root children in ONE `things:///json` import (A4), then reproduces each logged/canceled child's terminal state. The clone is a NEW capture: a new uuid, created now, landing at its container's native position (no implicit reorder); `--title` overrides the title, and `--preserve-created` copies the source's creationDate (MINUTE resolution — disclosed). **Refusals (fail-closed):** a trashed source (names the restore path), a repeating template (its recurrence rule is not reproducible on any write surface), and — for a project — a subtree holding a live nested repeating template (names the child, A6). Delivered as a txn of ordinary cataloged legs; the single undoable unit is the clone, whose inverse trashes the minted clone (and every child it minted). Cloning a logged source is allowed (A7); child checklist checked-state and child reminders are born plain (documented residuals). `project.add` gained an internal structured `items` (headings + rich children) for the json-import base leg. (`src/write/{clone,operations,commands,reversibility,undo,guards}.ts` (new `H-CLONE-SOURCE`), `src/write/vectors/simulator.ts` (structured `project.add`), `src/client.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`, `src/cli/commands/writes.ts`. Tests: `test/engine/write-clone.test.ts`, `test/cli/write-cli.test.ts`, `test/unit/reversibility-matrix.test.ts`, `test/mcp/server.test.ts`. Docs: `docs/capability-matrix.md`, `docs/up-next.md`, `README.md`, `skills/things-cli/`.)
- **Changed (TTY only) — the detail card's two recurrence lines merged into one `repeats:` line.** A repeating INSTANCE'S `todo show` / `project show` card previously carried a `repeating: instance of <uuid>` line AND a separate `repeats: on Aug 19` caption line; these are now ONE line — `repeats: on Aug 19 (instance of <uuid>)` / `repeats: 1 day after completion (instance of <uuid>)`, folding a paused template in as `repeats: on Aug 19 (paused; instance of <uuid>)`. With no renderable caption (dangling template FK / undecodable rule) the line is just `repeats: instance of <uuid>`. The JSON wire is UNCHANGED — `instanceOf` and `repeats` stay separate keys. (`src/cli/commands/todo.ts` `renderDetail`. Tests: `test/cli/render.test.ts`.)
- **Added — a repeating INSTANCE'S detail card now carries its repeat context (`repeats`), matching the Things app's lower-corner caption.** `todo show` / `project show` on a spawned occurrence previously showed only the instance link (`instanceOf`); it now also surfaces the repeat context joined from the template — "Repeats on Aug 19" (fixed) / "Repeats 1 day after completion" (after-completion), the same wording the GUI shows. **JSON (detail tier only):** an instance gains a presence-keyed sibling `repeats: {rule?, next?, paused?}` beside `instanceOf` — `rule` is the SAME decoded recurrence shape a template card emits under `repeating.rule` (byte-consistent for the same template), `next` is the template's projected next occurrence for a FIXED rule (ABSENT for after-completion, whose successor date does not exist until the current instance completes), and `paused: true` surfaces a paused template. `instanceOf` is unchanged and stays the write handle. **TTY:** the card gains a single merged `repeats:` line — `repeats: on <date> (instance of <uuid>)` / `repeats: <N> <unit>(s) after completion (instance of <uuid>)`, folding a paused template in as `(paused; instance of <uuid>)` (see the "two recurrence lines merged" entry above). MCP `get_item` gets the same enrichment automatically (shared detail-shaping path). Detail/`show` tier only (never list rows — token economy); a dangling template FK omits `repeats` without crashing, and a logged/trashed instance keeps the harmless context. (`src/read/detail.ts` mirror join, `src/read/shape.ts` `reshapeRepeatingWire`, `src/cli/commands/todo.ts` `renderDetail`, `src/model/entities.ts` `RepeatContext`. Tests: `test/unit/shape.test.ts`, `test/cli/render.test.ts`, `test/mcp/server.test.ts`, `test/unit/wire-key-inventory.test.ts`. Docs: `docs/contract.md`, `docs/design/contracts.md`, `docs/design/read-shape-doctrine.md`, `skills/things-cli/`.)
- **Fixed — `project delete` now refuses a repeating PROJECT template with `blocked:H-REPEAT-SCHEDULE`, at parity with `todo delete` (guard kind-asymmetry; register WG-8, evidence CLONE C3/C4).** The `H-REPEAT-SCHEDULE` guard already refused deleting a repeating to-do template (trashing a template is destructive and unvalidated), but the same guard was inert for `project.delete` — so the shipped CLI would trash a repeating project template into a broken state: the recurrence rule survives but the fixed `nextInstanceStartDate` cursor is cleared, the live instance is left orphaned (still reads as live), and the trashed template is non-restorable via AppleScript (move-to-Inbox → error 301). `project.delete` is now in the guard's `REPEAT_SENSITIVE` set, so a repeating project template is refused with the same hazard, detail, and remediation as the to-do path. Trashing only an INSTANCE stays freely allowed on both kinds (series-preserving) — an instance carries the template FK but no rule of its own, so it is not a template row. (`src/write/guards.ts`. Tests: `test/unit/write-guards.test.ts` — project-template refusal + plain-project/instance delete allowed. Docs: register **WG-8** (reconciled), `docs/capability-matrix.md`, `docs/up-next.md`.)

- **Fixed — `things project dissolve-heading` and `things project move-heading-to-project` are now CLI-invokable (drive-gui flag was not wired; SRCFATE finding, [docs/lab/srcfate-reconciliation-sweep.md](docs/lab/srcfate-reconciliation-sweep.md) finding #2).** Both are GUI-only ui-vector ops whose `--help` instructs `--dangerously-drive-gui`, but their command registrations were wrapped in `addWriteFlags(...)` only — with no `addDriveGuiFlag` — so passing `--dangerously-drive-gui` errored `unknown option` while omitting it fail-closed on `blocked:H-UI-DRIVE`: every invocation a dead end (only `things batch` with `dangerouslyDriveGui` reached the ops). Both registrations now wrap in `addDriveGuiFlag(addWriteFlags(...))` like their sibling ui-vector commands (`promote-heading`, `convert-to-project`). A completeness lock now walks the operation catalog: every op the catalog gates behind the GUI drive (`UI_DRIVE_OPS`) must expose `--dangerously-drive-gui` on its CLI command, so this class of bug cannot recur silently. (`src/cli/commands/writes.ts`. Tests: `test/cli/help-contract.test.ts` — the catalog-driven completeness lock; `test/cli/heading-drivegui-cli.test.ts` — both commands parse the flag and thread `WriteOptions` to the pipeline. The on-device GUI drive is VM-certified separately. Docs: `docs/up-next.md`.)

- **Added — `install-skill` appends a "Filing bugs and feature requests" section to the skill on DEV-checkout installs only.** When the installing binary is a source checkout (its version ends in `-dev`), the installed `SKILL.md` now carries a short section telling an agent running live source where defects and missing capabilities go — the public issue tracker at github.com/mikegreiling/things-api, via the exact non-interactive `gh issue create --repo mikegreiling/things-api …` command — with the redaction rail (the tracker is PUBLIC, so repro data must be synthetic; never real task titles, notes, or project/area names). A published (non-`-dev`) install never receives the section, so tarball users' skill copies are unchanged. Injected on the stamped temp copy inside `withStampedSkillDir`, so every materialized location (canonical `~/.agents`, `~/.claude`, and any skills-CLI-detected agent dir) inherits it. (`src/cli/skill.ts` new `isDevVersion` / `appendDevBugReportingSection` / `DEV_BUG_REPORTING_SECTION`, `src/cli/commands/install-skill.ts`. Tests: `test/cli/install-skill.test.ts` — dev present / release absent across the copy and skills-CLI paths.)

- **Evidence — TAGMOD: the `userModificationDate` (umd) footprint of tag + area lifecycle ops, and the writable `modification date` restore lever ([docs/lab/tagmod-tag-area-umd.md](docs/lab/tagmod-tag-area-umd.md); golden-v2 / Things 3.22.12).** Disposable-VM characterization for the maintainer's planned mass tag cleanup (does retroactive tagging flood the `changes` timeline?). Findings: **applying/removing a tag BUMPS the member's umd** on every working surface — open AND logged, to-do AND project (T1); **tag RENAME (T2) and DELETE — leaf and parent-subtree (T3) — are umd-SILENT** on members (join rows cascade, task rows untouched), so bulk delete/rename is timeline-free; `TMTag`/`TMArea` have **no umd column** (T6), so tag/area own-row changes are structurally invisible to a umd-keyed timeline. **Area delete's trash cascade is STATUS-DEPENDENT** — only OPEN direct members are trashed + umd-bumped; LOGGED/swept members are merely DETACHED (area→NULL, `trashed=0`, umd-silent), refining the documented AREADEL/RD-5 law (T4, oddities §9aa). **The AppleScript `set modification date` setter is a surgical, durable, reversible umd writer** (T5) — writes only the umd column, sticks across relaunch, restores to the floored second (1-second resolution) — enabling a capture-and-restore recipe (apply tag → restore umd → only the intended change remains) that a future `update … --preserve-modified` option can wire (up-next). **Caveat:** proven on an UNSYNCED store only; the interaction of a hand-written umd with Things Cloud's 3-way merge is UNKNOWN (SYNC2 blocked) — the restore recipe is sync-unsafe until probed. No shipped code changed; living docs updated (register RD-5 refinement + RD-23, capability-matrix tag/area rows, oddities §9aa, craft §3d, novel-paths #44). Driver: [`lab/scripts/research-tagmod.sh`](lab/scripts/research-tagmod.sh).

- **Evidence + copy — SYNC2B: the long-blocked Things Cloud sync campaign, on a DURABLE lab account ([docs/lab/sync2b-durable-account.md](docs/lab/sync2b-durable-account.md); golden-v2 / Things 3.22.12).** Two networked clones on a single durable throwaway account (minted once, kept alive — NO churn; creds gitignored). **SY-1** baseline convergence (both directions) + `BSSyncronyMetadata` taxonomy — a third account confirms the last-sync key is account-specific, validating the shipped nearest-to-now reader. **SY-2 (THE GATE)** `--preserve-modified` / AS `set modification date` vs Things Cloud: a hand-written past `umd` **propagates to the peer AND survives the round-trip both directions** (Things Cloud treats `umd` as ordinary synced data; content rides the change-log independent of `umd`), so **the flag is sync-safe** and the "UNSYNCED-only" caveat is **retired** (the ONE user-visible change: `--help` + MCP `preserve_modified` descriptions, `src/cli/commands/writes.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`). **SY-3** cross-device repeating-instance **dedupe** (the same occurrence materialized on two disconnected devices collapses to ONE, no duplicate) and the SERDEL S5 **zone residual RESOLVED** (a spawned occurrence's `creationDate` = occurrence-day midnight in the SPAWNING DEVICE's local zone). Living docs: register §D (SYNC-1/2/3), decisions.md (sync-safe ruling + durable-account doctrine), roadmap (cloud-probes unblocked), timestamps.md §4 + §1b, capability-matrix tag row, craft §4b/§4c, up-next (sync-gate resolved), skill. Driver: [`lab/scripts/research-sync2b.sh`](lab/scripts/research-sync2b.sh).

- **Evidence — UIC8: the promote-via-clone compounds certified END-TO-END in a VM ([docs/lab/uic8-promote-clone-cert.md](docs/lab/uic8-promote-clone-cert.md); golden-v2 / Things 3.22.12).** The `make-repeating` (rewired clone→trash→promote) and `add-repeating` compounds shipped with CI-only coverage (leg command-sequences + undo-record round-trips; CI has no app); this certifies them through the PRODUCTION CLI against a live Things app in one disposable clone, asserting the full DB byte-effect + JSON result envelope. Certified: X lands in the Trash byte-intact (incl. a backdated `creationDate` + a checked checklist item), template + instance minted with the right rule bytes, the `repeating{templateUuid,instanceUuid,replacedUuid}` block + warnings exact (C1/C2); the nested-repeater project refuses `H-CLONE-SOURCE` with X untouched (C2d); `things undo` performs the trash-both + restore (cursor cleared, zero spawns on a +2-day advance) and the sanctioned-internal series-removal fires ONLY via undo (a direct template delete still refuses `H-REPEAT-SCHEDULE`) (C3); `add-repeating` mints the correct spawn shape per rule type — one instance for after-completion, template+cursor (0 instances) for a future-dated fixed rule (C4); and a mid-compound promote failure rolls X back OUT of the Trash (C5). **Two findings captured to up-next** (no behavior change here): a reminder-bearing to-do can't be `make-repeating`/`clone --preserve-created`-d (the clone leg emits `reminder`+`createdAt` together, which `commands.ts` forbids); and `--preserve-modified` is a silent no-op on the promote compounds. `src/write/vectors/ui-certification.ts` gains `UIC8` on the make-repeating evidence + certification profile; living docs updated (register RD-15/WG-8 *Confirmed under*, capability-matrix promote rows, `docs/design/promote-via-clone.md` §3/§5, `docs/up-next.md`). Driver: [`lab/scripts/research-uic8.sh`](lab/scripts/research-uic8.sh).

## 0.15.0 — 2026-08-11

- **Added — `things deadlines`: the deadline-horizon query view (R9 family; maintainer-ratified 2026-08-11).** A new first-class read view whose identity IS the ordering — one flat `data.items[]` of every LIVE (open, untrashed, container-untrashed) to-do AND project carrying a deadline, `stage` kept (stage-mixed, like `search`/`changes`). Ordering is `deadline ASC` (most-overdue first), then `todayIndex ASC`, then `uuid` — the SAME comparator the global `upcoming` view uses (`COALESCE(startDate, deadline) ASC, todayIndex ASC, uuid`) RESTRICTED to deadlined rows: **one ordering law, two views**. Declared NON-SCOPE — deadline order is the view, so `--in deadlines` is refused with the standard unknown-token copy (not a reorder axis). A deadline-nag DISMISSAL does not filter it (suppression governs the Today pull, not deadline existence). Deadline-bearing REPEATING TEMPLATES are PROJECTED at their next occurrence's deadline (`nextOccurrence − rule.startOffsetDays`, the certified horizon-1 machinery `upcomingView` uses — same derivation, not a reimplementation), disclosing projection-ness via the R11 `repeating` presence; a deadline-less template, an undecodable rule, and a paused/after-completion one with no set next occurrence are EXCLUDED ("unsupported beats guessed"). Filters compose: `--overdue` (open, strictly past today; excludes projections by construction — a projection is never past), `--today` (current Today members, evening-inclusive — reuses the `todayView` membership predicate; excludes templates), `--project` / `--area` (container scope, mirroring `search`), `--tag` / `--exact-tag` / `--untagged`, `--limit` / `--all` (standard whole-view truncation). Exposed on the CLI (`things deadlines`), the MCP read tool family (`read_view deadlines`, with `today`/`project` params), and the library (`client.read.deadlines`). The TTY leads each row with its deadline chip under a `── ⚑ Deadlines ──` header; date labels are consumer-zone-correct (#414). (`src/read/{views,filter-contract,shape}.ts`, `src/client.ts`, `src/index.ts`, `src/contracts.ts`, `src/cli/{render,help,read-driver,commands/reads}.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`, `schema/envelope.schema.json`. Tests: `test/unit/{views,shape}.test.ts`, `test/cli/{render,e2e,help-contract}.test.ts`, `test/mcp/server.test.ts`, `test/engine/write-move.test.ts` (the `--in deadlines` refusal). Docs: `docs/contract.md`, `docs/design/read-shape-doctrine.md`, `skills/things-cli/`.)

- **Fixed/Changed — an EXPLICIT `things reorder … --in today` now preserves the observable Today order (minimal cohort-preserving wires; [docs/lab/todwire-partial-wires-today.md](docs/lab/todwire-partial-wires-today.md), the MOVPLC sequel).** #442 stopped a *bare* container move from firing the whole-view Today reorder, but an explicit today-axis reorder still compiled the OLD full wire (`[...movees, ...ALL remaining open bucket-0 members by raw todayIndex ASC]`), so it re-stamped every named row's `todayIndexReferenceDate → today` and rewrote/fused the whole visible order (ORD-20). The TODWIRE disposable-VM campaign (golden-v2 / Things 3.22.12, five entry-date cohorts) established that the native `list "Today"` reorder obeys the same partial-wire law as headings — `result = [named ids in wire order] ++ [unnamed rows in prior VISIBLE order]`, touching ONLY named rows — and that a single-ID wire is a clean front-insert to the visible top (movee alone re-stamped, `umd`-silent), while unnamed rows (all other cohorts + unswept-canceled + stale-evening bystanders) stay byte-identical (EXP1/EXP2/EXP4). Fix: the `today` scope now compiles `minimalReorderWire(currentVisibleOrder, targetVisibleOrder)` over the READER's Today comparator (shared `todayOrderBy`, one law — the reader's `todayView` ORDER BY and the reorder census now use the same fragment), so it names only what must move and leaves every other row's entry cohort + visible position untouched. Because the native primitive can only RAISE a named row's cohort to *today*, a `--before`/`--after`/`--end` placement must name the visible prefix down to the insertion point; those non-movee rows re-stamp, so the result `warnings` + `--dry-run` DISCLOSE the count (the #V11 heading-reopen disclosure pattern; the movees themselves always re-stamp inherently — a doc/help note, not a warning). The bench simulator's reorder applier front-inserts the today minimal wire to match. The GUI cross-cohort drag addendum (a plain Today drag ADOPTS the drop neighbour's entry cohort, a single-row write — so `tiRef` is not a faithful "entered Today" date) is recorded in oddities §9y. (`src/read/{predicates,views}.ts`, `src/write/{pre-state,commands,reorder,move,vectors/simulator}.ts`. Tests: `test/engine/write-reorder.test.ts` — multi-cohort minimal wire + anchored-prefix disclosure; `test/engine/write-move.test.ts` + `test/engine/write-simulator.test.ts` — the O12 mixed-kind / ORD-18 axis-isolation locks updated to the minimal-wire order. Docs: register **ORD-22**, oddities §9p TODWIRE addendum + §9y, `skills/things-cli/references/ordering.md`.)
