- **Fixed — a container-only `todo move` / `project move` with no explicit position no longer rewrites the whole Today order (field bug §0½.7; [docs/lab/movplc-move-placement-today.md](docs/lab/movplc-move-placement-today.md)).** A bare container move (e.g. `todo move <uuid> --to-project <P>`) auto-fired its default placement leg — a native `_private_experimental_ reorder to dos in list "Today"` over ALL open Today members. The MOVPLC disposable-VM campaign (golden-v2 / Things 3.22.12) established the mechanism the field audit was missing: that native reorder writes each named row's `todayIndex` to the wire order **and re-stamps its `todayIndexReferenceDate → today`**, collapsing every entry-date cohort into one so the visible order of hundreds of unrelated rows is rewritten from a one-item move (ORD-20). Fix: `finishPlacement` now SKIPS the placement reorder when no `--first/--last/--before/--after` is given AND the movee lands in the **today/evening** view axis — the membership move alone already preserves the movee's Today slot (MOVPLC step 3), so a bare move keeps it there (app-default) and only an explicit position opts into the view reorder. Index/day-scope landings keep their documented top-of-bucket placement (they re-rank only the destination container, no cohort collapse). This revises the ratified "destination + no position ⇒ `--first` implied" default for the today/evening axis ([docs/design/heading-demotion-and-move.md](docs/design/heading-demotion-and-move.md) §Bare invocations). Separately and unconditionally, `computeReorderPre` now applies the reader's derived-trash container exclusion (`CONTAINER_UNTRASHED`) so a reorder census no longer LEAKS a trashed project's Today-scheduled children (own `trashed=0`, project `trashed=1`) into the native wire — the blind-writer reorder (§9p) was mutating rows the reader hides (ORD-21). (`src/write/{move,pre-state}.ts`. Tests: `test/engine/write-move.test.ts` — the no-position Today skip + explicit `--first` still fires; `test/engine/write-reorder.test.ts` — the census derived-trash exclusion.)

- **Fixed — symbolic `when: today` UPDATE no longer false-fails `verify-failed:mismatch` when the app preserves an already-arrived `startDate` (field bug §0½.8; [docs/research/project-update-today-verify-mismatch.md](docs/research/project-update-today-verify-mismatch.md)).** `todo update --when today` / `project update --when today` on an item ALREADY in Today makes Things keep the item's historical (arrived) `startDate` instead of rewriting the storage byte to today — semantically correct (the arrived-date law), but the verifier asserted exact `startDate == today`, so a successful update retried 35× and then returned `verify-failed:mismatch` even though every semantic placement (active, Today, not-Evening) landed. The symbolic-Today UPDATE assertion is now an arrived-date PREDICATE (`startDate != null && startDate <= today`) instead of exact equality, so a preserved arrived date verifies while an undated deadline-only pull (null `startDate`) is still REJECTED (its Today membership rests only on a deadline, not the requested schedule). ADDs keep exact `startDate == today` (a fresh Today item has no schedule history to preserve), and explicit ISO-date updates keep exact equality; `when: evening` is unchanged (evening is date-anchored to today exactly, so exact equality is correct there). Implemented via a new JSON-serializable `satisfies` assertion form on the verify layer (`FieldPredicate` / `arrived-on-or-before`). ALPHA: pre-change audit records carry the old exact-equality assertion shape but undo unaffected (the undo path reconstructs from captured pre-values + `observed`, never from `expected.assert`). (`src/write/{commands,verify/delta}.ts`. Tests: `test/engine/write-when-today-verify.test.ts` — the report's 5-case matrix across both `todo.update` and `project.update` plus both add ops; `test/unit/verify-delta.test.ts` — the raw predicate.)

- **Agent skill: `references/model.md` renamed to `references/data-model.md` (bench-driven, ALPHA).** The deep data-model reference file is renamed to match the concept agents (and the skill's own "Data model (read this first)" section) reach for; SKILL.md's inline links, the reference-file inventory (`data-model` replaces `model`), and the inventory's negation (now "there is no `reads`/`writes`/`recurrence`/`safety` file" — `data-model` struck, since it now exists — superseding the negation added by the reference-inventory-signpost entry below) are all updated. Content of the file is byte-identical; only the name and its pointers change. Motivated by an AGENTBENCH paired A/B rename round (`mg/bench-rename-round`): in the pi/gpt-5.4-mini skill arm (static skill injection), 5 of 12 baseline runs issued a failed `references/data-model.md` open — the persistent wrong-filename guess documented across prior rounds — which the rename eliminates (0 wrong guesses; success non-inferior 10→11/12, safety clean, tokens flat). The Claude-Code/Haiku skill arm (native progressive-disclosure ingestion) never reached for a reference filename at all, so the guess-prior does not manifest there — neutral, no regression. Directional at N=3 reps/cell (quota-bound); the effect is mechanical (a filename match), not a noisy success delta. (`skills/things-cli/SKILL.md` + `references/`. Tests: `test/cli/install-skill.test.ts`. Bench: `bench/ROADMAP.md`, `bench/ledger/skill.md`, `bench/tasks/AUTHORING.md`.)

- **Added — `things log-now`: move completed items to the Logbook now (AppleScript `log completed now`; LOGNOW/A28/plog1).** A new global write op (`log-now`) on the API (`client.write.logNow`), CLI (`things log-now`), and MCP (`log_now`). It advances the log-move boundary (`TMSettings.manualLogDate`) so resolved-but-unlogged items become logged — mutating ZERO task rows (the log-move sweep is a derived boundary). The result discloses how many items were moved (`observed.logged`); when nothing is pending it is a clean no-op (`logged: 0`, not an error) — the certified law that the verb advances only when there are pending completions. Delivered through the AppleScript vector like other AS ops (macOS Automation consent on first use). Pre-reads the resolved-but-unlogged census (`pendingLogCount`, the boundary predicate inverted) for the count; verifies as a delta on the `TMSettings` singleton (`logged-now` delta mode + a `manualLogDate()` verify reader) — a re-read comparison, since the boundary is not a task row. IRREVERSIBLE (the boundary cannot be rewound by any official surface — TIMEZ/settings-stamp laws): no undo token, `undo` reports it irreversible, and it is refused under an active container scope (a library-wide effect). (`src/write/{operations,commands,reversibility,undo,scope-guard,pre-state,vectors/applescript,verify/delta}.ts`, `src/read/log-boundary.ts`, `src/client.ts`, `src/cli/{help,commands/writes}.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`. Tests: `test/unit/{write-compile,reversibility-matrix}.test.ts`, `test/engine/write-pipeline.test.ts`, `test/cli/e2e.test.ts`, `test/mcp/server.test.ts`. Docs: `docs/capability-matrix.md`, `docs/reference/{suite-audit,novel-paths}.md`, `docs/contract.md`, `docs/reference/glossary.md`, `skills/things-cli/`.)

- **Added — `things logbook` surfaces the log-move cadence at the head of the view (view-level fact, doctrine R8).** The Logbook render now leads with a card-style metadata header (indented `key: value`, the `things today` / `area show` header convention): `cadence:` in Cultured Code's own Settings words — `Immediately` · `Daily` · `Manually` (the certified `logInterval` enum, oddities §8c — there is NO weekly/monthly) — and, under Daily and Manually, `last logged:` (the `manualLogDate` instant, rendered viewer-local through the zone-aware date helpers, Z-LOGVIEW) — a manual `log-now` works under any cadence, and under Daily a `manualLogDate` past the daily edge IS the effective log-move boundary (`logBoundary = max(interval edge, manualLogDate)`), so the stamp is operationally live information, not just history; under Immediately it is OMITTED (the boundary is `now`, so the stamp is inert — a deliberate asymmetry). On the wire the same fact rides `meta.logging = { cadence, lastLoggedAt? }` (additive, non-breaking) — a whole-view fact on `meta`, exactly like the today view's `meta.counts`, so `data` stays pure logged rows. Present on the CLI `--json` envelope and the MCP `read_view logbook` metadata block. (`src/read/log-boundary.ts` `logState`, `src/{contracts,client,index}.ts`, `src/cli/{render,read-driver,commands/reads}.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`, `schema/envelope.schema.json`. Tests: `test/cli/{render,e2e}.test.ts`, `test/mcp/server.test.ts`, `test/contract/envelope-schema.test.ts`. Docs: `docs/contract.md`, `docs/reference/glossary.md`, `docs/capability-matrix.md`, `skills/things-cli/`.)

- **Fixed — `todo add` with a resolution timestamp AND `--checklist-item` no longer silently no-ops the whole import (§9y; RESID1 R-JSONPAR, #427).** The timestamped-add compile path (routed through `things:///json` because the plain `add` URL drops date params) emitted `checklist-items` as a bare STRING array, which `things:///json` rejects wholesale — the entire import silently produced no row (the CLI verify caught it as `verify-failed:silent-noop`, so it failed safe, but the feature was non-functional for that flag combination in 0.14.0). The add path now emits the OBJECT-array shape the app accepts (`[{type:"checklist-item",attributes:{title}}]`), via a shared `checklistItemsJsonAttr` helper the checklist-replace path also routes through. `project add` carries child to-dos (`items`), not checklist items, so it was unaffected. Locked by a `write-compile` golden asserting the object-array shape (and rejecting the string-array shape) on the timestamped path. (`src/write/commands.ts`. Tests: `test/unit/write-compile.test.ts`. Docs: `docs/things-app-oddities.md` §9y, `docs/capability-matrix.md`.)

- **CHANGE (breaking, ALPHA) — entity model: `derived.todaySection` + `derived.reminderLive` DELETED; `reminder` is now live-gated at the model (one-vocabulary arc finale; [docs/design/vocabulary-audit.md](docs/design/vocabulary-audit.md) addendum 2026-08-05).** The programmatic-API entity shape changed; the JSON/CLI/MCP wire AND the TTY are UNCHANGED (byte-identical), proven via the shape/render/e2e/wire-key-inventory suites.
  - **`derived.todaySection` deleted outright.** It was informationally redundant with the presence-keyed Today markers (`todaySection === "evening"` ⇔ `evening: true`; `"today"` ⇔ `today && !evening`). Every consumer now reads the markers: the write-verify schedule delta asserts the marker equivalents (`whenAssertions` today → `{today: true, evening: null}`, evening → `{evening: true}` — still gated to Today members under the write's clock/zone, the #211-family lock translated, not weakened); `verify/delta.ts` `getField` drops the `todaySection` name; `undo.ts` reads the `evening` marker (schedule reconstruction + the `start/startDate/today/evening` clobber-guard); the today-view evening split reads the `evening` marker (which already carries the exact `startBucket=1 ∧ startDate=today` semantics). The `TodaySection` type, `TODAY_SECTION_FROM_DB`, and `mapTodaySection` are removed. ALPHA: a pre-change audit record whose schedule delta carried the old `todaySection` assertion becomes an unrecognized field and is skipped by the undo planner — the undo degrades cleanly (the schedule leg simply isn't reconstructed), never crashes.
  - **`reminder` gated at INGESTION; `derived.reminderLive` deleted.** The top-level consumer `reminder` is now the LIVE value only — null once the byte is presentation-dead (§9n: its `startDate` gone strictly past) — live-gated at the mapper under the response clock, exactly where the `today`/`evening` markers are gated. The RAW stored byte (which the app keeps forever and REVIVES on re-scheduling) moves into the substrate as `derived.reminder`; the write engine reads THAT for its pre-state/delta predictions (`effectiveReminder` auto-preserve, `H-NO-REMINDER`, `clear-dated-reminder` verify + undo capture, and the `reminder` write-verify assertion, which `getField` resolves to the raw byte). Read-shaping's `reminderLive`-keyed `delete o.reminder` is gone — a null top-level `reminder` is pruned by omit-empty like any other nullable consumer field, so the wire is byte-identical (it already emitted only live reminders). The human-render bell (`render.ts`/`glyphs.ts`) reads the live top-level `reminder`.
  - **Substrate bag is now 6 fields:** `start`, `logged`, `trashed`, `today?`, `evening?`, `reminder` (raw byte). `DerivedSubstrate` JSDoc documents `derived.reminder`'s revival semantics + §9n; the wire-key-inventory lock is unchanged (key set unchanged, top-level `reminder` semantics changed).
  - (`src/model/{entities,mappers}.ts`, `src/read/{stage,shape,views,detail,snapshot,area-view,project-view}.ts`, `src/cli/{render,glyphs}.ts`, `src/write/{commands,guards,clear-reminder,undo,pipeline,verify/delta}.ts`, `src/client.ts`, `src/index.ts`. Tests: `test/unit/{mappers,shape,views,candidate-ref,ref-shape,glyphs,render-styling,width,undo-plan,did-you-mean,search-rank,serialize,wire-key-inventory,reversibility-matrix}.test.ts`, `test/engine/{write-verify-clock,write-undo}.test.ts`, `test/cli/{write-verify-clock-cli,e2e,project-today-pips}.test.ts`. Docs: `docs/design/vocabulary-audit.md`.)

- **Agent skill: reference-file inventory signpost (bench-driven, ALPHA).** `skills/things-cli/SKILL.md` gains one additive paragraph at its first decision point naming the exact six reference files (`model`/`contracts`/`ordering`/`errors`/`banner`/`gui`), mapping the topic an agent wants to the file that holds it, and stating outright that there is no `reads`/`writes`/`data-model`/`recurrence`/`safety` file. Motivated by observed AGENTBENCH failures on the 0.14.0 read shapes: skilled agents burned error-incurring shell calls hunting reference files by guessed topic-names, giving the skill arm HIGHER friction than the bare-CLI arm on self-evident read tasks. Additive only (no existing content compressed). Directional at N=3 — pending a ≥10-rep paired confirmation (bench/ROADMAP.md). (`skills/things-cli/SKILL.md`; bench round `mg/bench-0-14-round`.)

- **CHANGE (breaking, ALPHA) — write-timestamp op params unified to `createdAt`/`completedAt` (one-vocabulary audit, Batch 3 / R-3; [docs/design/vocabulary-audit.md](docs/design/vocabulary-audit.md)).** The kind-agnostic `set-dates` op (`todo.set-dates` / `project.set-dates`) previously took `SetDatesParams.creationDate` / `completionDate`, while the add-path (`todo.add` / `project.add`) already used `createdAt` / `completedAt` for the SAME two concepts — one concept spelled two ways on the write input surface. The `set-dates` params are renamed to `createdAt` / `completedAt`, so the whole programmatic write surface now names the creation/completion-timestamp concept ONE way. No behavior change: the compiled AppleScript (`set creation date` / `set completion date`) and the resulting DB writes are byte-identical, and the CLI flags (`--created-at` / `--completed-at`) and MCP params (`created_at` / `completed_at`) were already this spelling — the CLI/MCP wire is UNCHANGED. Reads are a DISTINCT concept-role (state, not input) and keep `created` / `stopped`; the DB column `creationDate` is untouched. ALPHA: pre-rename `set-dates` audit records carrying the old `requested` param spelling are still fully undoable (undo reconstructs the inverse from the captured pre-state delta fields, not from `requested`). (`src/write/{operations,commands,resolution-timestamps,guards,undo}.ts`. Tests: `test/unit/{write-guards,write-compile,reversibility-matrix}.test.ts`. Docs: `docs/design/vocabulary-audit.md`, `docs/up-next.md`.)

- **CHANGE (breaking, ALPHA) — internal entity vocabulary unified: the `derived` substrate bag (one-vocabulary audit, Batches 1–2; [docs/design/vocabulary-audit.md](docs/design/vocabulary-audit.md)).** The programmatic-API entity shape changed; the JSON/CLI/MCP wire is UNCHANGED (byte-identical), verified by the render suite and a new wire-key-inventory lock test.
  - **Batch 1 — the last `shape.ts` translation is gone (R-1/R-1b).** The internal day-block key is now built as `when` end-to-end (`WireDateGroup`/`rebucketChildren`), so `src/read/shape.ts` performs NO `internalName → wireName` translation on any emitted key. The render-internal `IsoDateGroup<T>.date` was likewise renamed to `when` (entities.ts + re-export, project-view/area-view builders, the area CLI upcoming reader) so the scheduled day-groups speak the same time-axis word. Neither reaches the wire in a renamed form; the emitted `when` was already the wire name.
  - **Batch 2 — the entity substrate is segregated into a nested `derived` bag (Option B).** The intermixed internal fields move off the flat `TaskCommon` surface into `TaskCommon.derived` (`DerivedSubstrate`, exported). `startDate` stays flat (a dual-role full-tier consumer field). `shape.ts`'s per-field deletes collapse to a single `delete o.derived`. `deriveStage`/`deriveWhen` stay pure structural functions; `entityStage` is added and exported alongside `entityWhen`/`entityProvisional` for programmatic ergonomics (get the wire word from an entity + clock without reaching into `derived`). The write-verify assertion vocabulary keeps its stable schedule-vocabulary names — `getField` reads them from `derived`. (Batch 2 initially moved `todaySection`/`reminderLive` into the bag; the vocab-arc finale entry above then DELETED both — see the 6-field end-state there.)
  - **The wire-key-inventory lock (`test/unit/wire-key-inventory.test.ts`).** A max-populated entity per kind through `shapeReadPayload` at both tiers: emitted item keys ⊆ the frozen `WIRE_KEYS ∪ EMISSION_DERIVED`, and every frozen key is reachable — so a future substrate field that forgets `derived`, or a latent rename, fails loudly.
  - Batch 3 (the write-timestamp `createdAt`/`creationDate` unification, audit R-3) is a separate write-input-surface vocabulary decision and is NOT in this change — it awaits a maintainer ruling.
  - (`src/model/{entities,mappers}.ts`, `src/read/{stage,shape,log-boundary,views,project-view,area-view,search-rank}.ts`, `src/cli/{render,glyphs,commands/area,commands/project,commands/todo,commands/reads}.ts`, `src/write/{pre-state,guards,undo,verify/delta}.ts`, `src/index.ts`. Tests: `test/unit/{wire-key-inventory,shape,stage,mappers,views,candidate-ref,ref-shape,glyphs,render-styling,width,undo-plan,did-you-mean}.test.ts`, `test/cli/render.test.ts`. Docs: `docs/design/vocabulary-audit.md`, `docs/up-next.md`.)

## 0.14.0 — 2026-08-05

- **CHANGE (breaking, ALPHA) — read-shape doctrine v2, PR 5 of 5 (FINALE): the global `anytime`/`someday` sections become bucket RECORDS with inline `total`, and the `meta.truncation.blocks[]` sidecar is RETIRED from the wire ENTIRELY.** Fifth and last migration PR of the ratified [read-shape doctrine v2](docs/design/read-shape-doctrine.md) — the doctrine migration is now COMPLETE (5 of 5):
  - **Sections record-ized (R1).** `things anytime` / `things someday` (and `read_view anytime`/`someday`) `data.sections` is now `[{ area, items, total? } …]` — each section carries its own inline `total`, present **iff** that section's `items` were capped (`items.length < total`); an untruncated section never restates its own length. `area` is the area node (or `null` for the loose section); `items` is the section's flattened rows (area-direct/loose to-dos, then each project row with its capped children). Completeness is answerable locally, no sidecar join.
  - **`blocks[]` retired from the WIRE ENTIRELY.** With anytime/someday swept, NO view emits the per-block `blocks[]` truncation sidecar any longer — so the `blocks` field is DELETED from the `Truncation` wire type (ALPHA — no dead shapes), and the `GroupBlock` definition drops out of the regenerated envelope schema. The whole-view `{shown, total, limit, truncated}` rollup still rides `meta.truncation` on every grouped view. The per-block detail the TTY renderers need (the "… N more — `things project show '…'`" drill-downs, the someday projects-vs-to-dos split) is now carried as INTERNAL render plumbing (`GroupBlock[]`, returned separately from the client's bounded views), never serialized.
  - **TTY byte-identical.** The renderers read the per-block detail directly (a function argument, never the wire), so only the JSON reshaped — proven byte-for-byte via the stash-revert method across every anytime/someday variant (bare, `--area-limit`, `--project-limit`, `--all`, `--area <ref>`, `--show-active-project-items` bare + numeric, and the hidden-active-items counting hint) plus the full render suite.
  - **Ordering contract consolidated.** `docs/contract.md` gains the "Read views — shapes and orderings" section for the v2 end-state: every view's `data` shape, each bucket's ordering, which orders are user axes vs presentation-derived (trash recency, the `search` composite rank), the global-upcoming day-block key law (`startDate ?? (template ? null : deadline)`), the container-children law, the `--in` token correspondence (bucket keys / `when` values = reorder tokens; `logbook` and the `when: null` resting block declared non-sortable / not-yet-implemented per R6/#V10), and the R1 `total` presence law. Essentials mirrored in `docs/design/contracts.md`; the skill (`skills/things-cli/`) teaches all v2 shapes end-to-end.
  - No new `apiVersion`; no new error code. The envelope schema is regenerated (the `blocks`/`GroupBlock` definitions drop; the `data` payload stays an open object). (`src/read/{truncation,shape}.ts`, `src/{contracts,client,index}.ts`, `src/cli/{read-driver,render,commands/reads,commands/area,commands/show}.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`, `schema/envelope.schema.json`. Tests: `test/unit/{truncation,shape}.test.ts`, `test/cli/{render,e2e}.test.ts`, `test/mcp/server.test.ts`, `test/contract/envelope-schema.test.ts`. Docs: `docs/contract.md`, `docs/design/{contracts,read-shape-doctrine}.md`, `docs/up-next.md`, `skills/things-cli/`.)

- **CHANGE (breaking, ALPHA) — universal `reorder`: ONE verb across to-dos, projects, headings, AND sidebar areas (plan PR D, #V11, targets 0.14.0).** `things reorder <refs…>` now accepts ANY single-KIND movee set and dispatches the protocol by kind, per the signed-off [resolution-timestamp-surface.md §7](docs/design/resolution-timestamp-surface.md) and read-shape doctrine v2 ruling #V11. The old `reorder` handled only to-dos (and the project rows they intermix with); it now also reorders projects, a project's headings, and sidebar areas.
  - **Anchor grammar (ratified):** `--start` / `--end` / `--before <ref>` / `--after <ref>` (replacing `--first` / `--last`; the MCP `reorder` tool likewise takes `start`/`end`/`before`/`after`), plus `--in <token>` (to-dos/projects only). Bare (no position) still assembles the block at the earliest movee's slot.
  - **Kind dispatch:** to-dos and/or projects → the existing index/day/view engine (a to-do+project set intermixes only on the shared Today/Evening/day axes; a pure-project set re-ranks its siblings); a project's **headings** → the certified heading-block wire; **areas** → the sidebar-drag driver.
  - **Refusals, not fragmentation:** a mixed-kind set, a cross-container set, a cross-axis (`--in` on a heading/area) anchor, and a non-member each get ONE precise refusal naming the fix.
  - **Headings (#V11):** same-project heading re-ranking is now expressible through `reorder` (dispatching onto `project.move-heading`, which REMAINS the placement verb for cross-project moves / demotion). Archived headings (unswept AND swept) are reorderable UNGUARDED like the GUI: the engine composes the MINIMAL front-cluster wire so an archived heading that need not move stays OUT of the wire and is provably untouched; when the target order FORCES an archived heading into the wire it PROCEEDS and DISCLOSES the reopened heading(s) (repositioning an archived heading brings it back to open — result warning + `--dry-run` leg plan). Never silent, never a hard guard, never an un-targeted row in the wire.
  - **Resolved to-do movees (LOGSORT):** a SWEPT resolved to-do (Logbook resident) is refused with copy pointing at reactivation (`things todo reopen`) or `--completed-at` for Logbook re-dating; an unswept resolved to-do gets a distinct reopen-first refusal on any uncertified protocol (its live-body index-only permit on the pure-native path is wired by the ORD-13 follow-up bullet below). `--in logbook` never resolves to a reorder scope.
  - **Areas:** area-rank reordering is reachable through the universal verb; `things area reorder` survives as a discriminating kind-specific spelling. **MCP `reorder_areas` is DELETED** (ALPHA) — area reordering folds into the unified `reorder` tool (which gains `dangerously_drive_gui` for the sidebar drag).
  - **Heading-children protection (O06) is absolute and locked:** a project-scope wire never includes a heading's child implicitly (the reparent hazard) — verified by test.
  - (`src/write/{move,pre-state,commands,pipeline}.ts`, `src/client.ts`, `src/cli/commands/writes.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`. Tests: `test/engine/write-universal-reorder.test.ts`, `test/unit/{heading-verbs,write-compile}.test.ts`, `test/mcp/server.test.ts`, `test/cli/help-contract.test.ts`. Docs: `docs/capability-matrix.md`, `skills/things-cli/`. Evidence: `docs/lab/headsort-heading-lifecycle-reorder.md` (#400), `docs/lab/logsort-logged-child-reorder.md` (#401).)

- **New — `reorder`/`todo reorder` now PERMITS an UNSWEPT-resolved to-do movee on the pure-native index path (LOGSORT ORD-13, follow-up to the PR D deferral).** LOGSORT (#401) certified that a completed/canceled to-do still in its live container body (`stopDate` ABOVE the Logbook sweep boundary) re-ranks `index`-only + `userModificationDate`-silent via the native reorder verb — no reopen, no reactivation needed. That permit is now wired, gated tightly so a resolved row can NEVER route through a leg family LOGSORT did not certify:
  - **Permitted iff the compiled plan is the pure-native `index` wire** (the private-verb in-place reorder — project/area/inbox/someday). The gate is `admitResolved && key === "index"`, passed `true` only by the native `reorder` op's preRead (the sole caller of that wire); every bounce / move / day-axis orchestrator leaves it `false`. A mixed OPEN + unswept-resolved wire is the certified case (L-UNSWEPT/MIXED) and is permitted under the same condition.
  - **Still refused (unchanged / new copy):** a SWEPT resolved to-do (Logbook resident) → reactivate (`things todo reopen`) or `--completed-at`; an unswept-resolved movee reaching any UNCERTIFIED protocol (a bounce/move fallback, e.g. native unavailable → PROJROOT, or an `--in <date>` day-axis target) → a native-only-condition refusal (reopen it first). Sweptness is derived at CALL time against the live log boundary (`logBoundary`) under the consumer zone, threaded through `preRead`.
  - **Byte-lock in verify:** the reorder `expectedDelta` gains a `frozen` per-row assertion for each admitted resolved movee — it must read back still-resolved (`status` closed, `stoppedDate` intact) with NO `umd` bump; a reopen (status→open / stoppedDate→null / umd bump) fails the ordering delta. The O06 heading-child reparent protection is unchanged and takes precedence over the permit.
  - (`src/write/{pre-state,commands,pipeline,reorder,move}.ts`, `src/write/verify/delta.ts`. Tests: `test/engine/write-universal-reorder.test.ts`, `test/unit/write-compile.test.ts`. Docs: `docs/capability-matrix.md`, `docs/up-next.md`. Evidence: `docs/lab/logsort-logged-child-reorder.md` (#401, ORD-13).)

- **CHANGE (breaking, ALPHA) — read-shape doctrine v2, PR 4 of 5: the global `upcoming` view becomes chronological day-block `sections`, with R7 dual-citizen seating locked across every view.** Fourth migration PR of the ratified [read-shape doctrine v2](docs/design/read-shape-doctrine.md). `things upcoming` / `read_view upcoming` `data` is now **`{ sections: [{when, items, total?} …] }`** — the flat `data.items[]` is GONE (ALPHA — clean break, no shims):
  - **Day blocks.** Each dated section is one chronological DAY BLOCK keyed by **`when`** (an ISO date that doubles as the `--in <when>` reorder token and states the block's COMPLETE global day scope), followed by ONE trailing **`{when: null, items}`** resting block holding the date-less recurring templates (#V8) — present only when such rows exist (membership-matched to what trailed the flat list). A scheduled row groups under its `startDate`, a deadline-forecast row (a stage-`anytime`/`someday` row with a future deadline, cohort 2) under its DEADLINE day, and a template under its next occurrence (or the resting block).
  - **R7 projection-side seating.** A row KEEPS its `stage` (the view is stage-MIXED — future-dated `upcoming` rows beside deadline-forecast `anytime`/`someday` ones — so dropping it would lose real information) and drops `when` only where it equals the block's date (a scheduled row's does; a forecast row has none; a horizon-projected divergent `when` is kept). Every row keeps its container refs (a global mixed view).
  - **Inline truncation (R1).** `total` is present INLINE on a day block iff the flat row cap (`--limit`, default 50) straddled it — the cut runs across the day-ordered stream, so at most one block is partial and blocks fully past the cut never appear. This view used a flat `truncateList` cap, never a `blocks[]` sidecar, so nothing was retired; the whole-view `shown`/`total`/`limit`/`truncated` rollup still rides `meta.truncation` and drives the row hint.
  - **Cross-view dual-citizen regression locks (doctrine §5 step 4).** A single clearly-named suite (`test/unit/cross-view-seating.test.ts`) asserts, on a shared fixture of dual citizens (someday+deadline, anytime+deadline, template projections): (a) global `upcoming` seats them at their projected/deadline day block; (b) container views (project / area) seat them ONLY in canonical stage buckets, never a day block; (c) NO uuid appears twice within any single view's payload (today, upcoming, project-view, area-view, anytime, someday); (d) global someday/anytime seat them canonically.
  - **TTY byte-identical.** The renderer groups the same flat list by day internally, so only the wire reshaped — proven byte-for-byte via the stash-revert method across every `upcoming` variant (bare/`--all`/`--until`/`--limit`/`--horizon`/`--until`+`--limit`) plus the full render suite.
  - No new `apiVersion`; no new error code; the envelope schema is unchanged (the `data` payload is an open object). (`src/read/{shape,truncation}.ts`, `src/{client,index}.ts`, `src/cli/{read-driver,commands/reads}.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`. Tests: `test/unit/{shape,stage,cross-view-seating}.test.ts`, `test/cli/e2e.test.ts`, `test/mcp/server.test.ts`. Docs: `docs/contract.md`, `docs/design/{contracts,read-shape-doctrine}.md`, `skills/things-cli/references/{contracts,model}.md`.)

- **Fixed — Logbook date labels now render in the consumer zone, not the host zone.** The TTY Logbook's month/year block headings (`renderLogbook`) and the blue logged-date chip (`loggedDate`, also on `todo show` / `project show` and every resolved list row) formatted the `stopDate` INSTANT through JavaScript `Date`'s host-zone getters, so a completion near local midnight could label the wrong calendar day (and month/year block) for a consumer whose `THINGS_TZ` differs from the host. Both now resolve the instant's calendar day through the consumer zone (`instantDateIso` in `src/model/dates.ts`), matching the app's own viewer-local Logbook grouping (Z-LOGVIEW). Membership math was already zone-correct (`src/model/clock.ts`); this was purely a label-formatting bug. Locked by a two-direction regression fixture (a `stopDate` near local midnight rendered under a zone 14h ahead vs 11h behind lands in the correct — and different — month/day) in `test/cli/render.test.ts`. Full timezone-behavior reference: `docs/reference/timezones.md`. (`src/model/dates.ts`, `src/cli/{glyphs,render}.ts`, `src/cli/commands/{todo,project}.ts`, `src/index.ts`.)

- **CHANGE (breaking, ALPHA) — read-shape doctrine v2, PR 3 of 5: the `area show` / `get_area` view becomes `children` bucket records + a `projects` record, with the per-block truncation sidecar retired for inline `total`s.** Third migration PR of the ratified [read-shape doctrine v2](docs/design/read-shape-doctrine.md). `data.view` is now **`{ area, children, projects }`** and NOTHING else at that level (ALPHA — clean break, no shims):
  - **`area`** is the area node (tags folded to string names), or **`null`** for the `loose` pseudo-area (unchanged discriminant).
  - **`children`** is the area's DIRECT to-dos as THREE stage-keyed bucket RECORDS — `anytime: {items, total?}`, `upcoming: [{when, items, total?} …]` (the day-block ARRAY, chronological — the block key renames `date` → **`when`**, R3 — ending with a single trailing **`{when: null, items}`** resting block when the area holds date-less recurring templates, #V8), `someday: {items, total?}`. There is **NO `logbook` key** (#346): an area has no logged-children region, and its logbook stays the bounded query `things logbook --area <ref>`.
  - **`projects`** is the child-project sidebar-rank scope as ONE bucket record `{items, total?}` — a mixed-stage listing whose rows KEEP `stage`/`when` (the someday-projects / active split stays TTY-only) and drop `area`.
  - **Ancestry / redundancy (R6):** every direct-to-do row drops `area` and its bucket-implied `stage` (and, being un-headed by construction, carries no `heading`). Dual citizens single-seat (R7): a someday/anytime direct to-do with a deadline stays in its canonical stage bucket and is NEVER duplicated into a day block; no uuid appears twice.
  - **Inline truncation (R1) — the `blocks[]` sidecar retires for THIS view.** Each capped scope carries its inline `total` (present iff `items.length < total`): `children.anytime` for the `--area-limit` cap and `projects` for the `--project-limit` cap on the ACTIVE project rows (the scheduled/someday direct-to-do blocks and scheduled/someday project rows always survive, so they never carry a `total`). `meta.truncation` no longer carries `blocks` for `area show` / `get_area` — its whole-view `shown`/`total`/`limit`/`truncated` rollup survives. The **TTY render is byte-identical** across every `area show` variant (projects/someday-projects/direct/upcoming/loose, `--show-later`, `--area-limit`/`--project-limit`, `--all`, `--tag`, and the bare-shorthand / `show` router — verified byte-for-byte on a rich fixture + the full render suite): the renderer reads the full bounded view + per-block truncation directly, not the wire.
  - No new `apiVersion`; no new error code; the envelope schema is unchanged (the `data` payload is an open object). (`src/read/{shape,truncation}.ts`, `src/{client,index}.ts`, `src/cli/{read-driver,commands/area,commands/show}.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`. Tests: `test/unit/shape.test.ts`, `test/cli/{e2e,loose-pseudo-area,project-today-pips}.test.ts`, `test/mcp/server.test.ts`. Docs: `docs/contract.md`, `docs/design/{contracts,read-shape-doctrine}.md`, `skills/things-cli/references/contracts.md`.)
- **CHANGE (breaking, ALPHA) — read-shape doctrine v2, PR 2 of 5: the `project show` / `get_project` view becomes recursive `children` + `headings[]` with per-container `logbook`.** Second migration PR of the ratified [read-shape doctrine v2](docs/design/read-shape-doctrine.md). `data.view` is now **`{ project, children, headings[] }`** and NOTHING else at that level (ALPHA — clean break, no shims):
  - **`children`** (the project's own un-headed body) is the four stage-keyed bucket RECORDS: `anytime: {items, total?}`, `upcoming: [{when, items, total?} …]` (the day-block ARRAY, chronological — the block key renames `date` → **`when`**, R3 — ending with a single trailing **`{when: null, items}`** resting block when the container holds date-less recurring templates, #V8/#V10), `someday: {items, total?}`, and per-container **`logbook: {items, total?}`** (the body's swept/resolved children, most-recently-completed first — R6).
  - **`headings[]`** is EVERY heading in `index` order and ALL lifecycle classes — open, archived-unswept, AND archived-swept (R5, one entity one place) — each `{ uuid, title, archived?, children }` with the SAME recursive `children` shape. A swept heading's logged children ride ITS `children.logbook`; an anomalous open child stranded under an archived heading (HEADARC2-C) rides that heading's `children.anytime`, the heading's `archived` mark making the anomaly self-evident.
  - **DELETED (#V12):** `logbookHeadings`, the root `logbook`, and BOTH advisory keys `openChildrenWhileResolved` / `openChildrenUnderArchivedHeading`. The GUI's unified logbook region (loose logged rows interleaved with swept-archived-heading groups) and the odd-child advisories are TTY-DERIVED from the per-container `children.logbook`, never encoded — the library keeps its internal structured grouping (it owns the clock + sweep boundary) and the **TTY render is byte-identical** (`things project show`, all variants incl. `--show-later`/`--show-logged`, HEADARC2/3 fidelity — verified byte-for-byte on a rich fixture + the full render suite).
  - **Ancestry / redundancy (R6, task item 6):** every bucket row drops `project`/`area`, its bucket-implied `stage`, and its `heading` ref — membership is STRUCTURAL now (position under the body `children` or a `headings[]` entry states it), so a swept child no longer carries the heading ref that the v1 flat logbook kept. Dual citizens single-seat (R7): a container child with a deadline stays in its canonical stage bucket and is NEVER duplicated into a day block (a deadline never makes the stage `upcoming`); no uuid appears twice in the view.
  - `total` is present INLINE iff a bucket was capped (R1); the project view is uncapped today, so every `total` is absent (the inline-truncation sweep is PR 5). No new `apiVersion`; no new error code; the envelope schema is unchanged (the `data` payload is an open object). (`src/read/{project-view,shape}.ts`. Tests: `test/unit/{shape,stage}.test.ts`, `test/cli/e2e.test.ts`, `test/mcp/server.test.ts`. Docs: `docs/contract.md`, `docs/design/{contracts,read-shape-doctrine,api-doctrine}.md`, `skills/things-cli/references/{contracts,model}.md`.)

- **CHANGE (breaking, ALPHA) — read-shape doctrine v2, PR 1 of 5: the `today` view reshapes to two keyed `children` bucket records + `meta.counts`.** First migration PR of the ratified [read-shape doctrine v2](docs/design/read-shape-doctrine.md) (buckets are reorder scopes). The `today` JSON view no longer uses `data.sections[]`/`data.badge` (both DELETED — ALPHA, no shims):
  - **`data.children = { today: {items, total?}, evening: {items, total?} }`** — two keyed bucket records; the keys are the `--in today` / `--in evening` reorder scopes (R2). Each bucket's `total` is present INLINE **iff** that bucket was capped (`items.length < total`, R1) — an untruncated bucket never restates its own length, so per-bucket completeness is answerable locally with no truncation sidecar (`meta.truncation.sections` / the `SectionCount` type are retired for `today`). The bucket key states today/evening, so a row's `when` is dropped inside the buckets (every Today member is stage-`anytime`, so `stage` drops too).
  - **`meta.counts = { dueOrOverdue, other }`** — the app's sidebar count (OPEN members, deadline ≤ today vs. the rest) moves off `data` onto `meta`, so `data` stays pure domain rows. Over MCP it rides the result's metadata block beside `truncation`. This re-lands v1 ruling #C1: the word **"badge" is purged from all consumer copy** and added to the banned-vocabulary regression lists (`docs/design/surface-copy.md` rule 6; `test/cli/help-contract.test.ts` + `test/mcp/server.test.ts`).
  - **TTY** (v1-ratified treatment restored): a clean `── ★ Today ──` header, with the counts rendered at the TOP as card-style metadata lines (indented `due/overdue:` / `other:`, the `things area show` header-metadata convention); This Evening is re-projected from the library's internal Today/Evening grouping (only the wire changed). Envelope schema regenerated.
  - This is ALPHA-CONTRACT: a clean break — no compatibility shims, no dual shapes. No new `apiVersion`; no new error code. (`src/read/{views,scope,area-filter,truncation,shape}.ts`, `src/{contracts,client,index}.ts`, `src/cli/{read-driver,render,commands/reads}.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`. Tests: `test/unit/{views,truncation,shape,stage,serialize,area-filter,render-styling}.test.ts`, `test/cli/{render,e2e,view-area-filter,help-contract}.test.ts`, `test/mcp/server.test.ts`, `test/contract/envelope-schema.test.ts`. Docs: `docs/contract.md`, `docs/design/{contracts,surface-copy,read-shape-doctrine}.md`, `skills/things-cli/`.)

- **CHANGE (breaking, ALPHA) — the read-shape doctrine (PRs #375–#379, shipped in 0.13.0) is REVERTED in reverse order; the `today` / project / area JSON views return to their pre-0.13.0 bucketed shapes.** The five doctrine PRs — today dissolve + badge purge (#375), project-view flatten (#376), project-view headings catalog + logbook flatten (#377), area-view flatten (#378), and the ordering-contract + skill sweep (#379) — are unwound cleanly:
  - **`today`** returns to `data.sections[]` (Today-proper / This-Evening) with the sidebar count back on `data.badge {dueOrOverdue, other}`; the flat `data.items[]` is gone and the "badge" vocabulary is un-purged.
  - **`project show` / `get_project`** returns to per-stage child buckets (`anytime` / `upcoming` / `someday`) under each heading and unheaded, the `{heading: node}` heading groups, and the separate `logbookHeadings` alongside the flat `logbook`; the flat `items[]` + heading catalog are gone.
  - **`area show` / `get_area`** returns to its direct-to-do stage buckets; the flat `items[]` is gone (`projects[]` is unchanged).
  - **`meta.counts` is removed** (the today count rides `data.badge` / the today-view metadata again).
  - This is ALPHA-CONTRACT: a clean revert — no compatibility shims, no dual shapes, no alias maps. The read-shape approach is under re-audit; `docs/design/read-shape-doctrine.md` is retained (status REVERTED) as the input to that review. The unrelated 0.13.0 flat-container-ref / `projectIsTemplate` / heading-`archived` changes are NOT affected. No new `apiVersion`; no new error code.

- **CHANGE (breaking, ALPHA) — resolution timestamps become universal flags; the bespoke `todo backdate` / `todo add-logged` ops are deleted (plan PR A, targets 0.14.0).** Setting an item's completion/creation timestamps is now `--created-at` / `--completed-at` on `add`, `update`, `complete`, and `cancel`, for BOTH to-dos AND projects — one field per flag, per the signed-off `docs/design/resolution-timestamp-surface.md`. The two single-purpose ops (`todo.backdate`, `todo.add-logged`) and their CLI commands, client methods, and MCP tools are removed outright (no shims); MCP parity for the new flags rides PR B.
  - **`add --completed-at [--created-at]`** — born RESOLVED straight to the Logbook (or born-open with a backdated creation), via the `things:///json` import (the only at-creation backdating surface, P4d/B-PROJ-JSON). A project `add --completed-at` that would seed OPEN child to-dos is refused (§5b: a completed-project import silently reverts to open unless every child is resolved).
  - **`update --created-at`** rewrites creation on any status (status-safe AS write); **`update --completed-at`** edits an already-resolved item's completion timestamp — a completed item in one AS leg, a CANCELED item via the certified 3-leg flip-dance (→completed · AS backdate · →canceled) that keeps it canceled; an OPEN item is REFUSED, pointing at `complete`/`cancel` (the open↔resolved boundary stays out of `update`).
  - **`complete --completed-at`** resolves-then-backdates (1–2 legs); **`cancel --completed-at`** ends canceled with the backdated "Completed on" stamp via the flip-dance (1–3 legs). Multi-leg sequences are NON-ATOMIC and fully disclosed — the leg list in the result (and a partial-failure recovery message) and in `--dry-run`; no new consent flag (disclosure over consent, every leg individually certified). A project whose backdate would have to transit through completed while it still holds OPEN children is refused.
  - **Zone-aware dates (§5):** each flag takes an ISO date OR datetime; a date-only value normalizes to NOON in the effective zone (per-call `zone` → `THINGS_TZ` → embedding zone → process-local — the same chain reads use), so it decodes to the intended calendar date in every viewer's zone.
  - The generalized **WG-7 law** is re-homed onto the new `todo/project.set-dates` ops: the AS `set completion date` leg fires EXCLUSIVELY against a verified-COMPLETED row (an OPEN row would be force-completed, a CANCELED row silently re-completed) — the orchestrators route a canceled item through the certified flip legs first. New mutations are undoable (single-leg set-dates like the old backdate; multi-leg composites replay their legs in reverse under one txn summary token). (`src/write/operations.ts`, `src/write/commands.ts`, `src/write/resolution-timestamps.ts`, `src/write/guards.ts` H-BACKDATE-OPEN, `src/write/{reversibility,undo,pipeline}.ts`, `src/model/dates.ts`, `src/client.ts`, `src/cli/commands/writes.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`. Tests: `test/engine/write-resolution-timestamps.test.ts` (§2 table + §5 normalization + flip-dance), `test/unit/{write-compile,write-guards,reversibility-matrix}.test.ts`, `test/cli/e2e.test.ts`. Docs: `docs/capability-matrix.md`, `docs/design/resolution-timestamp-surface.md`. Evidence: `docs/lab/backdt-project-backdating-and-flips.md` (#404), `docs/lab/s-campaign-results.md` P4a–P4d.)

- **FEATURE — MCP parity for the resolution-timestamp flags (plan PR B, targets 0.14.0).** `created_at` / `completed_at` params are now folded onto the existing MCP write tools wherever the CLI has `--created-at` / `--completed-at`, with the identical per-status semantics (refusals, flip-dance leg plans, disclosure) — no new tools, mirroring PR A's engine:
  - **`add_todo` / `add_project`** gain `created_at` and `completed_at` — a born-resolved Logbook import (or born-open with a backdated creation); an `add_project` `completed_at` that would seed an open child is refused (§5b).
  - **`update`** (kind todo/project) gains `created_at` (status-safe) and `completed_at` (rewrite an already-resolved item's completion timestamp; a canceled item rides the 3-leg flip-dance staying canceled; an OPEN item is refused, pointing at `set_status`).
  - **`set_status`** (status completed/canceled, both scopes) gains `completed_at` — resolve-and-backdate, the "Completed on" stamp applying to canceled items too; `completed_at` with status open is refused. The tool also gains `tz` so a date-only value normalizes to noon in the caller's zone.
  - Multi-leg sequences disclose their leg plan in MCP results exactly as the CLI does — the leg list rides the dry-run plan's `invocation` and the applied result's `warnings`. Shared parameter vocabulary (`RESOLUTION_DATE_FORMAT`) keeps the format wording identical across surfaces. (`src/mcp/server.ts`, `src/surface-copy.ts`. Tests: `test/mcp/server.test.ts` (§2 per-status semantics incl. a flip-dance leg-plan assertion and the open-item refusal). Docs: `docs/capability-matrix.md`, `skills/things-cli/SKILL.md` (already swept in PR A).)

## 0.13.0 — 2026-08-04

- **FIX — `things reorder` can now sort a MIXED to-do + project day set that ALSO contains a repeating template, in one call.** A day-block already intermixes to-dos and projects, and a template's projected occurrence already sorts on that block — but a set combining BOTH kinds AND a template was wrongly refused up front ("one kind at a time"), because the mixed-kind day relaxation had never been taught to count a template as a day member. It now does, so the full grand interleave — scheduled to-dos and projects (loose, in-project, headed, area-direct), deadline-forecast to-dos and projects, and the day's to-do template — reorders to any target in a single `things reorder … --in <YYYY-MM-DD>` call, each row's collateral preserved (heading and area links, the forecast rows' deadlines, the template's rule/projection, all byte-identical). The relaxation stays scoped to the day/Today/Evening axes — a stage-list or container `--in` token (`someday`/`anytime`/`inbox`/a project/area/heading) still sorts one kind at a time.
  - **Refusals surface cleanly.** When a template day-group genuinely can't be reordered headlessly — a project template asked to sit above a movable row, or any template while `allow-experimental` is off — the block is now reported as a top-level `BLOCKED (H-REORDER-SCOPE)` (exit 4) with its guidance up front, instead of being buried inside a generic "reorder leg did not complete" failure (exit 3). Nothing is ever touched before the refusal. Certified on the golden (Things 3.22.12): o-suite **O38/O39** (the full mixed-kind inventory, forward and reversed) on two clean passes + e2e-write-smoke (132 steps). (`src/write/move.ts` `globalAxisIntermix` template disjunct + the `repositionFailed` hoist + `MoveRefused.hazard`; `src/cli/commands/writes.ts` + `src/mcp/server.ts` canonical `blocked:<hazard>` rendering. Tests: `test/engine/write-move.test.ts` — the full mixed inventory compiles (all leg families), the reversed re-sort, day-axis scoping, the crash-path lock, and the canonical refusal hoist. Docs: `docs/capability-matrix.md`, `docs/reference/assumption-register.md` ORD-19, `docs/reference/suite-audit.md`. Evidence: `docs/lab/grndint2-mixed-interleave-fix.md`.)

- **FEATURE — `things reorder` can now position a repeating TEMPLATE in its Upcoming day-block.** A repeating template's projected occurrence renders in an Upcoming day-block, and its projection is now a first-class member of that day's reorder scope — previously templates were refused everywhere (a dated `when=` leg crashes a template, so ordering was app-default). Two constraints follow from the crash rule, which forces the app's own private surface as the only safe writer:
  - **On TOMORROW, full power.** When a template's next occurrence is tomorrow, BOTH to-do and project templates sort inline with the day's scheduled rows in one native `list "Tomorrow"` call — any position, exact order.
  - **On a later future day, to-do templates sort anywhere; project templates hold their spot.** A TO-DO template front-inserts via a single-id `list "Upcoming"` native leg, interleaved with the day's scheduled/forecast rows on one shared axis. A PROJECT template has no headless reach on a non-tomorrow day (only the Tomorrow call or a manual drag place it), so it stays byte-untouched at the bottom and every other item sorts above it (the **suffix rule**); a reorder that would need a project template above a movable row — or would re-order two project templates — is REFUSED, naming the arrangement it CAN reach.
  - **Templates require `allow-experimental` on.** Any day-group reorder that includes a template needs the private native surface; with `allow-experimental` off it is REFUSED, NAMING the template(s) — never silently skipped, never routed onto a crash-path `when=`/`deadline=` leg. A template's placement write is `userModificationDate`-silent (disclosed in the result). A template id never resolves onto the `project id` specifier (which would reparent it) nor `list "Later Projects"` (which would poison the call).
  - This is PR 1 of the template-ordering wiring: code + simulator tests + docs. The VM **grand-interleave suite certification** (a scheduled + forecast + to-do-template + project-template one-pass interleave lock) and the probe rider are a follow-up sitting; the engine (simulator) locks are green now, the golden ordering locks are pending. (`src/write/move.ts` template day-group routing, `src/write/pre-state.ts` day/tomorrow/`upcoming` template-inclusive members, `src/write/reorder.ts` the `day` template leg family + suffix rule + experimental gate + `umd`-silent disclosure, `src/write/commands.ts` the `list "Upcoming"` single-id specifier. Tests: `test/engine/{write-reorder,write-move}.test.ts`. Docs: `docs/capability-matrix.md`, `docs/reference/assumption-register.md` ORD-19/WG-2, `skills/things-cli/references/ordering.md`. Evidence: `docs/lab/{tmplsort-template-protocol,ptmpl-project-templates}.md`, #391/#392.)

- **FIX — `things reorder` enforces the single-KIND rule on index-axis `--in` tokens (follow-up to the day-axis work above).** An INDEX bucket — a stage list (`anytime`/`someday`/`inbox`) or a project/area/heading container — sorts each object kind in its own rank space, so those axes take ONE kind; the GLOBAL `today`/`evening` and day-block (`upcoming`/`<YYYY-MM-DD>`) axes keep intermixing to-dos and projects.
  - **Closed a bypass:** a same-day deadline-forecast set with `--in someday` / `--in anytime` / `--in <container>` used to slip the bucket-only intermix relaxation and **silently compile** a mixed to-do+project `index` re-rank. It is now refused, naming each movee's kind — **even when the kinds share one container** (an area's someday to-dos and someday projects are different index buckets).
  - **`--in inbox` gives a KIND refusal, not a stage message.** A project/heading handed to `--in inbox` previously refused with a confusing "not inbox-stage" message; it now refuses on kind (a project is never an inbox reorder member).
  - **Headings are never reorder-bucket members.** A heading movee on ANY `--in` token (stage, container, day, or view) is refused stating a heading has no stage/day/view order of its own, pointing at `things project move-heading`.
  - **Coherence:** because a cross-kind set has no shared container index, it is single-axis — a BARE `things reorder` of a mixed-kind forecast set now auto-routes to the day-block instead of raising a dual-axis refusal that offered a container spelling which would itself kind-refuse. (`src/write/move.ts` `runInPlaceReorder` `indexKindRefusal` + the `--in`-aware `globalAxisIntermix` gate; `resolveReorderAxis` nulls the shared `indexTarget`/`fIndexTarget` for a cross-kind set. Tests: `test/engine/write-move.test.ts` — mixed-kind refusals on every index token incl. the shared-container trap, heading-movee refusals on all tokens, `--in inbox` kind refusal, and the global-axis intermix + bare cross-kind auto-route locks. Docs: `skills/things-cli/references/ordering.md`, `docs/design/cli-grammar.md`, `docs/reference/assumption-register.md` ORD-18.)

- **FEATURE — `things reorder --in` gains two day-axis tokens, and the dual-axis refusal now covers deadline-forecast sets.** `things reorder` disambiguates a set that is a member of more than one sortable bucket; this extends that to the Upcoming day-block.
  - **`--in <YYYY-MM-DD>`** names one exact Upcoming day-block: every movee must be on that day (scheduled for it, or carrying it as a deadline) and the date must be strictly future (a today/past date is refused, pointing at `--in today`). **`--in upcoming`** is the proxy — it derives the single future day the whole set shares and refuses (listing each item's day) when they span days. Neither needs a shared container — a day-block is ONE cross-container sortability bucket. Both route to the day-group reposition (the deadline-cycle / dated `when=` bounce).
  - **Deadline-forecast sets are now dual-axis-aware (§9o dual-citizen).** A forecast row sits BOTH in its Upcoming day-block (`todayIndex`) and in its container's someday/anytime list (`index`). A bare `things reorder` of a set where every movee is a forecast member of ONE shared day AND they all share one direct container (same project/heading/area, or all loose) is now REFUSED, naming the real date and the container/list spelling (`--in 2026-07-20` vs `--in someday` / `--in "Work"`) — the forecast twin of the existing Today/Evening refusal. A forecast set spanning containers, or a scheduled + forecast mix on one day, is coherent on only the day axis and still auto-routes there.
  - **Bug fix — an explicit `--in` is never overridden by the day auto-route.** `--in someday` / `--in anytime` / `--in <container>` on a same-day forecast set now compiles the certified container **`index`** re-rank (its someday/anytime list order), not the day-block bounce.
  - **`--in someday` / `--in anytime` / `--in inbox` tighten to sanity + membership checks.** `anytime`/`someday` require every movee to BE that stage AND share one direct container (same project, heading, area, or all loose); `inbox` requires only inbox-stage. A mismatch is refused, naming the offending movee(s). (`today`/`evening` unchanged.)
  - Also codifies the **AXIS-ISOLATION** invariant (assumption-register ORD-18): a reorder protocol targeting one axis must not mutate the other axis of any touched row. (`src/write/move.ts` `resolveReorderAxis` / `parseInTarget` / the day auto-route guard. Tests: `test/engine/write-move.test.ts`. Docs: `skills/things-cli/references/ordering.md`, `docs/design/cli-grammar.md`, `docs/reference/assumption-register.md` ORD-18, `docs/contract.md`. CLI `reorder --help` + MCP `reorder` tool copy. Evidence: `docs/lab/{dlbnc-deadline-cycle,upcdl-deadline-axis}.md` §9o.)

- **FEATURE — day-scope reorder now accepts DEADLINE-FORECAST PROJECTS, not just to-dos.** `things reorder` already sorted a someday/anytime **to-do** with a future deadline on its Upcoming day-block; that day-group now admits a someday/anytime **project** with a future deadline too. A forecast project is a first-class member of the same block `todayIndex` axis, interleaved with forecast to-dos, and reorders via the public-URL **`update-project` deadline-cycle** (clear + re-set the same deadline, byte-identical) — the per-row leg op is chosen by kind (`update` for a to-do, `update-project` for a project). The project's someday-bucket `index`, its `start`/`startBucket` (no accidental Today star — PROJSTAR-safe), its area link, and its tags all survive the cycle. So a mixed set of forecast to-dos and forecast projects on one deadline day reorders to an exact interleaved order in a single `things reorder` call. Same gates as the to-do case: `bounce-enabled` (not `allow-experimental`), never the re-dating `list "Tomorrow"` sort, and an INBOX row merely carrying the deadline is refused. Certified on the golden (Things 3.22.11): o-suite **O33** (forecast to-do + forecast PROJECT mixed-kind interleave). (`src/write/move.ts` `forecastDeadlineDay` type gate `type IN (0,1)` + `globalAxisIntermix` allowance, `src/write/pre-state.ts` day forecast cohort SELECT, `src/write/reorder.ts` per-kind forecast leg op. Tests: `test/engine/{write-reorder,write-move}.test.ts`. Docs: `docs/capability-matrix.md`, `docs/reference/assumption-register.md` ORD-17, `skills/things-cli/references/ordering.md`. Evidence: `docs/lab/tmpldl-projdl-deadline-cycle.md` PROJDL-2a/2b/2c, #385.)

- **FEATURE — `things reorder` now accepts DEADLINE-FORECAST rows, sorting them on their Upcoming day.** A someday/anytime to-do with a future **deadline** and no start date renders in the Upcoming block on its deadline day; it is now a first-class member of that day's reorder scope, sorted on the same axis as the day's scheduled rows and INTERLEAVED with them — so a mixed set of scheduled and deadline-forecast rows reorders in one call (`things reorder <a> <b> --before <c>`, etc.). The move is a public-URL **deadline-cycle** (clear + re-set the same deadline, byte-identical), so it works with `allow-experimental` OFF (still gated by `bounce-enabled`, like every multi-leg reorder) and never rides the native `list "Tomorrow"` sort (which would re-date a forecast row). An INBOX to-do that merely carries the same deadline is not on that day's axis and is refused with an honest message. Certified on the golden (Things 3.22.11): o-suite **O31** (forecast-only protocol proof, someday-bucket `index` byte-identical) + **O32** (scheduled/forecast mixed interleave). (`src/write/pre-state.ts` day-scope forecast members + inbox refusal, `src/write/reorder.ts` forecast-leg dispatch + `checkStillMember`, `src/write/move.ts` planner routing. Tests: `test/engine/{write-reorder,write-move}.test.ts`. Docs: `docs/capability-matrix.md`, `docs/reference/assumption-register.md` ORD-17/RD-8, `skills/things-cli/references/ordering.md`. Evidence: `docs/lab/dlbnc-deadline-cycle.md`, #383.)

- **DOCS — the read-view ordering contract is now documented, and the skill is swept for the read-shape doctrine's flat shapes.** Fifth and final PR of the read-shape doctrine (`docs/design/read-shape-doctrine.md`, ruling #C6) — docs only, no code/behavior change.
  - `docs/contract.md` gains a **"Read views — shapes and orderings"** section: every view's `data` shape + the exact ordering of each kept bucket, flagging the two presentation-derived orders (`trash` recency, `search` relevance) as such vs. the real user-controlled axes. (The envelope JSON Schema models only the envelope layer — the `data` payload is an open coverage boundary — so the bucket orderings are documented in prose, per that boundary.)
  - The `things-cli` skill (`SKILL.md`, `references/model.md`, `references/contracts.md`) is swept for the flat shapes landed across PRs 1–4: the flat `today` `items[]` + `meta.counts` (no `badge`/`sections`), the project card's flat `items` + heading catalog + flat `logbook` (no `anytime`/`upcoming`/`someday`/`logbookHeadings`), the area card's flat `items` + `projects`, and the corrected `stage`-drop / heading-ref rules.
  - `docs/things-app-oddities.md` §5o lede corrected: "the desktop GUI is stricter" → platform-accurate "the GUI is stricter" (the observation was iOS; macOS presumed but unverified — the residual flagged in #373).

- **CHANGE (breaking, ALPHA) — the `area show` / `get_area` view dissolves its direct-to-do buckets into ONE flat `items[]`; `projects[]` is kept.** Fourth of five PRs implementing the read-shape doctrine (`docs/design/read-shape-doctrine.md` §3.13). An area has TWO order axes (project sidebar rank, direct-to-do index), so it keeps two flat lists.
  - **`data.view.items`** — ONE flat list of every direct to-do in area `index` order, each row carrying `stage` (KEPT — the list is stage-mixed) and `when`, `area` dropped (the card states it). The `anytime` / `upcoming` / `someday` direct-to-do buckets are GONE. Area direct to-dos are never headed nor project-nested, so they are fully self-describing.
  - **`data.view.projects`** — UNCHANGED (the area's child-project rows in sidebar order, mixed-stage, someday/scheduled projects included). This is the DISTINCT order axis; the `projects` / someday-projects / direct / upcoming / loose render variants collapse to TTY projections of `projects[]` + `items[]`.
  - **Truncation / caps unchanged:** the per-block caps still apply (`project_limit` on project rows, `area_limit` on the open/current direct to-dos); the `area` block reports the open/current direct-to-dos hidden, and the flat `items[]` drops exactly those (the later scheduled/someday rows always ride `items[]` — the `--show-later` toggle is TTY-only, never gated the wire).
  - **TTY + truncation byte-stable** — verified byte-identical (incl. the `area_limit` cut) on a rich fixture + the full render/loose suites; the library retains its structured direct-to-do grouping for the GUI projection, only the wire flattened. Removed the now-dead `rebucketChildren` card-bucketing helper (no wire surface has stage/date sub-buckets anymore). (`src/read/area-view.ts` adds `items`, `src/read/truncation.ts` `capAreaSections`, `src/read/shape.ts` `shapeAreaView`.) Tests: `test/unit/{shape,truncation}.test.ts`, `test/cli/{e2e,loose-pseudo-area,render}.test.ts`, `test/mcp/server.test.ts`. Docs: `docs/design/read-shape-doctrine.md`, `docs/contract.md`, `docs/design/contracts.md`, MCP `get_area`/`get_project` descriptions. No new `apiVersion`; no new error code.

- **CHANGE (breaking, ALPHA) — the `project show` / `get_project` `headings[]` becomes a flat catalog and `logbookHeadings` dissolves into the flat `logbook`.** Third of five PRs implementing the read-shape doctrine (`docs/design/read-shape-doctrine.md` §3.12, rulings #C3/#C3a/#C4).
  - **`headings[]` is now the flat catalog** `[{uuid,title,archived?}]` — EVERY heading (live AND swept archived) in project `index` order (the order axis). The PR-2 `{heading: node}` wrapper is unwrapped. A swept archived heading is now a catalog entry carrying `archived` (it was previously only inside `logbookHeadings`). Under a content scope (`--overdue`/`--tag`) a heading whose children all filtered out collapses. A heading catalog entry carries only `archived` — never `stage`/`status`; sweptness is TTY-derived, not on the wire (#C3a).
  - **`logbookHeadings` is GONE.** Its archived-heading children fold into the single flat **`logbook`** bucket — now ONE `stopDate DESC` list of ALL swept children (of open headings, un-headed, AND archived headings), each carrying its `heading` ref (flat title + project-scoped `headingUuid`). An odd OPEN child a Put-Back stranded under an archived heading (HEADARC2-C) rides here too with its live `stage` kept — so the flat `logbook` now **KEEPS `stage`** (the bucket is stage-mixed; it previously dropped `stage` as pure). Reconstruct an archived heading's logged children by filtering `logbook` on `heading` + reading `archived` off the catalog.
  - **Unchanged:** `items`, the two advisories, the card node. TTY **byte-stable** — verified byte-identical on the rich fixture (before/after) + the full render suite; the library retains its structured logged-region grouping (`loggedHeadings`) to project the GUI-faithful layout (it owns the sweep boundary), only the wire flattened. (`src/read/project-view.ts` adds `headingCatalog`, `src/read/shape.ts` `shapeProjectView`.) Tests: `test/unit/shape.test.ts`, `test/cli/e2e.test.ts`, `test/mcp/server.test.ts`. Docs: `docs/design/read-shape-doctrine.md`, `docs/contract.md`, `docs/design/contracts.md`. No new `apiVersion`; no new error code.

- **CHANGE (breaking, ALPHA) — the `project show` / `get_project` view dissolves its live child buckets into ONE flat `items[]`.** Second of five PRs implementing the read-shape doctrine (`docs/design/read-shape-doctrine.md`, §3.12): a heading's membership is a per-row attribute, not a bucket.
  - **`data.view` shape (breaking).** The unheaded `anytime` / `upcoming` / `someday` buckets AND the per-heading stage sub-buckets are GONE. In their place, `data.view.items` is ONE flat list of every LIVE child (unheaded and headed) in project `index` order — each row carrying `stage` (KEPT; the list is stage-mixed) and `when`, and each HEADED row carrying its `heading` ref (a flat title + a project-scoped `headingUuid` when the title would not round-trip, kept even in compact). Reconstruct a heading's members by filtering `items` on `heading`. An unheaded row omits `heading`.
  - **`headings[]` is now the memberless live-heading catalog** — `[{heading:{uuid,title,archived?}}]` in index order (incl. empty headings; an archived-but-unswept heading still appears here carrying `archived`). Its per-heading member sub-buckets are gone (membership rides the row refs). PR 3 will unwrap it to `[{uuid,title,archived?}]`, merge swept archived headings in, and dissolve `logbookHeadings`.
  - **Unchanged this PR:** the flat `logbook` rows, the `logbookHeadings` archived-heading groups, `openChildrenWhileResolved` / `openChildrenUnderArchivedHeading`, and the project card node.
  - **TTY byte-stable.** `project show` output is byte-for-byte identical (verified by before/after capture on a rich fixture + the full render suite). The library retains its structured render grouping (it owns the clock + `todayIndex`) to project the GUI-faithful layout; only the wire flattened. (`src/read/project-view.ts` adds `items`, `src/read/shape.ts` `shapeProjectView`.) Tests: `test/unit/shape.test.ts` (flat items[] + memberless catalog + heading refs), `test/cli/e2e.test.ts` (CLI wire shape), `test/unit/stage.test.ts` (per-row stage property), `test/mcp/server.test.ts` (get_project). Docs: `docs/design/read-shape-doctrine.md`, `docs/contract.md`, `docs/design/contracts.md`. No new `apiVersion`; no new error code.

- **CHANGE (breaking, ALPHA) — the `today` view dissolves its Today/This-Evening `sections` into one flat `items[]`, and its count moves to `meta.counts`.** First of five PRs implementing the ratified read-shape doctrine (`docs/design/read-shape-doctrine.md`): JSON models semantic state, the TTY projects GUI placement. The Today-proper / This-Evening split is GUI placement recoverable per row from `when`, so it is no longer a wire bucket:
  - **`data` shape (breaking).** `today`'s `data` is now `{ items }` — one flat list of Today members in comparator order (Today-proper and This-Evening interleaved by their SQL position), each row carrying its `when` (`"today"` / `"evening"`) and `provisional`; `stage` is still dropped (every Today member is stage-pure `anytime`, R13). The former `data.sections` (`[{key:"today"},{key:"evening"}]`) is gone — read `data.items` and split on `when === "evening"`. The `when` field, previously section-dropped, is KEPT on every today row.
  - **Count → `meta.counts` (breaking).** The former `data.badge {dueOrOverdue, other}` moves to `meta.counts {dueOrOverdue, other}` (the app's sidebar count over OPEN members: due/overdue by deadline vs. the rest), so `data` stays pure domain rows. Over MCP the today count rides the result's metadata block alongside `truncation`.
  - **"badge" vocabulary purged.** The word "badge" is eliminated from all consumer-facing surfaces (JSON keys, TTY, `--help`, MCP descriptions, contract) per the read-shape doctrine, and added to the banned-vocabulary regression lists (`docs/design/surface-copy.md` rule 6; `test/cli/help-contract.test.ts`, `test/mcp/server.test.ts`). Evidence docs describing the app's GUI badge stay as they are.
  - **TTY redesign.** The Today header is now clean (`── ★ Today ──`); the counts render at the TOP as card-style metadata lines (`  due/overdue: N` / `  other: M`, the `project show` / `area show` header-metadata convention). This Evening is still re-projected below (GUI-faithful), and its honesty hint under truncation is preserved — `meta.truncation.sections` survives as completeness metadata for the two TTY render sections (derived per row from `when`), not a `data` bucket.
  - `--evening`, `--area`, `--overdue`, tag filters, and per-call `tz` are unchanged in behavior. (`src/read/views.ts`, `src/read/truncation.ts`, `src/read/shape.ts`, `src/read/area-filter.ts`, `src/read/scope.ts`, `src/cli/read-driver.ts`, `src/cli/render.ts`, `src/cli/commands/reads.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`, `src/contracts.ts`, `schema/envelope.schema.json`.) Docs: `docs/design/read-shape-doctrine.md` (finalized/ratified), `docs/contract.md`, `docs/design/contracts.md`, `docs/design/surface-copy.md`. No new `apiVersion`; no new error code.

- **CHANGE (breaking, ALPHA) — the `project show` heading model becomes GUI-faithful: a heading GROUP node drops `type`, replaces `status` with a presence-keyed `archived`, and swept archived headings group under the logged region.** Implements the HEADARC/HEADARC2 macOS logged-section fidelity spec (`docs/lab/headarc-archived-heading-moves.md`, `docs/lab/headarc2-residual-captures.md`). Five parts:
  - **Heading node shape (breaking).** A project-view heading GROUP node (`headings[].heading`, `logbookHeadings[].heading`) no longer carries `type` (its slot always states heading — the "absent `type` = to-do" convention is scoped to ROWS/candidates, not this keyed sub-object) nor `status`. It emits a presence-keyed **`archived`** — the ISO archive timestamp (the `stopDate`, same emission convention as a row's `stopped`) — present iff the heading is archived, absent when open. A heading's lifecycle is archive/unarchive ONLY (never completed/canceled — oddity §169), so the node carries neither `status` nor `stage`; `archived` carries both the archive boolean and its timestamp.
  - **Trinary live/logged split (the completion≠logged law applied to headings).** The live `headings` array now holds OPEN headings AND archived-but-UNSWEPT headings (archived, not yet past the logbook boundary — the same `log-boundary.ts` predicate the sibling to-do rows use); such a heading keeps its place among the live groups and carries `archived`. Only a SWEPT archived heading moves into the logged region as a grouped section. WHERE a heading renders expresses the sweep axis — no third field, mirroring how a to-do expresses logged-ness by bucket membership.
  - **Logged-region archived-heading GROUPS (`logbookHeadings`).** A swept archived heading renders under `--show-logged` as a grouped section — the heading header plus ALL its children nested (most-recently-completed first), INCLUDING any odd OPEN child a GUI Put-Back stranded there (HEADARC2-C — the group renders what is there, no status filter). No per-child heading sublabel (the group header states it). `--json` gains a `logbookHeadings: [{heading, items}]` sibling of the flat `logbook`.
  - **Flat logged rows carry their heading (the two-view asymmetry).** A swept child of an OPEN heading (or an un-headed child) stays a flat `logbook` row, but now carries its `heading` ref even in compact — JSON: bare title + a project-scoped `headingUuid` when the title would not round-trip; TTY: a muted `(Heading)` hint. The GLOBAL `logbook` view still labels the PROJECT, never the heading (HEADARC2-B — same row, different sublabel by view; verified unchanged).
  - **Advisory extension (`openChildrenUnderArchivedHeading`).** The project card gains a count of open children buried under a SWEPT archived heading — the §6¾ odd state for headings — surfaced regardless of the project's own status (the existing `openChildrenWhileResolved` covers the resolved-project case). The TTY advisory line names the archived-heading case for a reader of the card.
  - Ordering of the archived-heading groups relative to the flat logged rows, and the styling of an archived-UNSWEPT heading in the live area, are NOT GUI-probed under macOS (HEADARC2's captures were all swept): groups render after the flat rows, by heading index; an archived-unswept heading renders like an open one with the existing dim heading style (per maintainer-observed iOS evidence — the only difference is the accent/grey color). (`src/read/project-view.ts`, `src/read/shape.ts`, `src/model/entities.ts`, `src/model/mappers.ts`, `src/cli/commands/project.ts`, `src/cli/render.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`.) Tests: `test/unit/mappers.test.ts` (heading `stopped`), `test/unit/shape.test.ts` (node shape + `logbookHeadings` + flat-row heading ref), `test/cli/render.test.ts` (trinary split; swept group render; flat heading hint; odd-child advisory). Docs: `docs/contract.md` (`archived` glossary row + scoped `type` + two-view asymmetry), the `things-cli` skill (`references/model.md`/`contracts.md`), `docs/things-app-oddities.md` (maintainer GUI-observed constraints). No new `apiVersion`; no new error code.

- **FIX — a `--json` row whose container project is a repeating TEMPLATE again carries the template signal, as a flat presence-keyed sibling `projectIsTemplate: true` beside the `project` ref.** The fact lives on the parent project's recurrence columns and is stamped onto the resolved container ref (`makeRefResolver` / `makeHeadingProjectResolver`, `src/read/queries.ts`); the TTY render reads it off the unshaped entity to draw the `↻` glyph. When the JSON container ref was flattened from `{uuid,title}` to a bare title string, the boolean silently fell off the wire — TTY kept the glyph, JSON consumers lost the signal. The read-shaping transform now re-emits it as `projectIsTemplate: true` on any child row whose container project (directly, or via its heading's project) is a repeating template — presence-keyed like `provisional`/`hasNotes` (emitted only when true), both the compact and `--full` tiers (a correctness signal, not detail), riding wherever the `project` ref rides (so a project-view child that drops `project` carries no orphaned marker; the project card node keeps exposing its OWN template nature via `repeating`, R11, unchanged). Acting on a marked row edits the blueprint, affecting future occurrences — target the intended copy via `projectUuid` (a same-titled visible occurrence exists alongside the hidden template). TTY unchanged. (`src/read/shape.ts`.) Tests: `test/unit/shape.test.ts` (direct + heading-nested template children mark on both tiers; occurrence children carry no marker; project-view children carry no orphaned marker; card node's own `repeating` intact) and `test/unit/ref-shape.test.ts` (end-to-end over a synthetic template + same-titled occurrence fixture: the resolver-stamped ref survives the flatten and promotes `projectUuid` on the ambiguous title). Docs: `docs/contract.md` (new `projectIsTemplate` glossary row + the `project`-ref note), the `things-cli` skill (`references/model.md` container-ref rule). No new `apiVersion`; no new error code.

- **CHANGE — the `≡` normalized-form echo and every TTY container-ref suggestion now render the resolved entity's CANONICAL ref, not the raw string the user typed.** The echo prefix and the disclosure/truncation footers that embed a container ref (`… 6 later items — \`things area show "New Stuff" --show-later\``) were parroting the user's input verbatim; they now render the entity's canonical ref, computed from the SAME round-trip promotion predicate the JSON `*Uuid` promotion and the inline container mentions already consult (`makeRefPromoter` / `titleRoundTrips` / `fusedRef`, `src/read/queries.ts` — the predicate's third consumer, no fork):
  - the entity's **exact title, bare**, when that title round-trips back to it — so a case-wrong ref is corrected (`things new STUFF` → `≡ things project show "New Stuff"`), and a decorated input whose brackets were unnecessary (`"New Stuff [StRTjZ2x]"`) is taught the shorter bare title;
  - otherwise the **fused form** `Title [8charPrefix]`, which re-resolves through the typed command's uuid-prefix tier — so a ref whose bare title collides with an ambiguous live twin echoes the fused form that pins THIS entity. Every echoed/suggested command re-resolves to the same entity by construction; the title is shell-quoted (`src/cli/shell-quote.ts`) so spaces/quotes stay copy-pasteable.
  - **To-dos are exempt** — a to-do has no round-trippable title (and no read path for its fused form), so the loose router keeps echoing the uuid/partial-uuid it was given (already the shortest re-resolvable ref); a to-do detail card carries no footer.
  - **Verb coherence (footers):** a container footer now speaks the SAME canonical verb the echo teaches — the loose `show` unifies onto the resolved `area show` / `project show` (`things Busy --area-limit 3` → footer `things area show Busy --area-limit 6`, no longer `things show Busy …`) because `--show-later`/`--show-logged`/`--area-limit`/`--project-limit` are all valid there.
  - **The invocation name stays the literal `things`** in every echo/suggestion (documented decision): the actual wrapper spelling (`npx -y things-api …`, an alias) is unrecoverable from inside the process — argv begins at the resolved bin.
  - `meta.resolvedCommand` (the `--json` sibling of the echo) rides the same canonical string, so it carries the canonical ref too; the JSON envelope still has no echo LINE.
