When resolution FAILS nothing is recorded — the raw did-you-mean error stands. (`src/cli/canonical-ref.ts` (new), `src/cli/commands/{show,area,project}.ts`.) Tests: `test/cli/e2e.test.ts` (case-corrected echo; decorated-input echoes bare; ambiguous-twin uuid echoes the fused form + re-resolves; footer carries the corrected ref on the coherent verb; shell-quoting of a spaced/quoted title; failed resolution untouched), plus updated plural-synonym / namespace / loose-footer expectations in `test/cli/{e2e,help-contract}.test.ts`. Docs: `docs/design/cli-grammar.md` (canonical-ref rule + literal-`things` decision). No new `apiVersion`; no new error code.

- **FIX — read-side name resolution now obeys ONE liveness law, so the bare-ref shorthand and `things project show` agree, and an ambiguity's count matches its candidate list.** A name resolved on the read side now matches LIVE (untrashed) rows only; an explicit uuid / partial-uuid still reaches a trashed project (viewing one by id is unchanged). Previously `things project show <name>` widened its whole pool to include trashed rows, so a name with one live twin and several trashed same-name twins was reported as an ambiguity (`"New StUfF" matches 5 projects`) whose rendered candidate list held only the ONE live row — while the shorthand `things "New StUfF"` resolved the same name cleanly against the live pool. Now:
  - **Name tiers are live-only everywhere on the read side.** A dead same-name twin never shadows a live one nor inflates an ambiguity count (`src/read/queries.ts` `resolveProjectUuid` under `trashed: true`, via `resolveNamedRef`'s `nameExtraWhere` — the same split the write side already used).
  - **Reads-only ergonomic fallback.** A name matching ZERO live rows but EXACTLY ONE trashed project still resolves to it (viewing a trashed project by name); the render discloses it as before (the card's `(trashed)` marker / the JSON node's `stage: "trash"`). Several trashed-only twins → not-found with the honest dead-row hint (`… trashed items match this name — see \`things trash\``).
  - **Count / list coherence.** An ambiguity refusal's `matches N` counts the SAME live pool its candidate list renders; additional trashed twins are DISCLOSED (`… also matched: N in the trash — \`things trash\` lists them, a uuid reaches one directly`) rather than folded into the count.
  - **Bare-shorthand cross-kind candidates.** `classifyShowTarget` spans to-dos, areas, AND projects, so when a name is ambiguous at one kind while another kind also has live name matches, its refusal now MERGES the live candidates across kinds (`"X" matches 2 areas and 3 projects — use \`things area show\` / \`things project show\`, or a ref below`). Precedence for a UNIQUE winner is unchanged (uuid → area → project).
  - **CLI ambiguity routing is now code-based.** The read commands (`show`, `project show`, `area show`) detect an ambiguity by the resolution error's `code === "ambiguous"` rather than a fragile message-word match, so a read-side ambiguity surfaces its own candidate list verbatim (error `code: "ambiguous"`) instead of being mis-coded `not-found` and wrapped in a did-you-mean block. Write targets are UNCHANGED (already strict).

- **NEW — `--dry-run` is a UNIVERSAL flag, accepted by every `things` command (root included) with one contract: "guarantee nothing changes".** Previously only the DB-mutation write verbs declared it, so every read (`things today --dry-run`, `things show <ref> --dry-run`, the bare-ref shorthand, the view-keyword sugars) died with `error: unknown option '--dry-run'`. Now a single decorator (`src/cli/dry-run.ts`, applied last in `buildProgram`) walks the whole registered command tree and adds a hidden `--dry-run` to every command that does not already declare one, so present and future commands inherit it automatically. Three-way behavior by command class:
  - **DB-mutation verbs** (todo/project/area/tag write family, batch, undo, reorder) — unchanged: they already implement a real dry-run that previews the planned change and its expected effect.
  - **Pure reads** (all list views, `show`/`search`/`logbook`/`trash`, `config get`, `capabilities`, `doctor`, `snapshot`, `legend`, `help`, completions) — accept the flag and ignore it: output is byte-identical with and without it, `--json` and TTY alike (the normal view IS the correct dry-run of a read).
  - **Local-side-effect commands** — honor it honestly instead of silently ignoring it: `config set` previews `would set <key> = <value>` and persists nothing; `install-skill` names every location it would write and touches nothing; `setup shortcuts` reports the install sheets it would open and opens none; the `open`/`todo open`/`project open`/`area open` reveals return the `would open …` URI without foregrounding the app. `mcp` is un-previewable (it serves a live surface whose tools make changes on request), so it REFUSES `--dry-run` loudly (exit 2) rather than launch a server that ignores the promise.
  The argv resolver (`src/cli/resolve-invocation.ts`) now treats a LEADING `--dry-run` as a universal boolean global (like `--json`), so `things --dry-run <subject>` normalizes onto the resolved command exactly as a trailing flag would; `things --dry-run` alone no longer errors. The root help gains one line stating the contract; per-command help stays quiet (the read/side-effect option is hidden). This makes `scripts/explore/server.ts`'s fail-closed `--dry-run` force-append harmless even for a read its allowlist misses. (`src/cli/dry-run.ts` (new), `src/cli/main.ts`, `src/cli/resolve-invocation.ts`, `src/cli/help.ts`, `src/cli/commands/{writes,install-skill,setup,mcp,reads,show,todo,project,area}.ts`, `scripts/explore/server.ts`.) Tests: `test/cli/universal-dry-run.test.ts` (completeness lock over the whole tree; read byte-identity incl. bare-ref shorthand + a view keyword, `--json` and TTY; `config set`/`install-skill`/`setup shortcuts` previews write nothing; `mcp` refuses; leading-flag normalization). Docs: the `things-cli` skill (`SKILL.md`). No new `apiVersion`; no new error code.

- **NEW — the SHORT-REF convention: decorated `Title [ref]` refs are accepted in every ref slot, and every TTY disambiguation candidate renders the round-trippable fused form.** Four parts:
  - **Decorated-ref input tier.** The shared resolver core (`resolveNamedRef`, `src/read/queries.ts`) gains a LAST tier: `Title [ref]` — the bracketed segment (`[0-9A-Za-z]{4,22}`) resolves through the uuid/partial-uuid tier and the **title half is an ignored comment**, so a stale copy still resolves after a rename. It runs regardless of whether a slot enables the bare uuid-prefix tier (the bracket is explicit ref intent), so EVERY `<ref>` slot inherits it — CLI flags (`--to-project`/`--to-heading`/`--area`), MCP args, library refs. A *literal* title like `Family [ab12cd34]` still wins (exact-title is an earlier tier); the empty-title form `[prefix]` (a titleless heading — its fused form ` [prefix]` trims the leading space) is legal; a bracket below the 6-char partial-uuid floor parses as the form but does not resolve.
  - **Unified fused candidate form `Title [8charPrefix]`** (`fusedRef`, exported). The did-you-mean area line (which printed a full 22-char uuid in parens) and the `resolveProjectWriteTarget` / `resolveHeadingUuid` ambiguity listings all render the fused form now — a candidate pastes straight back as a decorated ref.
  - **Inline TTY container-hint promotion.** A human list row's `(Family)` container hint renders `(Family [TC9yozLk])` when the SHARED PR-1 promotion predicate fires for that ref (no fork — the render driver arms `client.refPromoter()` via a render-time ambient, `src/cli/ref-render.ts`; TTY only, the `--json` path shapes its own refs). Bare `(title)` when the promoter is unset (non-TTY / no DB) or the title round-trips.
  - **Date-annotated duplicate candidates (TTY only).** When a did-you-mean candidate list has duplicate-titled project/to-do candidates, each duplicate line gets a `· started YYYY-MM-DD` tail (from the entity's creation date) so a human can pick between recurring twins. The structured JSON `candidates[]` payload is unchanged.
  - (`src/read/queries.ts`, `src/read/area-filter.ts` re-exercised, `src/cli/ref-render.ts` (new), `src/cli/render.ts`, `src/cli/did-you-mean.ts`, `src/cli/read-driver.ts`, `src/index.ts`.) Tests: `test/unit/short-refs.test.ts` (decorated tier + precedence + empty-title + too-short + ambiguous + the `--to-project`/`--area`/`--to-heading` slots), `test/unit/render-styling.test.ts` (inline promotion), `test/unit/did-you-mean.test.ts` (fused area line + duplicate-title dates + JSON payload unchanged). Docs: the `things-cli` skill (`SKILL.md`, `references/errors.md`/`ordering.md`), `docs/design/reference-resolution.md` (tier 5), `docs/design/heading-demotion-and-move.md` (polymorphic `--in`), `docs/contract.md`. No new `apiVersion`; no new error code.

- **CHANGE (breaking, ALPHA) — `--json` container refs flatten to bare title strings + a conditional `*Uuid` sibling, and a to-do row omits `type`.** Two shape changes to every `--json` read row (and the error `candidates`):
  - **Flat container refs.** `area`/`project`/`heading` are now bare **title strings** (`"area": "Family"`) instead of nested `{uuid, title}` objects. A flat sibling `areaUuid`/`projectUuid`/`headingUuid` (the full uuid) rides alongside **only when the round-trip law demands it** — the bare title would NOT resolve back, through its own resolver in its own scope, to that exact entity. The promotion predicate is the RESOLVER'S OWN judgment (`titleRoundTrips`/`makeRefPromoter`, `src/read/queries.ts`), not a re-derived uniqueness query: projects run through `resolveProjectWriteTarget` (uuid-prefix tier first, over the live+open write-target name pool — so a logged/completed/trashed same-titled twin never triggers promotion, but a title that is a valid unique uuid-prefix of another task does), areas over all areas, headings within their project. Not-found / ambiguous both read as "does not round-trip" → promote. Memoized per (kind, title, scope) for one response emission. The **FULL** tier (`--full` / `show` / `detail`) emits every `*Uuid` sibling unconditionally. To act on a ref, pass `.areaUuid // .area` (same for project/heading); for unattended pipelines or stored refs, use `--full` and key on the uuids. (A container ref's `isRepeatingTemplate` marker no longer rides the flattened JSON ref — it survives only on the human render, which reads the unshaped entity.)
  - **Absent `type` = to-do.** Every `--json` row (and every error candidate) omits `type` when it is a to-do; `type` stays present for `project`, `heading`, `area`, and `tag`. `omitEmpty` recognizes a type-less uuid+title row as a to-do so its empty optional fields still prune.
  - The single-source shaping transform threads a `RefPromoter` (the client exposes `refPromoter()`; a DB-less unit shaping assumes every title round-trips → bare titles, no uuid siblings). Existing R6 ancestry-drop rules are unchanged. (`src/read/shape.ts`, `src/read/queries.ts`, `src/model/serialize.ts`, `src/client.ts`, `src/cli/read-driver.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`, `src/surface-copy.ts`, `src/index.ts`.) Tests: `test/unit/ref-shape.test.ts` (colliding live pair promotes; logged/completed twins do not; uuid-lookalike prefix promotes; area collide/unique; per-project heading scope; FULL forces siblings; `type` omission; DB-less default), plus updated `test/unit/shape.test.ts` / `test/unit/candidate-ref.test.ts` / `test/unit/serialize.test.ts` / `test/cli/e2e.test.ts` / `test/mcp/server.test.ts`. Docs: the `things-cli` skill (`SKILL.md`, `references/model.md`/`contracts.md`/`errors.md`) and the MCP read-tool descriptions gain the ref/`type` contract sentences. No new `apiVersion`; no new error code.

## 0.12.0 — 2026-08-01

- **DOCS (agent skill) — the bundled `things-cli` skill re-swept for the full move/reorder + banner/liveness + error-contract vocabulary; restructured to a lean happy-path `SKILL.md` plus progressive-disclosure dossiers (roadmap §N phase 4).** The always-loaded `SKILL.md` now teaches the current `stage`/`when` decomposition (replacing the deleted `start`/`todaySection` reasoning), the compact/full tiers with `hasNotes`/`checklist`/`todos`/`match`, the kind-neutral `things reorder` with dual-axis `--in`, the guarded-write acknowledgments, the `loose` pseudo-area, and the MCP `reorder`/`reorder_areas` names — each as a one-liner routed to a dossier so agents that don't need the depth pay no context cost. Three new deep-dive references join `model.md`/`contracts.md`/`gui.md`: `ordering.md` (axes, gates `allow-experimental`/`bounce-enabled`/`bounce-max-items`, the SIT7 automatic non-experimental fallbacks, SIT6 flag-aware routing, placement guarantees, templates as the one dead class), `errors.md` (the fixed candidate shape capped at 8, live-scoped pool + dead-row hints, the hazard acknowledgments incl. `H-AREA-NOT-EMPTY`/`--allow-non-empty` and the `H-UI-DRIVE` two-key gate, and the error-code registry), and `banner.md` (the provisional `•` pip + "N new to-dos" banner law, the `today ok` ⛔ decision and the deadline-rewrite dismiss primitive, watcher implications, and §9n reminder/evening liveness). The skill's `minSupported` floor rises to **0.12.0** (the release that ships this behavior); the repo `SKILL.md` stamp stays `0.0.0-dev` (stamped at publish, #249). Every taught claim was re-verified against the live CLI `--help`/`--json` (read-only, against a synthetic fixture) and `docs/contract.md` across the refinement loops; the review also corrected a stale `when`-glossary example in `docs/contract.md` (a deadline-pulled Someday/Inbox row derives `stage: "anytime"`, R13 — not `someday`/`inbox`). `install-skill` packaging tests now assert all six references travel with the copy. Files: `skills/things-cli/**`, `docs/contract.md`, `test/cli/install-skill.test.ts`, `docs/roadmap.md`.
- **NEW — every reorder scope now works WITHOUT `allow-experimental`, via automatic non-experimental fallbacks (SIT7).** The scopes that used to run ONLY through the private `_private_experimental_ reorder` command (`inbox`, `someday`, `project` unheaded children, `area` members, `container-day`, `tomorrow`) no longer fail-explain when that surface is unavailable — when `allow-experimental` is off OR the sdef canary fails, each degrades to a proven, verified, flag-safe protocol (`docs/lab/sit7-backup-laws.md`). Re-entry geometry follows destination containment (LOOSE-like buckets FRONT-insert via a reverse-target dispatch; CONTAINERS BACK-insert via a forward-target dispatch), and every move leg preserves the Today/Evening flag + reminder + deadline + FKs:
  - **`inbox` → INBOXBACK** — park each row into a scratch PROJECT, then re-enter `move … to list "Inbox"` (AppleScript) in reverse target order; front-inserts and RESTORES `start=0` (a same-list Inbox move is a no-op, so park-first is mandatory). Verify the scratch EMPTY, then trash it.
  - **`someday` → SOMEBACK** — the URL `anytime↔someday` `when=` bounce front-inserts BOTH loose someday to-dos AND area-less someday projects (per-type `todo.update`/`update-project` legs, reverse target, `start=2` preserved). `--strategy bounce` now routes here too; a mixed to-do+project set is refused (same-type only).
  - **`project` → PROJROOT** — park each unheaded child into a scratch PROJECT, then re-home `list-id=<P>` in forward target order (back-inserts at the project-root end). ONE flag-safe protocol for ALL rows — SUPERSEDES the narrower SOMEBNC-project someday-only bounce.
  - **`area` → AREABACK** — park each member out (a to-do into a scratch PROJECT, a project into a scratch AREA), then re-home to the area (`list-id=`/`area-id=<area>`) in reverse target order (front-inserts at the area's member min, area FK preserved). Covers direct area to-dos AND in-area projects (O14).
  - **`container-day` / `tomorrow` → the dated `day` bounce** — the pure-URL cross-date round-trip (ORD-6 DAYBNC) reaches every same-day child; no separate protocol needed.
  The three move-based fallbacks share the SIT6 move family's gate (`bounce-enabled`) and cap (`bounce-max-items`), are non-atomic with loud placed/remaining abort disclosure, never trash/delete a non-empty scratch (verify-empty teardown, AREADEL), and are undoable via a single inverse reorder (pre-ranks on the summary). **The `ok` result discloses which fallback ran via a `warnings` note** (e.g. `reordered via the non-experimental PROJROOT fallback because the native reorder is unavailable (allow-experimental is off)`) — a native placement is never silently mistaken for a degraded one. The #354 flag-aware routing composes: a flagged set with experimental off runs a pure-move fallback, so no `when=` leg ever touches a flagged row. (`src/write/reorder.ts` `resolveStrategy` + `runMoveFallback`/`runInboxPark`/`runProjectRoot`/`runAreaBack` + the SOMEBACK `someday` BounceKind.) Tests: `test/engine/write-reorder.test.ts` (per-fallback happy path + order exactness + flag/FK preservation + abort + cap + undo; routing experimental-on→native / off→fallback / sdef-canary-fail→fallback). Docs: `docs/reference/assumption-register.md` (ORD-16 → WIRED), `docs/capability-matrix.md`, o-suite **O25–O30**. No new `apiVersion`; no new error code (reuses `H-REORDER-SCOPE`).
- **FIX (breaking-safe, ALPHA) — `things todo move <ref> --inbox` no longer errors with a planner bug; the Inbox-return leg now pins the AppleScript transport.** The Inbox return compiles ONLY to AppleScript (`move to do id X to list "Inbox"`; the url-scheme `update` has no Inbox target), but both vectors advertise `todo.move` in the matrix and url-scheme wins the tier-0 registry tie — so the membership leg was routed to url-scheme, whose `compile` threw `todo.move cannot be compiled for vector url-scheme (planner bug)` and aborted the move. The `inbox` membership leg (`membershipLeg`, `src/write/move.ts`) now carries a leg-pinned `vector: "applescript"` fallback (an explicit caller `--vector` still wins, spread after it), so the planner selects the transport that can actually compile the return. The `loose`/`no-heading` shapes needed no pin — they compile only to url-scheme, which is already the default winner. (The guest e2e's "move back to Inbox" step caught this live; CI never boots the VM, so it went unnoticed since the reorder/move vocabulary sweep.) Tests: `test/engine/write-move.test.ts` (inbox-return transport pin — dispatches via applescript, de-schedules, url-scheme never reached). No new `apiVersion`; no new error code.
- **NEW (diagnostics) — `things doctor` emits a PASSIVE behavioral-drift notice when the installed Things version differs from the certified one, plus a `certified-app-version` config key.** The schema fingerprint cannot see BEHAVIORAL drift — an app update can move an insertion/preservation/refusal law with zero schema delta — so this closes the blind spot for the behavioral laws catalogued in the new law-level register (`docs/reference/assumption-register.md`). `doctor` reads the installed app version (from the Things bundle Info.plist, the same place it probes for the sdef) and compares it against `certified-app-version` (set when a baseline ships or via `things config set certified-app-version <X>`); on a mismatch it prints one non-blocking line (`behavioral: Things <X> installed; behavioral laws last certified against <Y> — run the drift suite to re-certify …`). Non-blocking by design: the fingerprint is unaffected and writes stay enabled — the notice only points at the drift runbook, mirroring the skill-version passive drift notice (#249). The `app:` doctor line now also shows the installed version (`installed (Things <X>)`); `doctor --json` gains `app.version` / `app.certifiedVersion` / `app.behavioralDrift`. When no certified version is set (the default), or the installed version is unreadable, there is no notice. (`src/config.ts`, `src/diagnose.ts`, `src/cli/commands/doctor.ts`, `src/cli/commands/writes.ts` config-set map.) Tests: `test/cli/doctor.test.ts` (drift flagged / matching / unset / unreadable, writes stay enabled), `test/unit/config.test.ts` (round-trip + provenance), `test/cli/config-get-cli.test.ts` (key count). Docs: `docs/lab/drift-runbook.md` step 4 (set it), `docs/reference/assumption-register.md`, `docs/reference/suite-audit.md`. No new `apiVersion`; no new error code.
- **NEW (safety) — `area delete` refuses a NON-EMPTY area unless `--allow-non-empty` is passed.** Deleting an area sends its contained to-dos AND its projects (with their children) to the Trash and permanently destroys the area row itself (A25B/AREADEL, SIT6 #349 — the stale P20 "projects orphan to no-area" claim is superseded; the current app trashes contained projects) — so a delete now pre-counts the area's live members (direct to-dos with `trashed = 0`, any status, AND projects carrying the area FK) and refuses fail-closed when any exist, naming the counts (e.g. `the area is not empty — it contains 3 projects and 12 to-dos`) and both remediations: empty the area first, or pass `--allow-non-empty` to delete it together with its contents. An empty area is unaffected (it still only requires `--dangerously-permanent`, unchanged). New hazard `H-AREA-NOT-EMPTY`, wired through the guard machinery ahead of `H-PERMANENT-DELETE`. CLI gains `things area delete <ref> --allow-non-empty`; MCP `delete` (kind area), `run_operation`, and `batch` gain the `allow_non_empty_area` param; the library/batch `WriteOptions` gain `allowNonEmptyArea`. Dry-run shows the refusal like any other guard. (`src/write/guards.ts`, `src/write/pre-state.ts` `areaMemberCounts`, `src/write/commands.ts`, `src/write/pipeline.ts`, `src/write/batch.ts`, `src/write/operations.ts`, `src/cli/commands/writes.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`.) Tests: `test/unit/write-guards.test.ts` (non-empty refusal with counts + both remediations; singular/plural + kind-selective copy; live-status counting with trashed excluded; `--allow-non-empty` clears the gate; empty area never trips it), `test/mcp/server.test.ts` (dry-run refusal + `allow_non_empty_area` threading). Docs: `docs/contract.md` hazard-id list, `docs/capability-matrix.md` area-delete note. No new `apiVersion`; no new error code.
- **NEW — FLAG-AWARE protocol routing: a Today/Evening-flagged reorder no longer de-Todays; it SWAPS to a flag-safe MOVE protocol (SIT6 HEADMOVE/LOOSEPARK/PROJPARK).** The three `things:///json`-collapsible index bounces (`heading` BOUNCE2-h, `anytime` ANYBNC, `projects` P8e) are `when=someday → when=anytime` round-trips: the someday leg NULLs `startDate` (`start 1→2`) and the anytime leg only flips `start` back to `1` (`startDate` stays NULL), so a flagged movee routed through them loses its Today/Evening flag silently (PROJSTAR de-star). Each has a lab-proven flag-safe twin on the SAME `index` axis (SIT6, `docs/lab/sit6-flagsafe-index-protocols.md`), so `runBounce` (`src/write/reorder.ts`) now routes the WHOLE touched set (the coBounce run) through the twin whenever ANY touched row carries the flag — read through the single-source marker `todayEveningFlagOf` (`src/write/pre-state.ts`, the same `today`/`evening` membership marker `computeReorderPre` uses, never re-derived):
  - **`heading` → HEADMOVE** — UNHEAD each touched child (clean; `index` + flag preserved), then RE-HEAD in forward target order (each back-inserts past the heading-bucket max). Non-atomic: a mid-fail leaves rows unheaded in the project root, disclosed.
  - **`anytime` → LOOSEPARK** — create a scratch PROJECT, PARK every touched row, then UNPARK in reverse target order (each front-inserts at the loose Anytime `index` min); verify the scratch EMPTY, then trash it (never trashes a non-empty scratch — AREADEL would Trash the parked rows).
  - **`projects` → PROJPARK** — create a scratch AREA, PARK each project, then DETACH in reverse target order (front-insert at the sidebar min); verify the scratch area EMPTY, then delete it (supplies `H-PERMANENT-DELETE` internally; NEVER deletes a non-empty area). This SUPERSEDES #351's de-Today REFUSAL — a flagged project movee now reorders, flag intact.
  Every leg is a URL/AppleScript MOVE (`list-id=`/`area-id=`) — NO `when=` leg and NO private reorder surface — so the flag + reminder + deadline + FKs all survive; an all-UNFLAGGED touched set keeps the cheaper bounce. Gated on `bounce-enabled` + capped by `bounce-max-items` (the closest existing multi-leg-quiet-compound gate; a cap-exceeded flagged set still refuses). Pre-ranks over the full touched set make the summary undoable via re-run. **`move.ts` `--in` routing:** the three Phase B `--in` de-Today refusals DIE — `--in <heading>` / `--in anytime` on flagged members now route to HEADMOVE/LOOSEPARK, and the dual-axis ambiguity logic re-enables offering those `--in` spellings (the heading / anytime / projects index axes are honest alternatives again). A loose Today set is now genuinely dual-axis (ambiguous → name `--in today` or `--in anytime`); a MIXED flagged+plain set sharing one container index auto-routes there (the single-bucket / anchor-bucket guards relax on a forced index axis). Tests: `test/engine/write-reorder.test.ts` (HEADMOVE/LOOSEPARK/PROJPARK routing, flag+reminder+deadline preservation, front/back-insert order, teardown verify-empty refusal, park/re-head abort, undo, cap-exceeded refusal, dry-run copy), `test/engine/write-move.test.ts` (the three `--in` swaps, dual-axis ambiguity + auto-route), `test/mcp/server.test.ts`. **Docs:** `docs/capability-matrix.md` (heading-anytime / loose-anytime / sidebar-projects flagged cells), `docs/reference/assumption-register.md` (ORD-15 → WIRED, WG-5 → swap), `docs/reference/suite-audit.md`, o-suite O22–O24. No new `apiVersion`; no new error code (reuses `H-REORDER-SCOPE`).
- **CHANGE (breaking, ALPHA) — `search --area <real-ref>` is now SUBTREE-INCLUSIVE, matching every other `--area` view.** `searchView`'s real-area SQL clause widens from direct-only (`t.area = ?`) to the effective-area shape `logbookView` uses (`t.area = X OR the containing project's area = X`), so a title/notes hit nested in one of the area's projects (heading-nested included) now surfaces under `search --area`. `--area loose` is unchanged (already effective-null, #346). The CLI + MCP `search --area` help copy is corrected off "direct members". (`src/read/views.ts` `searchView`; `src/cli/commands/reads.ts`; `src/mcp/server.ts`.) Tests: `test/unit/views.test.ts` (project-nested hit now included; loose pinned unchanged).
- **CHANGE (breaking, ALPHA) — the hidden refusing `area show --show-logged` redirect stub is DELETED; the logbook footer now carries a LIVE COUNT.** Per the ALPHA no-compat doctrine a muscle-memory redirect serves no one, so the flag is removed outright from `area show` and `areas <ref>` — `--show-logged` now errors as an unknown option like any flag that never existed (the #346 redirect message is gone from the codebase). The `area show` TTY card's logbook footer is upgraded from a bare pointer to a live count (e.g. `4,217 logged items — \`things logbook --area Health\``): a cheap subtree-inclusive COUNT over the area's logged rows using the same predicate + log-boundary `logbookView` applies, formatted with thousands grouping and omitted entirely at zero. TTY only — the JSON area-view shape gains nothing. (`src/cli/commands/area.ts`; `src/read/views.ts` `areaLoggedCount`; `src/client.ts` `BoundedAreaView.loggedCount`.) Tests: `test/cli/e2e.test.ts` (unknown-option on `area show` + `areas <ref>`; live count is subtree-inclusive; omitted at zero), `test/cli/render.test.ts` (thousands grouping / singular / zero-omit).
- **CHANGE (breaking, ALPHA) — MCP surface brought to parity with the post-#345/#346 CLI/library: `reorder` reshaped to the planner form, a dedicated `reorder_areas` tool, phase-2 result framing, and shadow-disclosure warnings parity.** (2026-07-31, `src/mcp/server.ts`, `test/mcp/server.test.ts`.)
  - **`reorder` is now the planner-form in-place verb**, mirroring `things reorder <refs...>`: inputs are `refs` (uuid/prefix/name), an optional position (`first`/`last`/`before`/`after`), and an optional `in` axis-disambiguator (`today`/`evening`/`anytime`/`someday`/`inbox`, or a project/area/heading ref). It calls the same library entry the CLI calls (`write.reorderTodos`), so the dual-axis fail-closed refusal and the de-Today refusal flow through unchanged. The old scope-driven shape (`scope`/`container`/`strategy` + the `areas` scope) is DELETED outright — no aliases.
  - **Sidebar-area reordering split into a dedicated `reorder_areas` tool** (mirrors `things area reorder`): `target` + exactly one of `before`/`after`/`first`/`last`, plus `dangerously_drive_gui`; the two-key GUI gate (`H-UI-DRIVE`) is unchanged.
  - **Framing phase-2 (the parked D5 item):** the mutation/move content block no longer carries the internal success discriminator. A single-op success now emits through `mutationWireData` (no `kind`/`result` in `data`) and a dry-run emits the bare plan — the same wire vocabulary the CLI uses (`ok`/`kind`/`error.code`+`detail`). Batch lines are FLATTENED to the JSONL wire shape (`outcome` is a plain string tag with the variant fields hoisted to line level), matching the CLI stream.
  - **Warnings parity:** the `loose` reserved-word shadow disclosure (#333/#346) and the area-view placement `notice` now reach MCP consumers via `meta.warnings` on `read_view`, `search`, and `get_area` — merged after the schema-drift warnings, exactly as the CLI surfaces them.
  - **Copy sweep:** removed the deleted scope/strategy vocabulary from the tool schema, and corrected a stale `list_view` reference in `get_area` to `read_view`. Banned-vocab + import-boundary tests stay green; the MCP SDK stays lazily imported.
- **CHANGE (breaking, ALPHA) — view-doctrine package: trashed rows render ONLY in the `trash` namespace, the area logbook is a query (not a view section), and `--area loose` selects the area-less rows everywhere.** Five ratified points plus a resolver-side sixth (2026-07-31).
  - **BLANKET RULE — trashed rows render only in `things trash`.** The `trashed` array/section is removed from EVERY other view: the `area-view` and `project-view` wire shapes lose their `trash` bucket, both card queries now filter `t.trashed = 0`, and the TTY `── Trashed ──` sections are gone from `area show` and `project show`. This makes the reader GUI-faithful — the app already filters trashed children out of the project view (oddities §6½ / PLOG1-a). A trashed child is reachable only via `things trash`. (`src/read/area-view.ts`, `src/read/project-view.ts`, `src/read/shape.ts` — with defensive `delete` guards so no untyped source can leak the key.)
  - **`things area show --show-logged` is REMOVED, and the area-view `logged`/`logbook` section with it.** An area is an unbounded accumulator — its logbook is a bounded QUERY, not a view section. (Superseded within this same cycle: the flag was first kept as a hidden redirect parse target and the card carried a bare `logbook: …` pointer — both since deleted per the entry above, so `--show-logged` now errors as an unknown option and the card footer carries a live logged count.) The MCP `get_area` result no longer carries logged items either. (`src/cli/commands/area.ts`, `src/read/area-view.ts`, `src/read/shape.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`.)
  - **`things project show --show-logged` and the project-view `logbook` section are UNCHANGED.** A project is a bounded object with a real done-state, so its in-context logbook stays. (`things projects <ref> --show-logged` inherits.)
  - **`loose` is now an `--area` FILTER value on every area-filtered read.** `--area loose` selects the area-less rows — effective-area-null (own area null AND, for a project child, no area-carrying project) — on `today`/`anytime`/`someday`/`upcoming` (post-filter via a `LOOSE_REF` sentinel in `itemInArea`) and `logbook`/`search` (a native inverted null-area SQL clause). `meta.filter.area` reads `{uuid: "loose", title: "Loose"}` (the sentinel round-trips as a ref); the reserved word ALWAYS wins over a real area named "Loose", disclosed under `meta.warnings` — the same reserved-word-wins + shadow semantics as the composite loose view (#333). `search --area loose` uses the same effective-null rule as its subtree peers (so a project-nested hit whose project has an area is excluded, and area-less inbox captures are included). (`src/read/area-filter.ts`, `src/read/views.ts` `logbookView`/`searchView`, `src/cli/commands/reads.ts`, `src/surface-copy.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`.)
  - **The logbook law is documented where consumers look.** `things logbook --help` now states it: the logbook is FLAT and CHRONOLOGICAL (stopDate DESC) — a logged project and its individually-logged children are peer rows, each its own line — and `--area` is SUBTREE-INCLUSIVE (direct members + rows inside the area's projects). This documents existing proven behavior (SIT5 LOGSWEEP parity), not a change. (`src/cli/commands/reads.ts`, `docs/contract.md` `loose` glossary entry extended with the filter surfaces.)
  - **RESOLVER (point 6) — write-target project NAME resolution matches OPEN projects only; UUID still reaches any status.** A write whose only name-match is a completed/logged project used to resolve silently and strand the moved/added OPEN child in the invisible-open-child state (absent from Anytime/Today/Inbox, reachable only inside the logged project — PLOG1). NAME resolution for project destinations (`resolveProject`, `--to-project`/`--project`) and write-targets (`resolveProjectWriteTarget`, the project verbs) is now `status = 0 AND trashed = 0` — deliberately broader than "logged" (a completed-in-place project strands children identically, one sweep later; the clock-free resolver cannot tell logged from completed). A UUID / partial-uuid resolves FIRST (status-blind, via `resolveTaskUuidPrefix` / a new name-tier-only `nameExtraWhere` on `resolveNamedRef`), so explicit-uuid intent still reaches a done project — where the existing `H-REOPEN-RESOLVED-PROJECT` acknowledgment fires. When a name matches ONLY non-open projects, the error uses the #344 dead-hint pattern (no candidate): the destination not-found gains `1 completed project matches this name — target it by uuid if intended`, and candidates on write paths never include non-open projects. `project.reopen` (its whole purpose is a non-open target) opts back in to completed-by-name via `includeNonOpen`, exactly as `project.restore` opts in to trashed-by-name. Areas cannot be completed and headings carry no status axis, so neither needs the rule. (`src/read/queries.ts`, `src/write/pre-state.ts`, `src/write/guards.ts`, `src/write/pipeline.ts`.)
  - **Tests:** area/project-view schema pins lose `trash` (area also loses `logbook`), project keeps `logbook` (`test/unit/shape.test.ts`, `test/unit/stage.test.ts`, `test/unit/views.test.ts`, `test/unit/truncation.test.ts`, `test/cli/render.test.ts`); `--show-logged` refusal + no-Logged-section card (`test/cli/e2e.test.ts`); `--area loose` membership on logbook/search (unit, `test/unit/pseudo-area.test.ts`) and anytime/logbook + shadow disclosure (CLI, `test/cli/view-area-filter.test.ts`); the completed-by-name destination refusal + H-REOPEN-by-uuid (`test/unit/write-guards.test.ts`) and reopen-by-name-still-works + non-reopen-verb refusal (`test/engine/write-p19.test.ts`). No new `apiVersion`; no new error code.
- **FIX (breaking, ALPHA) — error `detail.candidates` now emit ONE fixed, slim shape; the pool is live-scoped.** The "did you mean" / disambiguation lists on not-found + ambiguous resolutions used to emit the FULL UNSHAPED internal entity (raw `untrashedLeafActionsCount`, `todaySection`, non-presence-keyed nulls, whole `notes` bodies), bypassing the R-series wire shaping and bloating error payloads. They now carry the ONE fixed `CandidateRef` disambiguation shape.
  - **Fixed shape (`CandidateRef`, `src/read/shape.ts`), flag-invariant.** `uuid` + `title` + `type` (`"to-do"|"project"|"heading"|"area"|"tag"`) always; a container hint `area`/`project` as a TITLE string only when set; the R10/R12 `stage`/`when` (reusing the wire's own `stageOf`/`whenOf` — never re-derived) only for a to-do/project candidate whose row carries the lifecycle fields. NOTHING else — no counts, notes, dates, or null-stuffed keys. A trashed/logged candidate reads it off `stage` (`"trash"`/`"logbook"`), no boolean. `--full`/`--all` NEVER widen it (an error payload is the most determinism-critical surface). ONE projector `candidateRef()` is the single source every emit site flows through: the not-found/ambiguous resolvers (`resolveTaskUuidPrefix`, `resolveUuidOrThrow`, `resolveProjectWriteTarget`, `resolveHeadingUuid`) and the did-you-mean fallback (`candidatesJson`), across CLI `--json` and MCP. The old `RefCandidate` `{uuid, title, context?}` is deleted (`context` → presence-keyed `area`/`project`).
  - **Capped at 8.** Every candidate list caps at `CANDIDATE_CAP` (8, was 10 for did-you-mean); when more matched the `message` states the total ("matches 12 projects … 4 more").
  - **Live-scoped pool (domain scoping).** Candidates are LIVE rows only by default — a trashed/logged row never appears as a "did you mean" suggestion (`resolveProjectWriteTarget` name resolution is now `trashed = 0`; the did-you-mean `liteTitleSearch` was already open+untrashed). The trash-domain `project restore` widens ITS pool to trashed rows. When a name matches ZERO live rows but DEAD rows exist, the error `message` gains an honest tail ("1 trashed item matches this name — see `things trash`" / logbook equivalent) and `candidates` stays `[]` — no dangling-ref invitation.
  - **Tests:** `test/unit/candidate-ref.test.ts` (schema pin: allowed keys only, presence-keying, per-kind, stage/when derivation, trash/logbook stage); wire pins + cap + live-scope + dead-row hint in `test/cli/e2e.test.ts` and `test/cli/write-cli.test.ts`; MCP shape parity in `test/mcp/server.test.ts`. **Docs:** `docs/contract.md` (fixed candidate shape + domain scoping), `src/contracts.ts` JSDoc. No new `apiVersion`; no new error code.
- **BREAKING — one kind-neutral in-place `reorder` verb replaces `todo reorder` AND the raw `reorder --scope`; axis disambiguation via `--in`.** Phase B of the move/reorder vocabulary (`docs/design/heading-demotion-and-move.md` §4/§8). ALPHA-CONTRACT clean break — the old spellings are DELETED outright, no aliases.
  - **`things reorder <refs…> [--first|--last|--before <ref>|--after <ref>] [--in <target>]`** is now the single in-place rearrange verb. It carries the promoted `todo reorder` semantics (refs sharing one container+bucket, planner-inferred scope, argument order = landing order, bare = block at the earliest slot, partial-selection friendly) and accepts the project rows the Today/Evening/day lists intermix with to-dos (O12/DAYBNC). The old subcommand `things todo reorder` and the old raw `things reorder <uuids…> --scope <s>` (with `--project`/`--area`/`--strategy`) are REMOVED, along with the CLI-side scope plumbing. `things project move-heading` (heading rows) and `things area reorder` (sidebar) are unchanged separate verbs.
  - **NEW `--in <target>` — dual-axis disambiguation (RATIFIED: fail-closed).** A Today/Evening member has BOTH a `todayIndex` slot in the view AND an `index` slot in its container (a project/area/heading child, or the loose Anytime bucket). When a set (and its anchor) is coherent on both axes and no `--in` is given, the reorder is REFUSED with a message naming both readings and their exact `--in` spellings (e.g. `--in today` vs `--in "Health"`) — this replaces the old silent always-Today resolution. `--in` accepts `today | evening | anytime | someday | inbox`, or a project/area/heading ref (uuid or unique title); `loose` is refused (a read view, not a bucket). Forcing the container index axis on a Today member genuinely compiles that container's index protocol — but ONLY where it is Today-flag-safe (the NATIVE project/area re-rank writes only `index`, sit3 EVEPROJ / DAYORD-b); a bounce-only index axis (a heading child, the loose Anytime bucket) is REFUSED with a de-Today-hazard explanation rather than silently stripping the flag (the `when=` bounce legs overwrite it). A wrong `--in` (naming a container/axis the rows are not in) is a clear error naming where they actually are. (`resolveReorderAxis`, `src/write/move.ts`; `ReorderRequest.in`.)
  - **Mixed-stage `todo move`/`project move` position semantics (RATIFIED).** A selection spanning stage sub-buckets (anytime + scheduled + someday + templates) with `--before`/`--after` is REFUSED unless every movee shares the anchor's sub-bucket (remediation: split the call or drop the anchor) — enforced pre-move by `preflightAnchor`. `--first`/`--last` on a mixed-stage selection now apply PER SUB-BUCKET: each stage-group lands at the top/bottom of ITS matching bucket in the destination, grouped off the post-move truth, with the result note stating every group's placement outcome (`placePerBucket`, `src/write/move.ts`). Repeating-template placement is always app-default and disclosed (§9e).
  - **Surface sweep.** `src/cli/help.ts` (the `move` topic teaches the new verb + `--in`; index entry), `src/mcp/server.ts` (the scope-driven `reorder` tool is unchanged and unrenamed — it never used the `todo reorder` vocabulary), `docs/capability-matrix.md` (verb spellings), CHANGELOG. All copy per `docs/design/surface-copy.md`; banned-vocab + help-contract tests pass.
  - **Tests:** dual-axis ambiguity refusal (names both `--in` spellings), `--in today` vs `--in <project>` compiling different axes on the same rows, `--in` validation errors, unambiguous sets needing no `--in` (per scope family), de-Today-hazard refusal for bounce-only index containers, `--in <project>` re-ranking Today members Today-flag-safely, mixed-stage anchor refusal + per-sub-bucket `--first`, and deleted-verb absence (no `todo reorder`, no `reorder --scope`) in `test/engine/write-move.test.ts` + `test/cli/help-contract.test.ts`. No new `apiVersion`; no new error code.
- **NEW — area-DIRECT scheduled project rows are dated-`day`-bounce members (SIT5 AREAPROJDAY).** Grounded in SIT5 (`docs/lab/sit5-areaproj-convinst-logsweep.md`): the `update-project?when=` legs preserve the area FK through the round-trip and re-enter at the day's global `todayIndex` min, and a scrambled bounce lands the exact order twice with the area'd project mid-target. The `day`-scope member predicate (`computeReorderPre`, `src/write/pre-state.ts`) relaxes from `(type=0 OR (type=1 AND area IS NULL))` to `type IN (0,1)`; the planner classifier (`reorderTargetOf`, `src/write/move.ts`) routes an area'd future-day project row to the `day` bounce (formerly app-default); the `sharedFutureDay` group predicate and the per-item live member guard (`src/write/reorder.ts`) drop their area'd-project exclusions. Tests: an area-direct project row compiles per-type `update-project` legs and its area FK is asserted preserved (`test/engine/write-reorder.test.ts`); `docs/capability-matrix.md` day cell.
- **NEW — arbitrary-day sorting (incl. projects) via the SIT4 dated `day` bounce; evening accepts project movees; the scratch-park day compounds are deleted.** Grounded in SIT4 (`docs/lab/sit4-daybounce-eveord-axdrag4.md`). Supersedes the ORDFIN1/ORDFIN2 day-compound entries below (all unreleased).
  - **NEW `day` scope — the DATED BOUNCE (DAYBNC).** A whole future day-group on an ARBITRARY day — loose, direct-area, headed, or cross-container to-dos AND area-less scheduled PROJECT rows, any mix — now sorts into an exact cross-container order by a cross-DATE `when=` round-trip: each row is re-whened out to the neighbour day D+1 and back to D (`update?when=` for a to-do, `update-project?when=` for a project — the first MIXED-kind bounce, per-type leg op), which FRONT-inserts it at the day's GLOBAL `todayIndex` minimum across containers, so a reverse-target pass lands the requested order. Non-destructive: `startDate`/`start`/`reminderTime`/`deadline`/heading FK/project FK all preserved (bare `when=<date>` carries the reminder, §2e/R21 — unlike the evening bounce). Bounce-only (no native surface reaches an arbitrary day), sequential URL legs (the `things:///json` when= reindex is unproven for dated placement, §9i), gated by `bounce-enabled` + `bounce-max-items` — NOT `allow-experimental`. A repeating template movee/anchor is refused (a dated when= leg crashes it, §9e/§1); area-direct project rows are members too (SIT5 AREAPROJDAY — see the dedicated entry above). **A scheduled PROJECT row is now placeable on ANY future day, not just tomorrow** (the loose-scheduled-project-row cell flips to WIRED).
  - **`evening` scope accepts PROJECT movees (EVEORD).** A project's `when=today`→`when=evening` re-entry front-inserts at the shared evening-group `todayIndex` min (below to-dos AND projects), so the `evening` bounce now dispatches a per-type leg op and `things reorder`/`project move` sort mixed evening sets; `when=evening` clears a project's reminder too (§9n/R07).
  - **SUPERSEDED + DELETED.** The `day` bounce serves the entire population of the ORDFIN1 scratch-park compounds plus area-less project rows, with no scratch project and no experimental gate, so `loose-day`, `area-day`, `upcoming-day`, and the `heading-day` unhead→re-head round-trip are all REMOVED (`runDayCompound` / `runHeadingDay` and their origin-capture/restore machinery deleted from `src/write/reorder.ts`). `container-day` (single unheaded-project native re-rank) and `tomorrow` (one-call `list "Tomorrow"` sort) are KEPT for their cheaper degenerate cases. Planner routing (`src/write/move.ts`): a future day-group → `container-day` (single unheaded project) | `tomorrow` (day == tomorrow) | `day` (everything else).
  - **`stranded` field REMOVED.** With the bounce there are no strandees — an unrequested same-day project row is just an unrequested MEMBER of the day-group (co-bounced + disclosed as `touched` only when an anchor spans it). The `MutationResult.stranded` field, its `strandSuffix` disclosure, and the `upcoming-day` PRJMIX plumbing are deleted.
  - **FIX — axis-aware anchor validity (reorder).** The `--before`/`--after` anchor preflight compared STRUCTURAL containers even for the GLOBAL todayIndex scopes (today, evening, tomorrow, `day`), refusing a valid cross-container anchor with a self-contradictory identical-label message (e.g. a single arrived Today member `--after` a Today member in a different container refused with "the anchor … is in the today list, not … the today list"). Anchor validity is now AXIS-aware: for a global-axis scope the anchor need only share the movees' bucket/day-group (the reorder TARGET key, `containerKey`), not the structural container — the GUI permits exactly that drag; index-axis scopes (anytime/someday per container, inbox) keep the structural-container rule. The span guard and the comparator/describer read the SAME key, so an identical-label refusal is structurally impossible.
  - **Tests:** the dated bounce (exact scrambled cross-container order incl. project rows + headed child, collateral preserved, per-type leg dispatch, template + area-project refusal, gate/cap, undo, dry-run) and the evening project bounce in `test/engine/write-reorder.test.ts`; planner routing (loose/area/headed/cross-container/mixed → `day`, single project → `container-day`, tomorrow → `tomorrow`, anchored `--after` a project row, cross-day anchor refusal) and axis-aware anchor validity (cross-container Today/Evening/`day` anchored reorders compile; index-axis anchor refusals keep DIFFERING labels) in `test/engine/write-move.test.ts`; the `day` undo in `test/unit/undo-plan.test.ts`. **Docs:** `docs/capability-matrix.md` (day cells → WIRED via the dated bounce incl. project rows; evening → project movees wired; scratch-park references superseded), `src/cli/help.ts` placement honesty. No new `apiVersion`; no new error code.
- **FIX — arrived dated rows reorder as Today members, not day-group members (reorder planner).** The dated branches of the reorder planner's classifier (`reorderTargetOf`, `src/write/move.ts`) routed a dated container child by its date-GROUP without first checking arrived-ness, so an ARRIVED row (`startDate <= today` — a Today-view member) misrouted into a future day-group compound: a direct-area to-do dated in the past landed in the `area-day` compound, a project child dated today landed in `container-day` (and likewise `heading-day`). The GUI renders arrived/today-dated rows in the TODAY view; the Upcoming day-groups hold STRICTLY FUTURE dates only. This is the same class of bug #325 fixed in `scheduleBucket`. Now a dated row that has arrived routes DATE-FIRST to the shipped cross-container `today` scope (or `evening` when the §9n evening flag is live — `startBucket=1` while `startDate == today`), exactly like an undated Today member, for every container (loose, project-, heading-, area-child); the arrived split reuses `scheduleBucket`'s single-source verdict (no re-derive). Strictly-future dates keep their day-group routing unchanged.
- **FIX — day-group refusal copy now names the ISO date.** Every future day-group container label in the reorder refusal / disclosure copy (`describeScope`, `src/write/move.ts`) omitted its date, so a cross-day anchor refusal read self-contradictorily — anchoring a `2026-08-05` loose row after a `2026-08-07` loose row refused with "the anchor … is in the loose future-day group, not the movees' container (the loose future-day group)", the same label on both sides. A day-group's identity IS its day, so the label now carries the date: "the loose 2026-08-05 day-group" vs "the loose 2026-08-07 day-group" (and analogously for `area-day`, `container-day`, `heading-day`, and the cross-container `upcoming-day`). The packed day is threaded into the day-group `ScopeTarget` and decoded at render.
  - **Tests:** two regression blocks in `test/engine/write-move.test.ts` — arrived direct-area / project-child / headed-child / evening-flagged routing to today/evening (never a day-group) with strictly-future routing pinned unchanged per scope; and a cross-day loose anchor refusal asserting both ISO dates render coherently. No new `apiVersion`, no new error code.
- **FIX — stale reminders are no longer reported or resurrected (§9n / SIT3 REMSTALE).** A time-of-day reminder renders in the Things GUI ONLY while its row's `startDate` is the current day or future; once `startDate` goes strictly past, the app hides the reminder bell at the presentation layer while leaving the `reminderTime` byte in the DB forever (never cleared by arrival or the Today banner OK). Our reader keyed on the raw byte and over-reported: it emitted `reminder` on arrived/stale rows the GUI shows no bell for.
  - **READ (GUI-faithful gating).** A new single-source predicate `reminderIsLive(startDate, today)` (`src/read/stage.ts`, beside `entityWhen`/`whenIsProvisional`) decides whether a stored reminder still renders. It is computed once at materialize into a presence-keyed `reminderLive` marker (`mappers.commonFields`, under the response clock — exactly like the Today/Evening markers), and the read-shaping emit boundary (`shape.ts`) now drops the `reminder` key when the marker is absent, on every surface (detail, list rows, JSON, search). A **today**- or **future**-dated reminder is kept (a future reminder is a live upcoming reminder); a **strictly-past** one is omitted (presence-keyed, as always); a reminder with no `startDate` keeps the byte (defensive — the app never produces that shape). The DB bytes are never touched.
  - **TTY consumes the SAME marker** — the list-row bell (`◷`, `render.ts`) and the detail-card reminder (`whenValue`, `glyphs.ts`) render only when `reminderLive` is set, so the human chip can never disagree with the emitted `reminder`.
  - **WRITE (no resurrection).** A bare `when=` re-schedule auto-preserves the current reminder (R07) — but now consults the same `reminderIsLive` against the target's CURRENT `startDate` at pre-read: a STALE reminder byte is NOT carried into the re-schedule (previously `things project update <ref> --when evening` on a weeks-past-dated row with an 18:00 byte compiled `when=evening@18:00`, re-arming a reminder the user believed gone; it now compiles a bare `when=evening`). A LIVE reminder is preserved exactly as before. (`effectiveReminder`, `src/write/commands.ts`; the response-clock date rides `PreState.todayIso`.)
  - **Tests:** the `reminderIsLive` boundary matrix (`stage.test.ts`); the marker computed from raw bytes for past/today/future/null + projects (`mappers.test.ts`); shape-time drop/keep on list + detail (`shape.test.ts`); the stale row emits no `reminder` and no TTY chip while a live row keeps both (`project-today-pips.test.ts`); the write-side stale-vs-live auto-preserve (`write-cli.test.ts`). No new `apiVersion`, no new error code; the machine `HH:MM` format is unchanged for live reminders.
- **NEW — TTY provisional pip: the yellow `•` "new item" marker in `things today`.** The wire already emits the presence-keyed `provisional: true` on every unmaterialized Today member (BANNER1 law — a Today entrant the app has not yet acknowledged, `start != active OR startDate IS NULL`). The human `things today` view now mirrors the GUI's yellow "new item" pip: a provisional row renders a yellow `•` (U+2022) in the after-the-box mark slot the section headers free up (the ★/⏾ is header-deduped in this view). It is per-row (it distinguishes rows within a section) and — per the GUI — renders ONLY in the Today view (Today and This Evening sections), never in anytime or any other view (there the ★/⏾ marks alone apply).
  - **SINGLE-SOURCE:** a new `entityProvisional` helper (`src/read/stage.ts`) wraps the one `whenIsProvisional` law that the wire emit boundary (`shape.ts`) now also consumes, so the rendered pip can never disagree with the emitted `provisional`. Wire emission is byte-unchanged.
  - **Legend + help:** `things legend` gains a `•` entry (behavior-only wording); the `things today --help` epilog carries a one-line mention.
  - **Tests:** the pip renders in the today view for a provisional row and is absent from the anytime view for the same row; the legend coverage guard now requires `•` (`test/cli/render.test.ts`). No new `apiVersion`, no new error code.
- **NEW — projects carry Today/This-Evening state exactly like to-dos (probe O12); TTY when-state is single-source; 12-hour reminder chips.** Projects can be assigned to Today/This Evening (their rows live in the Today bucket), but the TTY omitted the ★/⏾ pip on some project-row renderers and the detail card re-derived when-state from raw bytes.
  - **WIRE (audit — already correct, now pinned):** every surface that emits a project row (projects list, area-view project section, anytime/someday sections, search, changes, today view) already derives and emits `when: "today"|"evening"` for projects under the SAME rules as to-dos — `mappers.commonFields` stamps the Today/Evening markers for BOTH kinds and `shapeItem`/`whenOf` derive `when` for both. Regression tests pin the per-surface emission (`test/cli/project-today-pips.test.ts`).
  - **TTY — the ★/⏾ pip on project rows.** `renderProjectsSidebar` (`things projects`) now places the Today ★ / This-Evening ⏾ mark on project rows, matching the anytime/someday/sections renderers (which already did). The `today` view keeps its dedup rule (the section header carries the mark; rows don't).
  - **TTY when-state is SINGLE-SOURCE (R12 fix).** A new library helper `entityWhen` (`src/read/stage.ts`, composing `deriveStage` → `deriveWhen`) is the ONE time-axis derivation the wire emit boundary and the TTY renderers share. `whenValue` (detail/card header) and `todayMark` (row pips) now consume it instead of re-deriving Today/Evening from `startBucket`/`todaySection` — those DIVERGE from the marker on stale bytes (a `startBucket=1` row with a past `startDate` is Today, not This Evening; its evening membership expired). The detail card had rendered `⏾ This Evening` while the wire correctly emitted `when:"today"`; both now agree. `deriveWhen` itself is unchanged. (Evening staleness is handled by the marker — an arrived `startBucket=1` row derives `when:"today"`; the sibling reminder-staleness gate landed in the REMSTALE entry above.)
  - **TTY reminder chips render 12-hour** (`◷ 6:00pm`, `9:00am`, `12:00pm` noon, `12:00am` midnight) via `formatReminderTime`; the wire keeps the machine 24h `HH:MM` exactly. No locale detection (fixed am/pm).
  - **REORDER intermix (O12) — a blocked shipped path, now FIXED.** `things todo reorder <todo> <project>` on a mixed loose Today-bucket set previously refused "homogeneous kinds" at the `runInPlaceReorder` gate — even though the whole downstream pipeline supports it (`computeReorderPre` keeps `mixedTypes=false` for the `today` scope and compiles a `type IN (0,1)` wire list; the `H-REORDER-SCOPE` guard passes). The gate now allows a project movee WHEN every movee shares the Today bucket (routing to the native `today` scope), and keeps the refusal for every other bucket. Evening intermix for projects is untouched (unprobed — EVEPROJ queued). Regression + narrowness tests in `test/engine/write-move.test.ts`; `docs/capability-matrix.md` Today row updated.
- **NEW — the `loose` pseudo-area: address the NULL area (area-less items) as an area on READ surfaces.** A reserved, case-insensitive ref `loose` renders the composite null-area view mirroring the GUI sidebar's leading loose block: area-less projects first, then loose direct to-dos; `--show-later` adds the Upcoming (date-ordered) and Someday sections (the "Later Projects" sidebar view). Reuses the area-view pipeline with the area filter swapped to `IS NULL` (`src/read/area-view.ts`, `src/read/pseudo-area.ts`); the Inbox (`start=0`) and project-nested rows are excluded, exactly as a real area's `t.area = ?` excludes them for free. All `area show` flags apply unchanged (`--show-later`/`--show-logged`/`--overdue`/`--all`/`--full`/tag filters/`--json`).
  - **Envelope:** `kind: "area-view"` with `area: null` (the null-group precedent from the sectioned views).
  - **Router parity:** `things areas loose`, the loose `things show loose` (and bare `things loose`), and `things projects --area loose` all resolve to the loose group through ref resolution — no separate `--loose` list flag exists. `projects --area loose` lists only the area-less projects.
  - **RESERVED-WORD semantics:** `loose` ALWAYS resolves to the pseudo-area, case-insensitively, even when a real area is named "Loose". When one shadows, a resolution disclosure rides `meta.warnings` (and stderr on a TTY) naming the real area's uuid so it stays targetable — `things area show <uuid>` reaches the real one.
  - **READ-ONLY:** `things area open loose` / `things open loose` and every area WRITE verb refuse the reserved word by name (`the loose pseudo-area is a derived view …`), not a generic no-such-area error. The write vocabulary is untouched: `todo move --loose` keeps its detach meaning; `project move --no-area` detaches a project; `--to-area loose` is refused.
  - **Tests:** resolution + shadow disclosure + case-insensitivity, view content (active/later buckets match the null-area filter; inbox/nested/real-area rows excluded), open/write refusals, and router parity (areas/show/projects `--area`) in `test/cli/loose-pseudo-area.test.ts` + `test/unit/pseudo-area.test.ts`. **Docs:** `docs/contract.md` glossary (the reserved ref). No new `apiVersion`, no new error code.
- **NEW — ORDFIN2 ordering round 2: `upcoming-day` project-strand disclosure, the `tomorrow` one-call fast path, and area-reorder duplicate-title support.** Three ratified changes grounded in the ORDFIN2 evidence (`docs/lab/ordfin2-followups.md`).
  - **`upcoming-day` project-row refusal → DISCLOSED-STRAND (PRJMIX).** PR #331 refused the cross-container day-sort fail-closed when a scheduled PROJECT row (type=1) shared the target day. ORDFIN2 PRJMIX proved the strand law is deterministic: an untouched same-day project row keeps its `todayIndex` byte-identical and the park-sorted block always lands ABOVE it (the block's fresh values are computed below the GLOBAL day-group minimum across all containers). So the refusal is replaced with a disclosure — the to-do sort proceeds, and the stranded project rows are surfaced in the ok result's new `stranded` field (`{uuid, title}[]`) plus a placement-honesty `warnings` note (block at the top of the day; N project rows remain below in prior order). A REQUESTED project movee stays refused for an arbitrary day (unsortable there); a repeating TEMPLATE movee stays refused (§9e). `src/write/reorder.ts` (`runDayCompound`), `MutationResult.stranded` (`src/write/pipeline.ts`), disclosed through `move.ts` `strandSuffix`.
  - **NEW `tomorrow` scope — the one-call `list "Tomorrow"` day-sort (TOMORROWLIST).** ORDFIN2 proved `_private_experimental_ reorder to dos in list "Tomorrow" with ids "…"` is a CLEAN one-call cross-container day-sort for exactly one day out: it re-ranks the whole next-day group on `todayIndex` in the sent order, ACCEPTS a scheduled PROJECT row inline (O12 analog) and re-ranks it in position, and preserves `startDate`/`start`/`startBucket`/FKs (no §9g re-date, unlike `list "Upcoming"`). The planner (`src/write/move.ts` `reorderTargetOf`/`repositionInPlace`) now compiles a tomorrow day-group to this ONE native call instead of the scratch-park compound — regardless of container mix — and project-row movees are ALLOWED on this path only (every other day scope refuses them). `container-day`/`heading-day` (single-container project/heading children) keep their existing paths. Gated like `container-day` (`allow-experimental` + sdef canary); full day-group pinned in the wire list; pre-ranks captured for undo (the inverse re-runs the native call in prior order — no scratch project). `ReorderScope` gains `tomorrow` (planner-selected only, never a CLI flag); no new `OperationKind`, no new error code.
  - **`area.reorder` duplicate-title support — WIRED, LAB-CERT PENDING.** The sidebar drag driver addressed rows by their visible name and refused a duplicate area title up front. ORDFIN2 AXDRAG3 pinned the sidebar sort law (`TMArea."index" ASC, uuid ASC`, ties broken uuid-lexicographically), so a duplicate-titled uuid-target is now disambiguated positionally: the driver (`src/write/vectors/ui-drag.ts`) predicts the target's (and a `--before`/`--after` anchor's) sidebar row ordinal by that law — the Nth same-titled row, recomputed from live state each hop so it stays correct as the dragged area crosses a same-titled sibling — grabs THAT row, and the existing post-gesture DB assert verifies the INTENDED uuid's `index` moved; on a wrong-area mismatch it SELF-INVERTS (re-drags the displaced area back to its captured slot) and aborts with a positional message. The up-front duplicate-title refusal is removed for a uuid-targeted ref (`src/write/commands.ts`); a duplicate NAME ref stays refused (intent ambiguity — the resolver's candidates error); a sanity cap (title shared by >8 areas) refuses rather than loop. **Not yet certified on-device — an AXDRAG4 VM sitting will drive it before trust.**
  - **GUARD — the reserved `loose` pseudo-area ref refuses in `area reorder`.** A self-contained check in the area-reorder target resolution refuses `loose` (case-insensitive) as the target or an anchor with a specific message (the loose pseudo-area is a derived view — no sidebar row, cannot be reordered), never a generic no-such-area error. Independent of the concurrent `loose` pseudo-ref read-view work.
  - **Tests:** the strand disclosure (`stranded` + warning, sort still runs) and the native `tomorrow` scope (re-rank + inline project row + experimental gate) in `test/engine/write-reorder.test.ts`; planner routing (tomorrow → one native call/no scratch, later day → scratch-park compound, project movee accepted for tomorrow / refused otherwise) in `test/engine/write-move.test.ts`; `findAreaRowNth`/`areaTitleRank` helpers in `test/unit/ui-drag.test.ts`; the `loose` guard, uuid-target-with-shared-title acceptance, duplicate-NAME ambiguity, and sanity cap in `test/unit/area-reorder-guards.test.ts`. **Docs:** `docs/capability-matrix.md` (upcoming-day → disclosed-strand; scheduled-day + loose-project-row cells → the `tomorrow` one-call exception; Sidebar: areas → duplicate-title WIRED, cert pending).
- **NEW — ORDFIN1 ordering endgame wired: three more ordering cells → GUARANTEED (headed evening, `area-day`, `upcoming-day`).** The certified ORDFIN1 laws (`docs/lab/ordfin1-ordering-endgame.md`) close the last reachable ordering gaps; the planner (`src/write/move.ts` `reorderTargetOf`/`repositionInPlace`) routes them to wired protocols instead of app-default.
  - **Headed EVENING child → the shipped `evening` bounce (Arm 2b).** The today↔evening bounce preserves a headed evening child's heading FK byte-identical (`startBucket` round-trips 1→0→1, today `startDate` kept), so the planner now routes a headed evening movee to the already-wired `evening` scope unchanged — no new machinery, and there was never a "GUI-ambiguous display axis" (the earlier app-default justification was wrong). Rides `bounce-enabled` and inherits the R07 reminder-loss caveat exactly like the project/area-child evening slot. (Corrects the HEADSUB1 "headed evening stays app-default" entry below, same release.) Corollary (Arm 2c): the native PROJECT-specifier reorder is evening-SAFE (keeps `startBucket=1`); de-evening is specific to the `list "Today"` today-scope reorder (O03).
  - **NEW `area-day` scope — a direct-area's dated children sharing one future day (Arm 3).** The AREA reorder specifier is destructive (§9f de-schedules dated members), so this rides the scratch-project park-sort-restore compound: create a scratch PROJECT, park each dated child into it (URL `list-id`, schedule preserved), run the shipped `container-day` reorder, restore each to the area (`list-id=<area>` — area FK round-trips, `startDate`/`start=2`/reminder/deadline preserved), then trash the scratch. The destructive AREA specifier is NEVER used.
  - **NEW `upcoming-day` scope — a whole CROSS-CONTAINER future day-group (Arm 4).** Any mix of loose, project-child, headed-child, and area-child to-dos sharing one future Upcoming day. Each member's ORIGIN (loose / project / project+heading / area) is captured BEFORE parking; the whole group is parked into ONE scratch project, re-ranked by a single `container-day` reorder, then each member is restored to its captured origin FK — restore-leg order is irrelevant (the reorder alone sets `todayIndex`). Refuses fail-closed on an unparkable member: a repeating TEMPLATE movee (§9e) and any scheduled PROJECT row on the day (UPCORD1: not parkable), including a same-day project SIBLING.
  - **Refactor (ALPHA-CONTRACT clean).** `runLooseDay` is generalized into the origin-aware `runDayCompound` (`src/write/reorder.ts`); `loose-day`/`area-day` are the uniform-origin degenerate cases (every member homes loose / to the area), `upcoming-day` the general one. No compatibility shims.
  - **Planner routing.** Refs sharing one future day now route: same single project → `container-day`; all headed under one heading → `heading-day`; all loose → `loose-day`; all direct children of one area → `area-day`; ANY cross-container mix → `upcoming-day` (the new `repositionInPlace` branch that no longer refuses a cross-container group when it shares one future day). A `--first/--last/--before/--after` anchor into these scopes co-parks the whole day-group; a `--before`/`--after` anchor must be a member of the same day-group (positions, never migrates).
  - **Gates/caps/undo.** All three compounds are gated by `allow-experimental` (the `container-day` reorder leg) and fail BEFORE creating a scratch project when the gate is off; capped by `bounce-max-items` (counts every co-parked day-group member); non-atomic with a `placed`/`remaining` abort payload naming the scratch project for recovery; the scratch uuid + its trashed state disclosed in `warnings`. Undo pre-captures the day-group's `todayIndex` order and re-runs the compound in prior order (`todayIndex` ties → irreversible, the `loose-day`/area-reorder precedent); `area-day` preserves its area container across the round-trip.
  - **Tests:** the `area-day`/`upcoming-day` compounds end-to-end (leg sequence, exact order, FK restoration per origin, template + scheduled-project refusals, cap, gate, dry-run, undo round-trip) in `test/engine/write-reorder.test.ts`; planner routing per class (headed evening → `evening`, same-area day → `area-day`, cross-container day → `upcoming-day`, different-day cross-container → span refusal, anchor-outside-group refusal, cross-container end-to-end FK restoration) in `test/engine/write-move.test.ts`; `planUndo` invertibility + container preservation + all-tied irreversibility in `test/unit/undo-plan.test.ts`. **Docs:** `docs/capability-matrix.md` (Ordering: the three cells → WIRED/GUARANTEED, APP-DEFAULT shrunk to loose scheduled PROJECT row + templates), `src/cli/help.ts` (PLACEMENT HONESTY refreshed, 40-line budget kept). `ReorderScope` gains `area-day` + `upcoming-day` (planner-selected only, never CLI flags); no new `OperationKind`, no new error code.
- **Docs/help — refreshed two stale ordering claims in CLI help to match the current guaranteed-placement picture.** After UPCORD1/HEADSUB1/HEADSUB2 promoted heading someday/scheduled sub-buckets, loose future days, and container-child evening to GUARANTEED, two `--help` strings still described the old picture. The `things help placement` "PLACEMENT HONESTY" paragraph (`src/cli/help.ts`) now lists the full guaranteed set and shrinks its APP-DEFAULT list to the four remaining best-effort cells (headed evening sub-bucket, direct-area scheduled-day child, loose scheduled project row, templates). The `reorder` command description (`src/cli/commands/writes.ts`) no longer claims "project children under headings cannot be reordered" — instead it notes the raw `--scope` surface does not reach a heading's children and points to `things todo reorder` for that, keeping the existing pointer to `things project move-heading` for the headings themselves. Copy-only; no behavior change.
- **NEW — heading sub-bucket ordering + child-evening: three HEADSUB1 promotions (3 ordering cells → GUARANTEED).** The certified HEADSUB1 laws (`docs/lab/headsub1-heading-subbuckets.md`) close the remaining within-heading and container-child-evening ordering gaps. The planner (`src/write/move.ts` `reorderTargetOf`) now routes them to wired protocols instead of app-default.
  - **`heading-someday` — within-heading SOMEDAY order (Arm B-someday / Arm C + HEADSUB2 Q1).** The unhead → re-head round-trip. A re-head of a row ALREADY under the target heading is a same-heading NO-OP on `index` (HEADSUB2 Q1(b), oddity §9l — the move-to-heading back-insert only fires when the heading FK actually changes), so the block is UNHEADED first (`update?list-id=<project>`, clean — `heading`→NULL, `index`/`start=2` preserved), then RE-HEADED in FORWARD target order so each now-loose row genuinely BACK-INSERTS at the heading someday-bucket end (§9h renumber) — the block lands the target. Two `todo.move` legs per item (unhead + re-head), a terminal order verify, `--first/--last/--before/--after` supported, unnamed siblings co-unheaded + co-re-headed and disclosed as `touched`. NO json collapse (list-id moves, not a `when=` reindex); needs neither the experimental surface nor the bounce — pure URL move legs; a mid-protocol failure leaves items UNHEADED in the project root and fails loudly. `runHeadingSomeday` in `src/write/reorder.ts`. (HEADSUB1 first shipped the inert direct-re-head-only compile; HEADSUB2 Q1 caught it and this entry ships the corrected round-trip — the broken form never reached a release.)
  - **`heading-day` — within-heading same-day SCHEDULED order (Arm C2).** The native container-day reorder RIPS a headed child's heading FK on the `todayIndex` axis too (§9k / Arm A — O06 is axis-agnostic), so the direct re-rank is forbidden. Delivered by the unhead → `container-day` reorder → re-head round-trip: unhead the WHOLE same-day headed sub-bucket (clean — `heading`→NULL, date/`todayIndex`/`start` preserved), re-rank the now-unheaded rows via the shipped `container-day` scope against the real project (date-preserving), then re-head each (`todayIndex` preserved). Gated like `container-day` (`allow-experimental`); a mid-protocol failure leaves items UNHEADED in the project root and fails loudly with `placed`/`remaining` detail. `runHeadingDay` in `src/write/reorder.ts`.
  - **NON-NEGOTIABLE §9k rail:** a headed row is NEVER fed to the native container-day reorder — the sub-bucket is unheaded FIRST, and `computeReorderPre(container-day)` independently filters `heading IS NULL`, a second structural guard. A regression test asserts the planner routes a headed scheduled child to `heading-day` (never `container-day`) and that `computeReorderPre(container-day)` can never select a headed row.
  - **Child-EVENING → the shipped `evening` bounce (Arm D + HEADSUB2 Q2).** A project OR area child flagged this-evening front-inserts identically to a loose evening item (project/area FK + `startBucket=1` + today `startDate` preserved), so the child-refusal/app-default routing is removed — the planner now routes a container-child evening movee to the `evening` scope unchanged. HEADSUB1 Arm D proved this for a PROJECT child; HEADSUB2 Q2 CONFIRMS it directly for an AREA-direct child (area FK byte-identical, same front-insert direction as the loose control), so the area routing is proven, not inferred. Inherits the R07 reminder-loss caveat the loose evening scope already carries. A HEADED evening child stays app-default (evening IS representable under a heading, but the display ordering axis is GUI-ambiguous headlessly, Arm B).
  - **Gates/caps/undo.** `heading-someday` is ungated (URL move legs); `heading-day`'s container-day leg is gated by `allow-experimental`; child-evening rides `bounce-enabled` like the loose evening scope (disabled → app-default, never a destructive fallback). `bounce-max-items` counts touched items incl. co-unheaded/co-re-headed sub-bucket siblings. Undo pre-captures the sub-bucket's prior ranks and re-runs the protocol in prior order (`heading-someday` unheads → re-heads in prior order; `heading-day` re-runs the round-trip — `todayIndex` ties → irreversible, the `loose-day`/area-reorder precedent).
  - **Tests:** the two scope protocols end-to-end (exact order, state preservation, anchor/subset compile + `touched` disclosure, caps, gate, mixed-date + template refusals, mid-protocol abort honesty, dry-run, undo round-trip) in `test/engine/write-reorder.test.ts`; the §9k never-constructible assertion; planner routing per class (headed someday → `heading-someday`, headed scheduled-day → `heading-day` not `container-day`, headed evening → app-default, project/area child evening → `evening`, bounce-disabled degrade) in `test/engine/write-move.test.ts`. **Docs:** `docs/capability-matrix.md` (Ordering: the three cells → GUARANTEED with HEADSUB1/HEADSUB2 citations, APP-DEFAULT list pruned to headed-evening + direct-area day + loose scheduled project row + templates); `docs/lab/headsub1-heading-subbuckets.md` §HEADSUB2 + `lab/scripts/research-headsub2.sh` (the confirmations: Q1 caught the broken `heading-someday` compile → corrected to the unhead→re-head round-trip, Q2 confirmed the area-child evening front-insert); oddity §9l (same-heading re-head is index-inert); `docs/reference/README.md` + `novel-paths.md` #42 updated. `ReorderScope` gains `heading-someday` + `heading-day` (planner-selected only, never CLI flags); no new `OperationKind`, no new error code.
- **Fixed — the move/reorder planner classified every FUTURE-scheduled to-do as a Someday member and misrouted it (potential de-schedule averted by defense-in-depth).** `scheduleBucket` (`src/write/move.ts`), the display-bucket classifier the Phase-A planner (`todo move`/`todo reorder`) keys on, tested `start === 2 → someday` BEFORE consulting `startDate`. But the app's ONLY representation of a future-scheduled item is `start=2` (someday) + a future `startDate` — a plain active `start=1` is always undated (UPC1/BANNER1; live prod scan 2026-07-30: 4/4 future-scheduled to-dos are `start=2`, ZERO `start=1`+future). So a REAL scheduled to-do read as a Someday-bucket row and routed to the Someday-family reorder protocols. **Fix:** reorder the classifier DATE-FIRST — `inbox → dated (arrived → today/evening; future → scheduled:<date>) → then start=2 = an UNDATED someday only → else anytime` — mirroring the read layer's settled `deriveStage` precedence (`src/read/stage.ts`, unchanged; verified consistent). An arrived `start=2` (someday-scheduled, `startDate <= today`) buckets today/evening, matching `deriveStage`'s Today+Anytime derivation.
  - **Was any de-schedule reachable? No — but the margin was thinner than it should have been.** Traced end-to-end for `things todo move <scheduled> --first` (a bare-position in-place reorder): a LOOSE or DIRECT-AREA dated movee routed to the `someday`/`area-someday` scope, but the reorder member-enumeration (`computeReorderPre`) requires `startDate IS NULL` for every someday-family scope, so the `H-REORDER-SCOPE` guard (`src/write/guards.ts`) rejected the dated movee as a non-member and BLOCKED before any mutation — surfacing a confusing false refusal ("not an eligible member of the Someday list"), never a mutation. A PROJECT-CHILD dated movee routed to the native `project` scope (index re-rank), which preserved the date (`somedayProjectChildren` also requires `startDate IS NULL`, so the destructive `project-someday` bounce fallback never engaged) — a harmless wrong-field no-op on the todayIndex-ordered day position. The DESTRUCTIVE path — the `area-someday`/`project-someday` when= bounce whose `when=anytime` away leg CLEARS a `startDate` — was therefore never entered. **No user data was lost;** this fix removes the misroute at the source rather than relying solely on the downstream member-guard.
  - **Sibling audit.** `scheduleBucket` was the ONLY start-before-date classifier in the write layer. The read-view classifiers (`src/read/project-view.ts`, `src/read/area-view.ts`) and the Today-marker mapper (`src/model/mappers.ts`) already conjoin `startDate IS NULL` / test the date first; the reorder wire-protocol member queries and verify predicates (`src/write/pre-state.ts`, `src/write/reorder.ts`) correctly guard someday scopes with `start=2 AND startDate IS NULL`. None needed changes.
  - **Tests.** New planner regression block in `test/engine/write-move.test.ts` with the app-true `start=2` representation: a loose future-day movee routes to `loose-day` (not the someday protocol), a project-child future-day movee to `container-day` (date-preserving), an undated `start=2` still to `someday`, an arrived `start=2` to today/evening, the single-bucket rule separates a dated `start=2` row from an undated Someday sibling, and a planner-level assertion that a dated movee NEVER routes to the de-scheduling `area-someday` bounce. **Fixture realism:** the future-scheduled engine fixtures that seeded the app-unreal `start=1`+future form (`test/engine/write-move.test.ts`, `test/engine/write-reorder.test.ts`) now seed `start=2`+future, the representation the app actually writes.
- **NEW — `loose-day` reorder scope: order STANDALONE loose to-dos within a future Upcoming day (UPCORD1 park-sort-unpark).** The last ordering gap.
