Loose (no project/area/heading) to-dos sharing ONE future day had no clean surface (`list "Upcoming"` re-dates them destructively §9g; date-shaped `list` specifiers don't exist; the area scratch de-schedules dated members §9f). The certified protocol (`docs/lab/upcord1-loose-day-order.md` Arm B) wires them GUARANTEED: create a scratch PROJECT (unique synthetic title), PARK each day-group member into it (URL `list-id` — preserves `startDate`/`start`/`todayIndex`/reminder/deadline), run the shipped `container-day` reorder against the scratch project (native `todayIndex` re-rank, date-preserving), UNPARK each (empty `list-id` → loose again), then TRASH the scratch project (delete-to-trash, NEVER permanent). Orchestrated in `src/write/reorder.ts` (`runLooseDay`) as a compound txn (one undoable summary; legs excluded from undo).
  - **Planner-selected** by `todo reorder`/`todo move` when the movees are loose + share one future day (`src/write/move.ts` `reorderTargetOf`); anchor vocabulary (`--first/--last/--before/--after`) compiles onto it per the Phase A conventions. The WHOLE day-group is parked as one unit (the `container-day` leg only orders the scratch project's own children, so an un-parked member would keep a stale `todayIndex`) — every un-named day sibling is a co-parked sibling, disclosed as `touched`.
  - **Rails:** NEVER routed through an area scratch (§9f); movees must share ONE future date (a mixed-date request is refused — a single reorder cannot span days); repeating TEMPLATE movees are refused naming the §9e law (the reorder SKIPS them); loose scheduled PROJECT rows stay app-default (no surface). Gated by `allow-experimental` exactly like `container-day` (the reorder leg is that native command) — the protocol fails BEFORE creating a scratch project when the gate is off. Capped by `bounce-max-items` (counts every co-parked day-group member).
  - **Non-atomic, disclosed:** a mid-protocol failure leaves items PARKED in the scratch project and fails loudly with `placed`/`remaining` detail naming the scratch uuid (recovery is one manual drag). On success the result discloses the scratch uuid + its trashed state (`warnings`). **Undo** pre-captures the day-group's full `todayIndex` order and re-runs the protocol with the prior order (all-tied/unreadable prior order → irreversible, the area-reorder precedent).
  - **Tests:** planner selection + each refusal (mixed dates, template movee, non-loose routes to `container-day`), the protocol leg sequence, a simulator end-to-end order assertion, the undo round-trip, cap + gate behavior, and the error-detail recovery payload (`test/engine/write-reorder.test.ts`, `test/engine/write-move.test.ts`, `test/unit/undo-plan.test.ts`). **Docs:** `docs/capability-matrix.md` (Ordering: loose future day → GUARANTEED, refused sub-cases noted), `docs/lab/probe-backlog.md` (DAYORD loose gap closed). `ReorderScope` gains `loose-day` (operation catalog); no new `OperationKind`, no new error code.
- **⚠️ BREAKING — R13: provisional Today members + GUI-faithful someday/inbox/anytime membership for deadline-pulled rows (BANNER1/BANNER1b); ALPHA-CONTRACT clean break, `apiVersion` STAYS `1`.** Implements the GUI-verified banner laws (`docs/lab/banner1-research.md`, oddities §8s). Reshaped at the read emit boundary + the flat-view SQL (`src/read/views.ts`, `src/read/predicates.ts`, `src/read/stage.ts`, `src/read/shape.ts`); in-memory entities + human render unchanged. Old shapes DELETED, no shims.
  - **NEW — `provisional: true`, the banner-as-data marker.** A presence-keyed marker on every Today member the GUI pips / counts in the "You have N new to-dos" banner: a Today member NOT yet materialized — `start != active OR startDate IS NULL` (BANNER1 law L1). Banner `N` = the count of provisional Today members, exactly. Derived from the SAME today/evening markers + fields the `stage`/`when` axes use (never re-derived, so it can never disagree with the star). Emitted on EVERY tier, NEVER dropped (the banner is not a section). Absent on non-Today rows, on materialized members (`start=1 ∧ startDate set` — a user-placed `when=today`, or a post-OK deadline-pull), and on templates (never Today members). **Read-only:** the app clears it by MUTATING the row (`start:=1`, `startDate:=today`) when the user acknowledges the banner — a GUI-only side effect our read cannot perform, and one that silently de-inboxes/de-somedays the row (§8s). Watchers beware.
  - **⚠️ BREAKING — deadline-pulled rows re-file to stage `anytime`; the today view's sections now DROP `stage`.** Law L-A (BANNER1b): a due/overdue unsuppressed deadline pulls an undated Inbox/Someday row OUT of its origin list and INTO Today + Anytime, even while its raw `start` still reads 0/2. `deriveStage` gains step 2½ — a Today-marked undated row (a pull) derives its DESTINATION `anytime`, not its origin. **Consequence:** EVERY Today member now derives `anytime` (arrived-dated via the arrived-startDate branch, pulled via step 2½), so the `today` view's own sections are provably stage-PURE and `stage` is DROPPED there (was kept as "mixed" pre-R13; a strict property test verifies purity). `provisional` is NOT dropped there — the banner is not a section.
  - **Fix — GUI-faithful flat someday/inbox/anytime membership.** The flat `someday` and `inbox` views now EXCLUDE deadline-pulled undated rows (the GUI removes them from those lists at pull time), and the `anytime` view now INCLUDES them (Anytime ⊇ Today's to-dos). One shared predicate `DEADLINE_PULLED` (`src/read/predicates.ts`) — the EXACT Today deadline arm, reused verbatim by `todayView`/`inboxView`/`somedayView`/`anytimeView` so the four memberships can never drift. A SUPPRESSED deadline (dismissed nag, `deadlineSuppressionDate ≥ deadline`) is not a pull: the row stays in someday/inbox, out of anytime/today (UPC1 suppression parity). The `anytime` catalogue stays stage-PURE (pulled rows derive `anytime`), so it still drops `stage`. Card someday buckets follow automatically (rebucket-by-`deriveStage`): a pulled child lands in the card `anytime` bucket.
  - **Ripple — `inbox --overdue` / `someday --overdue`.** An UNSUPPRESSED overdue-deadline undated capture is now a deadline pull → removed from the Inbox/Someday list, so it no longer appears under `--overdue` there; only a SUPPRESSED overdue row (a genuine list member) survives the compose. The pull removal AND-composes with `--area` / `--tag` filters (narrows, never lifts).
  - **Tests:** view membership both directions + filter composition (`test/unit/views.test.ts`); the provisional matrix (five BANNER1b entrant classes → `provisional`, user-placed/materialized → not, suppressed → not a Today member, template → not) + banner-count reconstruction + a STRICT today-section stage-purity property test (`test/unit/stage.test.ts`); today-section `stage` drop (`test/unit/shape.test.ts`); MCP spot-check (`test/mcp/server.test.ts`); `--overdue` compose fixes (`test/cli/e2e.test.ts`). **Docs:** `contract.md` glossary (`provisional` added, `stage`/`when` today-section note), `design/contracts.md` (R13 section + item tables + view shapes), `up-next.md`.
- **⚠️ BREAKING — R12: `when`, the derived TIME-AXIS position (replaces `startDate` + the `today`/`evening` markers in compact); ALPHA-CONTRACT clean break, `apiVersion` STAYS `1`.** One derived, presence-keyed field `when` ∈ `"today" | "evening" | <future ISO date>` now carries an item's time position (`src/read/stage.ts` `deriveWhen`, beside `deriveStage`; reshaped at the read emit boundary in `src/read/shape.ts`). In-memory entities + human render unchanged. Old fields DELETED outright, no shims.
  - **Doctrine:** `stage` enumerates the sidebar BUCKETS an item lives in; `when` enumerates its TIME POSITIONS. `when` is DERIVED from the SAME `today`/`evening` markers the Today view uses (never re-derived), so `when` ∈ {today, evening} can never disagree with Today-view membership; a FUTURE date is a strictly-future scheduled row (`startDate > today`) or a repeating TEMPLATE's projected next occurrence; absent means unscheduled + not in Today (an unprojected/paused template and every logged/trashed row carry none). Someday is deliberately NOT a `when` value (it is a bucket → `stage`); the write-side `--when someday` input vocabulary is unchanged (input mirrors the app's When control; reads decompose the resulting state onto the two axes).
  - **Deleted from the wire:** the `today` and `evening` marker keys (every tier — `when` carries the fact); the raw `startDate` in COMPACT (a list needs the position; FULL/DETAIL KEEP `startDate` beside `when` as the substrate — `startDate` = what is stored, `when` = where it sits); and a template's `repeating.nextOccurrence` (moved to `when` — same fact, one word; an unadorned template now emits `repeating: {}`, which survives omit-empty).
  - **Dropping (R6-consistent):** `when` is dropped inside the `today` view's own sections (the section key states today/evening) and inside a card/heading `upcoming` date-group for a member whose `when` equals the group's date; KEPT everywhere else it is present — the flat `upcoming`/`anytime`/`inbox`/`someday` catalogues, search, and changes. A deadline-pulled inbox/someday row reads `when: "today"` there while `stage` still reads inbox/someday (the informative cross-axis cases).
  - **Tests:** exhaustive `deriveWhen` matrix + a property test (`when` ∈ {today, evening} ⟺ Today-view membership) in `test/unit/stage.test.ts`; drop-table + full-tier-keeps-`startDate` in `test/unit/shape.test.ts`; template-wire updates in `test/cli/e2e.test.ts` + `test/mcp/server.test.ts`. **Docs:** `contract.md` glossary (`when`/`startDate` added, `today`/`evening` deleted, `repeating` updated), `design/contracts.md` (R12 section + item tables + view shapes + reshape (5) + omit-empty).
- **⚠️ BREAKING — R10.2: arrived-dated items derive stage `anytime`; Upcoming is STRICTLY FUTURE (UPC1); ALPHA-CONTRACT clean break, `apiVersion` STAYS `1`.** Corrects the R10 `stage` derivation, which encoded "any `startDate` → `upcoming`" on a Today⊆Upcoming SUPERSET assumption the GUI corpus contradicts. GUI Upcoming membership is STRICTLY FUTURE — `COALESCE(startDate, deadline) > today` (UPC1, GUI-verified, `docs/lab/upcoming-research.md`). Reshaped at the read emit boundary (`src/read/stage.ts` + `src/read/shape.ts`); in-memory entities + human render unchanged. Old derivation DELETED, no shims.
  - **`deriveStage` splits the `startDate` branch on ARRIVAL.** A STRICTLY-FUTURE `startDate` (`> today`) → `upcoming`; an ARRIVED `startDate` (`<= today`) → `anytime` — an arrived-When-dated open item is a Today (star) + Anytime member, NOT Upcoming; its Today membership rides the existing `today` marker. Covers active-arrived AND arrived someday-scheduled (`start=2`, `startDate <= today`) rows. Repeating TEMPLATES are UNCHANGED — still `upcoming`. The arrived/future split reads the presence-keyed `today` marker (its scheduled arm is exactly `startDate <= today`, computed with the response clock), so `stage` can never disagree with Today membership.
  - **`stage` is now DROPPED on the flat `anytime` catalogue (reverses R10.1's keep).** With arrived rows deriving `anytime`, every Anytime-view member (ANYTIME_SELF — undated-active, arrived-active, arrived someday-scheduled) derives `anytime`, and repeating templates are excluded from the view (`NOT_TEMPLATE`) — so the catalogue is provably stage-PURE and `stage` is redundant there. `upcoming` REMAINS stage-MIXED (it carries deadline-forecast `anytime`/`someday` rows) and KEEPS `stage`.
  - **Card / heading rebucketing.** Falls out of rebucket-by-`deriveStage`: an arrived (past/today) dated child rebuckets into the `anytime` bucket, NOT the `upcoming` date-groups — which now hold only FUTURE dates + the trailing `{date: null, items}` resting-template group.
  - **The UPC1 §8d banner-materialized case** (acknowledging the "new to-dos" banner mutates the item to `start=1`, `startDate := deadline = today`) now derives `stage: "anytime"` + `today: true` (was `upcoming`) — an arrived When-date is Anytime + Today.
  - (Touched: `src/read/stage.ts` (`deriveStage` + `StageInput.today`), `src/read/shape.ts` (`stageOf`, `ANYTIME_SECTION_DROP`); tests `test/unit/stage.test.ts` (derivation matrix arrived/overdue→anytime, future→upcoming, §8d pin; strict property test with `anytime` moved to the stage-pure set; card arrived-child rebucket), `test/unit/shape.test.ts` (anytime section drops stage); docs `contract.md` (glossary `stage`), `design/contracts.md` (R10 precedence + dropping), `design/heading-demotion-and-move.md` (R10 reshape note), `up-next.md`.)
- **Dev tooling — `npm run explore --public`: token-gated LAN access.** The explorer dev server gains a `--public` flag that binds `0.0.0.0` (instead of `127.0.0.1`) so a phone/tablet on the same network can reach it, gated behind a random per-launch token (`crypto.randomBytes(16)`). Startup prints every non-internal IPv4 LAN URL with the `?key=` appended; a valid `?key=` GET of `/` plants an `HttpOnly; SameSite=Lax` `exploreKey` cookie ("open once on your phone — a cookie keeps you signed in"), and `/run` accepts the cookie, the `?key=` param, or an `x-explore-key` header (timing-safe compared). Unauthorized requests get a `403` naming the fix; the served banner reads "LAN access · token-gated". Default (no-flag) behavior is unchanged: `127.0.0.1`, no auth. `--no-open` / `--port` compose. (Touched: `scripts/explore/server.ts`; tests `test/unit/explore.test.ts`; docs `README.md`.)
- **Fixed — `latestInstance` now EXCLUDES trashed instances (SL2 parity).** The template-detail `latestInstance` derivation (`src/read/queries.ts` `latestInstanceUuid`) gained `AND trashed = 0`: the GUI "Show Latest" never selects a trashed occurrence — it skips a trashed newest instance to the newest UNTRASHED one, and a template whose instances are all trashed derives `null` (docs/lab/sl2-trash-dynamics.md, law L1; the SL1 query shipped without this filter). The *status* filter stays absent — a COMPLETED newest-spawned instance is still the latest (SL1 D1); only `trashed` is filtered. (Touched: `src/read/queries.ts`; tests `test/cli/e2e.test.ts` (two new R11 pins: trashed-max excluded, all-trashed → no key); docs `contract.md` (`latestInstance` glossary), `docs/lab/sl2-trash-dynamics.md` (new), `docs/reference/README.md` (SL2 row), `docs/things-app-oddities.md` (§8q/§8r).)
- **⚠️ BREAKING — R11: `repeating` is TEMPLATE-only rule state, `instanceOf` on instances, `latestInstance` per SL1; ALPHA-CONTRACT clean break, `apiVersion` STAYS `1`.** The wire's repeating shape is reshaped at the read emit boundary shared by CLI `--json` and the MCP read tools (`src/read/shape.ts`, before omit-empty); the in-memory library entities and the human render are unchanged (minimal blast radius — the R9/R10 precedent). Old shape DELETED, no shims.
  - **`repeating` becomes TEMPLATE-only rule state.** The `isTemplate` / `isInstance` discriminators are DELETED from the wire everywhere — **key PRESENCE carries the fact**. A repeating TEMPLATE carries `repeating: {nextOccurrence, paused?, deadlined?}` (the rule facts; plus `rule` on a `detail` read) — presence of `repeating` MEANS the row is the template. The inner false booleans (`paused`/`deadlined`) are default-pruned (presence-keyed); `nextOccurrence` stays an EXPLICIT `null` when there is no projected date (paused / after-completion — the `area: null` section precedent, since absence would be ambiguous). The nested object is not a pruned entity, so omit-empty preserves its `nextOccurrence: null`.
  - **Instances get a flat presence-keyed `instanceOf: <templateUuid>`** and NO `repeating` object — presence of `instanceOf` MEANS instance (it replaces the former `repeating.isInstance` + `repeating.templateUuid`). A plain to-do/project carries NEITHER `repeating` nor `instanceOf`; a row carries at most one.
  - **`latestInstance: <uuid>` on template DETAIL only.** A `detail`/`show` read of a repeating TEMPLATE (to-do or project) carries the `latestInstance` uuid nested inside `repeating` (R11.1) — the GUI "Show Latest" pick, derived by the SL1 GUI-verified law: `SELECT uuid FROM TMTask WHERE rt1_repeatingTemplate = :t ORDER BY creationDate DESC LIMIT 1` — `max(creationDate)` among the template's instances, NO status filter (a completed newest-spawned instance is still the latest — the SL1 D1 case), blind to `startDate`/`userModificationDate`/`stopDate` (docs/lab/sl1-show-latest.md, `src/read/queries.ts` `latestInstanceUuid`). Omitted when the template has no instances; never on list/card rows (token economy).
  - Internal call sites that read `repeating.isTemplate`/`isInstance` (stage derivation, the write-layer hazards/verify, the human render/glyphs) keep working — the in-memory `RepeatingInfo` KEEPS its flags; only the WIRE loses them. Batch dotted refs (`$x.instance`/`$x.replaced`) are input-side and untouched.
  - (Touched: `src/read/shape.ts` (`reshapeRepeatingWire`), `src/read/detail.ts`, `src/read/queries.ts` (`latestInstanceUuid`), `src/model/entities.ts` (`RepeatingInfo.latestInstance`, internal-only); tests `test/unit/shape.test.ts`, `test/cli/e2e.test.ts`, `test/mcp/server.test.ts`; docs `contract.md` (glossary: `repeating`/`instanceOf`/`latestInstance` added), `design/contracts.md` (reshape (5) + omit-empty tables + Booleans note), `up-next.md`.)
- **⚠️ BREAKING — R11.1: `latestInstance` nests INSIDE `repeating`.** The template-detail `latestInstance` uuid moves from a flat top-level wire key to a member of the `repeating` object — `repeating: {nextOccurrence, paused?, deadlined?, rule?, latestInstance?}` — making `repeating` the complete series object: rule config, the forward pointer `nextOccurrence` and the backward pointer `latestInstance` (symmetric derived series state), plus state flags, under one presence-keyed key that MEANS template. Still detail-only, still omitted when no untrashed instances. (Touched: `src/read/shape.ts` (`reshapeRepeatingWire` — hoist deleted), `src/read/detail.ts`, `src/model/entities.ts` (comment); tests `test/unit/shape.test.ts`, `test/cli/e2e.test.ts`, `test/mcp/server.test.ts`; docs `contract.md`, `design/contracts.md`.)
- **R10.1 — stage-mixed catalogue correction + `todaySection` retirement (three ratified follow-ups to R10; ALPHA-CONTRACT clean break, `apiVersion` STAYS `1`).** Corrects two over-aggressive drops from R10 and retires a now-redundant field, at the same read-shaping emit boundary (`src/read/shape.ts`). The in-memory library entities and the human render are unchanged.
  - **Fixed — `stage` is KEPT on the flat `upcoming` catalogue.** R10 dropped `stage` there as "bucket-implied", but the Upcoming view is genuinely stage-MIXED: it carries deadline-forecast stage-`anytime`/`someday` rows — so dropping `stage` deleted NON-redundant information, violating the R6 rule (drop only what the enclosing node provably states). `stage` is now dropped ONLY in the provably stage-pure flat views (`inbox`, `someday`, `logbook`, `trash`) and the stage-named card sub-buckets; it is KEPT on `upcoming`, `today`, `search`, `changes`, `projects`, `areas`, and detail. (This entry originally also kept `stage` on the flat `anytime` catalogue, on the belief it carried arrived-dated stage-`upcoming` rows — CORRECTED by R10.2 above: an arrived `startDate` derives `anytime`, so the Anytime catalogue is stage-PURE and drops `stage`.) The property test was tightened from the weakened `∈ {anytime, upcoming}` membership form to STRICT equality — every emitted `stage` equals `deriveStage`, and `stage` is present exactly where the table says.
  - **⚠️ BREAKING — `todaySection` is retired from the wire.** It duplicated the presence-keyed `today`/`evening` markers (`todaySection: "evening"` ⇔ `evening: true`) — one-word doctrine. It is deleted at the read emit boundary (all tiers, incl. `detail`); it REMAINS an internal entity field, still read by the human render (evening styling) and the write-verify schedule delta (minimal blast radius). The Today view's own section grouping is unaffected — only the per-item wire field dies.
  - **Docs (no behavior change) — area-view block-count honesty note.** `meta.truncation.blocks[kind=area]` counts the internal `active` set ("direct to-dos hidden"), while the wire now splits those rows across the `anytime` + arrived-`upcoming` buckets — so the count is not per-wire-bucket. A one-line note is added in `docs/design/contracts.md`; reconciling block counts with the stage buckets is queued in `docs/up-next.md`.
  - (Touched: `src/read/shape.ts`, `src/model/entities.ts` (`todaySection` doc → internal-only), `src/mcp/server.ts` (read-semantics copy); tests `test/unit/stage.test.ts` (strict property test), `test/unit/shape.test.ts`; docs `contract.md` (glossary `stage`/`todaySection`), `design/contracts.md` (field lists + block-count note), `up-next.md`.)
- **⚠️ BREAKING — R10 stage taxonomy: ONE derived `stage` word replaces `start`/`logged`/`trashed`; today/evening markers; view buckets renamed to match; ALPHA-CONTRACT clean break, `apiVersion` STAYS `1`.** The read-payload JSON is reshaped at the emit boundary shared by CLI `--json` and the MCP read tools (`src/read/shape.ts`, before omit-empty); the in-memory library entities and the human render are unchanged. Old shapes DELETED, no shims.
  - **`stage` — the one lifecycle word.** Every to-do and project carries `stage` ∈ `inbox | upcoming | anytime | someday | logbook | trash`, derived by ONE pure function (`src/read/stage.ts` `deriveStage`) that REUSES the list views' own membership rules so an item's `stage` can never disagree with the bucket a view puts it in. `start`, `logged`, and `trashed` are DELETED from every item, all tiers incl. `detail`. Precedence: trashed → `trash` (wins over everything, incl. logged); logged (past the logbook boundary) → `logbook`; else `inbox`; else `upcoming` (a repeating template OR a STRICTLY-FUTURE `startDate` — Upcoming is strictly future, `> today`; CORRECTED by R10.2 above — R10 shipped this as "any `startDate` — Upcoming is a SUPERSET of Today"); else `anytime` (undated-active OR an arrived `startDate <= today`); else `someday`. A completed/canceled row not yet past the logbook boundary keeps its live stage.
  - **`today` / `evening` markers.** Presence-keyed `today: true` / `evening: true` (evening implies today) — a SEPARATE axis from `stage`, derived with the Today view's own two-arm predicate (a scheduled `startDate <= today`, OR an undated due/overdue deadline that is not suppressed, per the F-DL/UPC1 GUI-verified corner, oddities §8d–8e). So an undated deadline-today item reads `stage: "anytime"` + `today: true`; a someday item with a due unsuppressed deadline reads `stage: "someday"` + `today: true`; the suppressed form drops the marker.
  - **View cards + heading groups renamed/reshaped to the six words.** `active`→`anytime`; the `scheduled` date-groups and the separate `repeating` array MERGE into `upcoming` (`[{date, items}]`, date ASC — a dated row under its `startDate`, a template under its `nextOccurrence` — with a trailing `{date: null, items}` group for date-less resting templates); `someday` stays; `logged`→`logbook`; `trashed`→`trash`. Heading groups are `{heading, anytime, upcoming, someday}`; the area card keeps its `projects` list. (R10 shipped this with an arrived (past/today) dated child riding `upcoming` — CORRECTED by R10.2 above: an arrived child rebuckets into `anytime`, and `upcoming` holds only future dates + the `{date: null}` template group.)
  - **Bucket/section-implied dropping.** `stage` is dropped where the enclosing view/section states it (the stage-scoped flat catalogues + the card sub-buckets) and kept on the mixed/derived surfaces (search, changes, `today`, projects/areas listings, the card node, detail). The `today`/`evening` markers are dropped inside the `today` view's own sections and kept elsewhere.
  - (Touched: `src/read/shape.ts`, `src/read/stage.ts` (new), `src/model/entities.ts` + `src/model/mappers.ts` (today/evening markers, `deadlineSuppressionDate`); tests `test/unit/stage.test.ts` (new — derivation matrix, GUI-verified marker corners, property-style view↔stage consistency), `test/unit/shape.test.ts`, `test/cli/e2e.test.ts`, `test/mcp/server.test.ts`, `test/unit/mappers.test.ts`; docs `contract.md` (glossary: `stage`/`today`/`evening` added, `start` deleted), `design/contracts.md` (R10 + view shapes + omit-empty), `design/heading-demotion-and-move.md` §9, `up-next.md`.)
- **⚠️ BREAKING — R9 payload refinements: string tags, project `todos` counts, `hasNotes`, one `project` key, compact `heading` drop; ALPHA-CONTRACT clean break, `apiVersion` STAYS `1`.** Five coordinated wire/emission-layer changes at the read shaping boundary shared by CLI `--json` and the MCP read tools (`src/read/shape.ts`, before omit-empty); the in-memory library entities and the human render are unchanged (minimal blast radius — the checklist-reshape pattern). One consumer (the maintainer) → the old shapes are DELETED, no shims.
  - **String tags (UNIVERSAL — both tiers + `detail`).** `tags` and `inheritedTags` fold from `{title}` objects to a plain array of tag NAMES (`["errand","home"]`) on to-dos, projects, AND areas. Tag uuids were never on the wire; the title is the identity. The per-tag object wrapper is DELETED.
  - **Project `todos` counts (UNIVERSAL — both tiers + `detail`).** A project's flat `untrashedLeafActionsCount` / `openUntrashedLeafActionsCount` are REMOVED from the wire and fold into one presence-keyed `todos: {open, total}` (same shape as `checklist`; absent when `total` is 0). The source is the app-maintained materialized leaf-action columns — leaf to-do children only (headings and checklist items excluded by construction; trashed excluded).
  - **Notes preview REMOVED.** The compact `notes` first-line preview and the `notesTruncated` marker are DELETED from the wire. Compact drops the full `notes` string for a presence-keyed `hasNotes: true` (absent = no notes); full/detail carry the complete `notes` string (omit-empty). `--full` remains the sanctioned way to get notes in a list context.
  - **One `project` key.** An item whose membership routes through a heading emits its owning project under the single key `project`; the former `headingProject` is merged in and DELETED from the wire (the two could never coexist or disagree). R6 project-view dropping is unchanged.
  - **Compact `heading` drop.** The `heading` ref is dropped from the COMPACT tier everywhere (mixed lists included — search/today/inbox/upcoming/logbook/trash/changes): the GUI shows the project, never the heading, outside a project view. The full tier and `detail` KEEP `heading`.
  - (Touched: `src/read/shape.ts`, `test/unit/shape.test.ts`, `test/cli/e2e.test.ts`, `test/fixtures/seed.ts` (settable leaf-action counts); docs `contract.md` (glossary: `tags`/`todos`/`hasNotes` added, `notesTruncated` deleted), `design/contracts.md` (R6/R7 + universal reshapes + omit-empty table), `design/api-doctrine.md`, `up-next.md`.)
- **NEW (dev) — `npm run explore`: a localhost-only live response explorer.** Serves a single-page debug view (`scripts/explore/`) that runs `things …` commands via a tokenized argv (never a shell) against the default database and renders the envelope as a collapsible JSON tree, with presets, history, stderr/exit-code panes, and the exact executed argv echoed. Reads on an allowlist (cross-checked against the CLI registry by a unit test) run verbatim with `--json` implied; EVERYTHING else gets `--dry-run` force-appended server-side, so mutations can only ever produce plans; `batch`/`mcp` are refused. Binds to `127.0.0.1` only; zero new dependencies. (New: `scripts/explore/server.ts`, `scripts/explore/index.html`, `test/unit/explore.test.ts`; touched: `package.json`, `README.md`.)
- **⚠️ BREAKING (search) — R8 match provenance `{field, text}` + a checklist-body search arm; ALPHA-CONTRACT clean break, `apiVersion` STAYS `1`.** The heading-only `matchedVia: {kind, title}` annotation on a `search` hit is REPLACED by the converged, presence-keyed **`match: {field, text}`** shape across all three arms (`src/read/views.ts` `searchView`) — one word, one shape, shared by CLI `--json`, the MCP `search` tool, and the human TTY render. `field` ∈ `"heading"` | `"notes"` | `"checklist"`; `text` is the matched heading title, a bounded ~80-char notes snippet (whitespace-collapsed, centered on the hit, `…`-elided), or the matched checklist item's title. **A TITLE match carries NO annotation** (presence-keyed — absence = matched where you'd expect); at most one annotation, precedence title (none) > heading > notes > checklist. **Two behavior changes:** (1) **notes matches are now annotated** — previously a notes-only hit carried nothing; it now carries `match: {field: "notes", …}`. (2) **NEW checklist-body arm** — `search` now joins `TMChecklistItem` and matches checklist-item titles, surfacing the PARENT to-do (deduped — a to-do appears once no matter how many of its checklist rows match, credited to the FIRST matching row by index). **Checklist-item uuids appear on NO surface** (an implementation detail, like tag uuids; a regression test asserts the payload carries none). The heading arm is skipped for a `--type to-do` search; the checklist arm for a `--type project` search. The result RANK order is unchanged (title > notes > heading > checklist); only the annotation is new. **TTY:** an annotated hit gains an indented muted matched-on line (`  ⤷ heading: "…"` / `  ⤷ notes: "…snippet…"` / `  ⤷ checklist: "…"`), replacing the old `(via heading "…")` suffix; a title hit renders as a plain row. `match` rides the compact tier (a non-default fact). (Touched: `src/read/views.ts`, `src/read/search-rank.ts`, `src/read/shape.ts`, `src/cli/render.ts`; docs `contract.md`, `design/contracts.md`, `design/api-doctrine.md`, `up-next.md`; tests `test/unit/views.test.ts`, `test/unit/shape.test.ts`, `test/cli/e2e.test.ts`, `test/mcp/server.test.ts`.)
- **NEW — single-op idempotency: `--op-id <key>` on the single-invocation write commands (MCP `op_id` on the single-mutation write tools), phase 1.** Generalizes the shipped batch `opId` machinery to single mutations so an ambiguously-failed write is safe to resubmit without a double. A caller-supplied key (`[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}`, same charset as a batch line) is recorded on the mutation's audit record; before dispatch it is matched against the recent change history (last 1000 records ∧ 7 days — the SAME lookback as `batch`, now lifted into the shared `src/write/opid.ts` and reused, not forked) for a VERIFIED-OK record with that key. **On a match** the mutation SKIPS execution entirely, exits 0, and returns the command's normal success envelope carrying the ORIGINAL result's identity (`op`, `uuid`, `undoToken`, and `title` when the record stored one) plus presence-keyed **`alreadyApplied: true`**; no new record is written. **On no match** it runs normally and the new record stores the `opId`. The idempotency CHECK runs in the client's single-op entry, so the `batch` path and the compound orchestrators are unaffected; the id namespace is shared across the trail (a batch leg and a single op with the same key match each other). A malformed key is a usage error (exit 2 / MCP `usage`). **The variadic/compound verbs refuse `--op-id`** with a teaching usage error naming the batch analogue — multi-item `move`/`reorder`, granular checklist edits, `clear-reminder`, project `make-repeating`/`add-repeating`, `archive-heading`/`unarchive-heading`, and project `reopen` are multi-leg compounds whose idempotency is the batch-shaped per-line `op_id`. **Phase 1 matches ok records only**; reconciling a resubmission against a `verify-failed:timeout` original is queued as phase 2 (`docs/up-next.md`). (New: `src/write/opid.ts`, `test/unit/opid.test.ts`, `test/cli/single-op-idempotency-cli.test.ts`; touched: `src/write/batch.ts`, `src/write/pipeline.ts`, `src/client.ts`, `src/index.ts`, `src/cli/commands/writes.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`, `test/mcp/server.test.ts`; docs `contract.md`, `design/api-doctrine.md`, `design/contracts.md`, `up-next.md`.)
- **TEST (no wire/behavior change) — a 30 s explicit timeout on two slow-seed tests that flaked at the default 5 s on loaded CI runners (green on rerun during #305).** The tag-echo-hint e2e (`test/cli/e2e.test.ts`, seeds ~120 tagged rows) and the bench-world DB-hash determinism test (`test/unit/bench-world.test.ts`, builds three fixture DBs) now carry `{ timeout: 30_000 }`, matching the precedent set for the two five-seed bench-world loops (#283). No other change.
- **⚠️ BREAKING — R6 no-redundant-ancestry + R7 compact detail tiers (`--full` escape hatch); ALPHA-CONTRACT clean break, `apiVersion` STAYS `1`.** The read-payload JSON shape is reshaped at the emit boundary shared by CLI `--json` and the MCP read tools (`src/read/shape.ts`, applied before omit-empty); the in-memory library entities are unchanged and the human render is untouched. One consumer (the maintainer) → the old shapes are DELETED, no shims. Four coordinated changes:
  - **R6 — no-redundant-ancestry (deterministic by VIEW KIND).** An item never states a fact its enclosing node already states. A **project-view** child (in any bucket, incl. heading-group members) drops `project`/`headingProject` + `area`; a heading-group member additionally drops `heading`. An **area-view** child/project-card drops `area` (project-child items keep `project`). An **anytime/someday** section item drops `area` (incl. the explicit `area: null` section), keeping `project`/`heading`. **Mixed-provenance lists keep every ref** (`inbox`, `today`, `upcoming`, `logbook`, `trash`, `search`, `changes`, `projects`). The dropped `area` is provably the enclosing node's: it is the EFFECTIVE area and a project/heading child carries `area = NULL` in the DB, so it resolves through its container to the card's area — only redundant facts are deleted. **Teaching read: absence of a container field INSIDE a container view = inherited from the enclosing node.**
  - **R7 — named detail tiers (compact | full), NOT a field-projection language.** Every list context (`items`, `sections`, the collection arrays inside `view`) now defaults to a COMPACT line-item; `detail`/`show` return the FULL record; the **new `--full` flag** (CLI list/show commands) and **`full: true` param** (MCP `read_view`/`search`/`changes_since`/`get_project`/`get_area`/`list_collections`) force the full record in a list context. Compact = identity + structural + non-default facts: `status`/`logged`/`trashed` are omitted at their default (**absence = the default**), `created`/`modified` are dropped (get them from `detail`), and `notes` is a ≤120-char first-line preview with a presence-keyed `notesTruncated: true` marker when the full text differs. `--full` restores per-row density but R6 still applies. `--brief` (force compact on a detail) was NOT built.
  - **Checklist reshape (UNIVERSAL — both tiers + `detail`).** The flat `checklistItemsCount` / `openChecklistItemsCount` are REMOVED from the wire and fold into one presence-keyed `checklist: {open, total}` (a `detail` read nests the items at `checklist.items`); a to-do with no checklist carries no `checklist` key at all.
  - **Repeating reshape (UNIVERSAL) + bucket-implied lifecycle flags.** The all-false `repeating` block is dropped entirely (a real template/instance keeps a minimal truthful object). `logged`/`trashed` are dropped even when TRUE where the enclosing view/section states them (the `trash`/`logbook` views, the card `logged`/`trashed` sections) but SURVIVE on mixed surfaces (search/changes) where they disambiguate.
  - (New: `src/read/shape.ts`, `test/unit/shape.test.ts`; touched: `src/index.ts`, `src/surface-copy.ts` (`FULL_DESC`), `src/cli/read-driver.ts`, `src/cli/commands/{reads,show,project,area}.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`, `test/cli/e2e.test.ts`, `test/mcp/server.test.ts`, `test/fixtures/seed.ts` (settable checklist counts); docs `contract.md` (glossary), `design/api-doctrine.md` §7, `design/contracts.md`, `up-next.md`.)
- **NEW — the machine-readable envelope JSON Schema (`schema/envelope.schema.json`), GENERATED from the types with three-way sync enforcement (PR B of the contract canon).** Makes true the `docs/contract.md` promise that the envelope "also ships in machine-readable form." A new `WireEnvelope` union in `src/contracts.ts` (the discriminated union — on `ok` — of the success envelope and the error envelope; exported through the barrel with `WireOkEnvelope` / `WireOkKind` / `WireData`) is the schema's root; `npm run schema:gen` (`scripts/generate-schema.ts`, `ts-json-schema-generator`) regenerates the committed draft-07 schema and runs `oxfmt` as its last step so it is format-stable. The schema is packaged for npm consumers (`package.json` `files` gains `schema`). **Coverage boundary:** the envelope layer is modeled EXACTLY — `apiVersion` (const `1`), `ok`, `kind` (an enum of the known success kinds + the literal `"error"`), the whole `meta` object, and the entire `error` object — while the command-specific `data` payload is an OPEN object (the per-`kind` payloads, with their omit-empty pruning, are not individually modeled; `src/contracts.ts` + `docs/contract.md` stay authoritative). **Three-way sync is test-enforced** (`test/contract/envelope-schema.test.ts`): (1) types↔schema — regenerate in-process and deep-equal the committed file (drift → "run `npm run schema:gen`"); (2) schema↔runtime — compile with `ajv` and validate a representative set of real emitted CLI envelopes (flat list, `today`, `anytime`, `area-view`, `project-view`, `detail`, a mutation-ok, and an error envelope), each failure reporting the ajv error path. Generator note recorded in the docs: `error.code` models as a plain `string` because the `ErrorCode` template-literal families (`` `blocked:${string}` `` / `` `verify-failed:${string}` ``) widen the union to `string` (an open `${string}` suffix does not become a `pattern`). `ts-json-schema-generator` + `ajv` are devDependencies only (pinned; kept out of runtime/guest-bundle paths). (New: `schema/envelope.schema.json`, `scripts/generate-schema.ts`, `test/contract/envelope-schema.test.ts`; touched: `src/contracts.ts`, `src/index.ts`, `package.json`, `docs/contract.md`, `docs/up-next.md`.)

Both DEFINE the covenant that begins at v1.0 and add NO compatibility machinery now (ALPHA-CONTRACT still governs). - **DOCS — README "Why this wrapper is different" differentiators.** A new section after the intro presenting verified writes (read-after-write, `ok` = observed), the safety rails (writes only through official app surfaces, disruption tiers + hazard guards, container scope), the written contract (envelope grammar + covenant + glossary + registry, linking `docs/contract.md`), and the agent-native design (self-correcting errors, honest truncation, token-economy discipline). Every claim is code-true today.

No behavior change; the doc now matches shipped code and the new canon.
- **INTERNAL (registry enforcement, NOT a wire change) — the error envelope's `code` field is narrowed from `string` to the exported `ErrorCode` union in `src/contracts.ts`, so the compiler IS the error-code registry.** No emitted code changes (the same values ship on the wire); the type simply refuses an undocumented code at compile time. Surfaced one stale artifact — a unit test asserting `drift-blocked`, a code the CLI never actually emits (the real drift-block code is `blocked:drift`, exit 5) — now corrected. `ErrorCode` is exported through the barrel; the MCP `errorResult` and the `diagnose` error shape are tightened to it too. - **⚠️ BREAKING — the 1.0 JSON envelope shape break (ALPHA-CONTRACT clean break; `apiVersion` STAYS `1`, there is no "v2").** The read/write envelope `data` is reshaped per the ratified rules R1–R5 (`docs/up-next.md` §0½ item 3). One consumer (the maintainer) → the old shapes are DELETED outright, no shims/aliases/dual-emission. The library keeps its in-memory unions unchanged; this is purely the JSON wire boundary (CLI `--json` + the shared library view/error payloads MCP inherits).
  - **R1 — `data` is ALWAYS a JSON object, never a bare array.** Every flat list (`inbox`, `upcoming`, `logbook`, `trash`, `changes`, `search`, `projects`, `areas`, `tags`, `legend`, `capabilities`, `config` list form) is now `{ items: [...] }`. `today` is `{ sections: [{key:"today",items},{key:"evening",items}], badge }`. `anytime`/`someday` are `{ sections: [{area, items}, …] }`.
  - **R2 — one wrapper per payload class, and the `show` router disappears from the wire.** `data.item` (single entity), `data.view` (composite area/project card), `data.items` (flat), `data.sections` (sectioned). The kinds `show` and `todo-detail` UNIFY into kind **`detail`** with `data.item` (`item.type` discriminates). A loose `show` that resolves to an area/project now emits the IDENTICAL `kind:"area-view"` / `kind:"project-view"` envelope (`data.view`) that `area show` / `project show` do — the old `{type, view}` wrapper is gone. (The CLI command vocabulary still has `show`; `meta.resolvedCommand` records the routing.)
  - **R3 — inside a view every named collection is an ARRAY; date-groups `{date, items}` are the only intermediate nesting.** The `later: {…}` object wrapper is flattened away in BOTH area-view and project-view → top-level `scheduled: [{date,items}]`, `someday: []`, `repeating: []` alongside `active` / `projects` / `logged` / `trashed`. Omit-empty behavior is unchanged.
  - **R4 — ONE `meta.truncation` shape absorbs `meta.grouped` (now DELETED).** `{ shown, total, limit, truncated, sections?, blocks? }`: `today` puts its per-section counts in `sections`, grouped views (anytime/someday/`area show`) put their `GroupBlock[]` nesting in `blocks` (with aggregate `shown`/`total`, `limit: null`). **`meta.truncation.truncated` is the universal completeness check.**
  - **R5 — the envelope carries call success; the payload carries NO redundant success discriminator.** Doctrine (one field, one job): `ok`/exit code = did the call work; `kind` = payload class, nowhere else; `outcome` = per-line disposition in JSONL streams only; `status` = domain lifecycle on resources only; `error.code` + `error.detail` = why it failed. So the single-op mutation/move success payload's internal `kind` discriminator is DROPPED from the wire entirely — `data` has no `result` and no `kind`, just the payload fields (`op`, `uuid`, `title`, `undoToken`, `observed`, `vector`, `tier`, `touched`, notes, …); the envelope's `ok:true` + `kind:"mutation-result"`/`"move-result"` are the success signal. **Batch JSONL lines FLATTEN to one level:** `outcome` becomes the internal tag as a plain string (`"ok"`, `"blocked"`, `"verify-failed"`, `"unsupported"`, `"dry-run"`, `"invalid"`, `"skipped"`, `"already-applied"`, `"bounce-aborted"`) and each variant's fields (`uuid`, `detail`, `plan`, `expected`/`observed`, `considered`, `placed`/`remaining`/`cause`, …) hoist to line level as siblings of `index`/`op`/`tempId`/`boundUuid`/`opId`; the duplicate per-line `op` collapses to the single line-level key (no variant field collides). The summary/undo streams are unchanged (they carry no nested discriminator). **Plus the mandatory rider: `error.detail` and `error.details` reconcile into ONE `error.detail`** object `{ expected?, observed?, candidates?, suggestions?, considered?, placed?, remaining?, cause?, failed?, completed? }`.
  - **BEHAVIOR CHANGE (read-layer fidelity, §9) — project-view heading sub-buckets.** `projectView` previously pooled a heading's scheduled/someday/repeating children into the PROJECT-level buckets regardless of their heading FK — wrong vs the GUI. Now each heading group carries its OWN `{ heading, items, scheduled, someday, repeating }` sub-buckets, and the project-level buckets retain only UNHEADED members (R3's array-flatten applies at both levels). New read-layer tests cover headed scheduled/someday/repeating children.
  - MCP framing alignment is explicitly OUT OF SCOPE (phase 2): MCP keeps its content-block framing and inherits the reshaped library view/error payloads automatically; its mutation content block still carries the internal discriminator until the phase-2 sweep.

- **NEW op `project.dissolve-heading` — remove a heading but KEEP its to-dos, which become direct children of the project (`things project dissolve-heading <project> <heading>`; MCP `heading` tool `dissolve_heading` action).** The child-KEEPING "unwrap this section" op — GUI-only via the heading row's `…` ellipsis → `Delete` (**lab-certified DISS1** 2026-07-28: the heading row is hard-deleted while its children re-home — `heading`→NULL, `project`→parent, `index` preserved, **NOT trashed** — with **no confirm sheet**). **⚠️ Named `dissolve`, not `delete`, and contrasted LOUDLY with the Shortcuts heading-delete cascade (P12), which TRASHES the children** — the two "delete a heading" surfaces do opposite things to the to-dos (oddities §9j); confusing them loses data. **Two-key gated** (`ui.enabled` + `--dangerously-drive-gui`), tier 3; fails closed on a source-heading title collision and on a titleless heading. **Undo: none wired** (irreversible-flagged — the compound re-create-heading + re-home-children inverse spans surfaces; children survive, so nothing is lost). Verify oracle is the heading GONE via a new `gone`/`task` delta mode (a hard-deleted task row). (Touched: `src/write/operations.ts`, `pre-state.ts`, `commands.ts`, `guards.ts`, `verify/delta.ts`, `vectors/ui-recipes.ts`, `vectors/ui-certification.ts`, `undo.ts`, `reversibility.ts`, `src/client.ts`, `src/cli/commands/writes.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`; tests `test/engine/write-heading-dissolve.test.ts`, updated reversibility-matrix + readme-contract; docs `capability-matrix.md`, `design/heading-demotion-and-move.md`, `things-app-oddities.md` §9j, `lab/reordgaps-results.md` DISS1.)
- **NEW op `project.move-heading-to-project` — relocate a heading (with its to-dos) to a DIFFERENT project (`things project move-heading-to-project <src> <heading> --to <dst>`; MCP `heading` tool `move_heading_to_project` action). Closes the last heading capability gap.** GUI-only — no headless spelling exists on any vector (URL ⛔, AppleScript `move → project` 301, Shortcuts ⛔) — so it is a ui-vector op: the heading row's `…` ellipsis → `Move…` menu → a search-by-title project picker (HEADXPROJ recipe; **lab-certified HXPC1** 2026-07-28 — heading `project` FK rewritten, children follow via their intact heading FK, a single-row change). **Two-key gated** (`ui.enabled` + `--dangerously-drive-gui`), disruption tier 3. **Fails closed on BOTH title-collision surfaces** the title-addressed drive cannot disambiguate: a source heading title shared by another heading in the project, and a destination title shared by another project (the picker searches by title). Titleless headings are refused. **Kept a SEPARATE verb** from `project.move-heading` (a pure within-project reorder) so the cross-container GUI move is never conflated with an in-place shuffle. **Undo: none wired** — app-reversible by moving it back; `undo` reports it irreversible and names the manual inverse. (Touched: `src/write/operations.ts`, `pre-state.ts`, `commands.ts`, `guards.ts`, `vectors/ui-recipes.ts`, `vectors/ui-certification.ts`, `undo.ts`, `reversibility.ts`, `src/client.ts`, `src/cli/commands/writes.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`; tests `test/engine/write-heading-move-to-project.test.ts`, updated reversibility-matrix + readme-contract; docs `capability-matrix.md`, `design/heading-demotion-and-move.md`.)
- **BOUNCEJSON collapse — a bounce whose placement leg is `when=anytime` into a loose/heading bucket now dispatches as ONE pre-validated `things:///json` array instead of 2N sequential URL opens (~7×; `docs/lab/reordgaps-results.md` BOUNCEJSON, oddities §9i).** The reorder compiler collapses exactly the two eligible classes — a heading's anytime children (`heading`, back-insert) and area-less loose anytime to-dos (`anytime`, front-insert) — into a single array carrying `[{when:someday},{when:anytime}]` per item in the same per-item order the sequential loop uses (array order == result index order). Eligibility is keyed off the placement-leg VALUE, not the front/back direction: a someday-placement class (`area-someday`, `project-someday`) or an area-direct member is json index-INERT (a collapse would be a silent no-op), so those stay on the sequential URL bounce, as do `today`/`evening` (todayIndex legs) and `projects` (`project.update`, unproven). The collapse is validate-first FULL-ABORT (a single unresolvable ref rejects the whole array), so a failed dispatch means NOTHING landed — the result/abort copy drops the sequential path's "stranded in someday"/partial-progress branch, and one terminal ordering verify replaces the per-item checks. `--dry-run` reports `json-collapse ×N … 1 dispatch / 2N ops` for the eligible classes. Under the write simulator (no json-array applier) the proven sequential legs are used, unchanged. Certified by o-suite **O18** (`order.bounce-json-collapse`). Encoded on `BounceSpec.jsonCollapsible` + `bounceJsonCollapsible()`, pinned by a classification regression test. (Touched: `src/write/reorder.ts`; `test/engine/write-reorder.test.ts`, `test/engine/write-move.test.ts`; `lab/suites/o-suite.json`; docs `capability-matrix.md`, `reference/suite-audit.md`, `lab/reordgaps-results.md`.)
- **Phase A.1 BUILD — the proven ordering protocols are wired into the move/reorder planner; five placement classes flip from app-default to GUARANTEED (`docs/lab/reordgaps-results.md` REORDGAPS + BOUNCE2).** The `move`/`reorder` planner now compiles the exact leg laws banked by the BOUNCE2 campaign, so these buckets are placed deterministically instead of falling back to app-default:
  - **Within-heading order** — a heading's anytime children reorder via a FORWARD-order `someday→anytime` bounce (back-insert; BOUNCE2-h).
  - **Area someday order** — an area's someday members reorder via a REVERSE-order `someday↔anytime` bounce (front-insert; SOMEBNC-area). The destructive area reorder command (§9f) is NEVER used.
  - **Project someday children** — the native SOMEORD-b re-rank stays primary; a FORWARD-order bounce (SOMEBNC-project) is the documented fallback when the native surface is unavailable.
  - **Container same-day scheduled children** — a project's same-day scheduled to-dos re-rank on `todayIndex` via the container specifier, date-preserving (DAYORD-b, native).
  - **Area-less loose anytime** — reverse-order front-insert bounce (ANYBNC).
  Result payloads flip from the app-default note to `guaranteed` for these classes; the honesty note machinery stays for anything still unproven (a headed scheduled/someday sub-bucket, a project/area child's evening sub-bucket, a loose future day, repeating templates).
- **NEW config keys `bounce-enabled` (default `true`) and `bounce-max-items` (default `30`) — `things config set bounce-enabled|bounce-max-items`, `THINGS_API_BOUNCE_ENABLED` / `THINGS_API_BOUNCE_MAX_ITEMS` env overrides (bidirectional), surfaced by `config get` with provenance.** With `bounce-enabled=false` the planner REFUSES every bounce-dependent placement with a teaching error naming the flag — it never silently falls back to a destructive or unverified path (those matrix rows degrade to unsupported).
- **⚠️ BEHAVIOR CHANGE (not breaking): the bounce cap default rises from a hardcoded `10` to a configurable `30`.** `bounce-max-items` replaces `BOUNCE_MAX_ITEMS=10` everywhere bounce legs compile (today/evening/projects/heading/area-someday/anytime scopes — one knob). Backed by the BOUNCE2-t timing (~110 ms/item guest-local, 2 verified mutations/item). `--dry-run` reports the leg count.
- **⚠️ BEHAVIOR CHANGE (not breaking): `allow-experimental` now DEFAULTS TO TRUE.** The reorder command it gates is the app's PRIVATE, UNDOCUMENTED sdef surface — not a half-baked "experimental" one. It is safe on by default because every write is verify-per-write, fingerprint-gated, and canaried by the o-suite (including O17, the DAYORD-b certification) — together the divergence-detection Mike required. The bidirectional env/config off-switch is retained (`things config set allow-experimental false` / `THINGS_API_ALLOW_EXPERIMENTAL=false`). The planner now selects the best ENABLED strategy per placement — native preferred, proven bounce as fallback — and refuses only where no enabled path exists (generalizing the old today-scope-only fallback).
- **`--before`/`--after` against an UNMOVED sibling is now SUPPORTED inside bounce buckets via an extended bounce (was refused).** Back-insert buckets (heading, project someday) forward-bounce the block-through-end suffix; front-insert buckets (area someday, area-less anytime) reverse-bounce the top-through-block prefix. The `bounce-max-items` cap counts TOUCHED items (block + co-bounced siblings), `--dry-run` reports "touches N unnamed siblings, 2N legs", and the ok result payload lists the co-bounced-but-unnamed refs explicitly (`touched`) — they get modification bumps, changes-feed entries, and audit legs, disclosed honestly. The ONLY remaining anchor refusal class is a bucket with no reorder protocol at all.
- **Bounce verify relaxation: one verify per item round-trip instead of per leg.** The transient AWAY/BACK `when=` legs dispatch with the state-verify skipped (new `WriteOptions.skipVerify`, transport fail-loud retained); the orchestrator's placed-position check after each item is the single per-round-trip assertion (asserts the position delta + the when-restore).
  - Touched: `src/config.ts`, `src/write/reorder.ts`, `src/write/move.ts`, `src/write/pre-state.ts`, `src/write/commands.ts`, `src/write/operations.ts`, `src/write/pipeline.ts`, `src/cli/commands/writes.ts`, `src/cli/help.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`, `skills/things-cli/SKILL.md`; docs (`capability-matrix.md`, `design/heading-demotion-and-move.md`, `up-next.md`, `lab/probe-backlog.md`); tests in `test/engine/write-reorder.test.ts`, `test/engine/write-move.test.ts`, `test/unit/config.test.ts`, and the updated cli/mcp suites.

- **The `move` / `reorder` vocabulary — variadic membership moves and in-place reordering, honest to the wire (spec `docs/design/heading-demotion-and-move.md` §4/§5/§7).** MOVE changes what an item belongs to; REORDER changes only its arrangement in place. Both verbs are variadic (selection order = landing order; reverse by naming them backwards) and compile onto the existing `todo.move` / `project.move` / `reorder` wire primitives through a minimal-move planner — **no new op kind** is introduced.
  - **`things todo move <refs…>` is reworked (BREAKING).** Destinations respell to the `--to-*` family: `--to-project <ref>` / `--to-heading <sel>` / `--to-area <ref>` (was `--project`/`--heading`/`--area`), plus the detach family `--no-heading` (leave the heading, stay in the project) and `--loose` (the total sever: heading, project, AND area). `--inbox` stays. Positions: `--first | --last | --before <ref> | --after <ref>`.
  - **`things todo reorder <refs…>` is NEW** — pure positioning within the movees' current shared container+bucket, never a membership change. **Bare form is legal**: it assembles the named to-dos as one contiguous block at the EARLIEST movee's current slot, in argument order (`--first` is NOT implied); partial selections are fine (unmentioned siblings keep their order). Cross-container/cross-bucket operands fail closed.
  - **`things project move <refs…>` is reworked (BREAKING):** variadic + `--to-area <ref>` / `--no-area` (was `--area`/`--detach`) + the position flags.
  - **BREAKING: `--detach` is REMOVED outright** (no alias). Its three conflated intents split into the detach family: `--no-heading` and `--loose` (to-dos) and `--no-area` (projects). The removed flag and the wrong-kind flags are TEACHING errors: `--detach` names the replacement family; `--loose` on a project points at `--no-area`; `--no-area` on a to-do explains its area is inherited (use `--loose`, or move the project). The single-item op params rename accordingly: `todo.move` `detach` → `loose` (+ new `noHeading`), `project.move` `detach` → `noArea`.
  - **Six core rules (§4):** selection order = destination block order (per bucket); anchors POSITION but never MIGRATE (an anchor-only move that would cross a container fails closed); homogeneous movee kinds (one KIND per call — a mixed selection is a usage error naming the offenders); mixed-bucket = GUI parity (membership is always legal, each movee lands top-of-its-own-bucket in selection order; `--before`/`--after` require every movee in the anchor's bucket); **placement honesty** (calibrated to the REORDGAPS verdicts, `docs/lab/reordgaps-results.md`) — "top of bucket" is GUARANTEED where a lab-clean protocol exists (loose inbox/today/evening/someday, a project's unheaded anytime OR someday children per SOMEORD-b, an area's anytime members, area-less someday/anytime projects); APP-DEFAULT (membership lands, order best-effort — a protocol exists but is not wired into the `reorder` op yet) for a container's scheduled-day bucket (DAYORD-b), area-less loose anytime (ANYBNC), heading buckets (HEADORD-b — no native surface), and repeating templates (§9e); and REFUSED as destructive for ordering an area's someday to-dos (§9f). The result payload states which class applied; compilation via the minimal-move planner (membership legs + a placement reorder leg).
  - **The full §7 refusal matrix** ships with exact teaching-error copy per surface-copy.md and uuid-bearing candidates on resolution failures (homogeneity, illegal destination, anchor-outside-container, anchor bucket-mismatch, the three detach rows, both bare-invocation rows).
  - **MCP.** `move_todo` / `move_project` are reworked to the variadic vocabulary (an in-place reorder is a position with no destination); the `reorder` multiplexer's description teaches the move-vs-reorder distinction. Container scope (#276) composes — every leg runs the universal scope gate, and an out-of-scope anchor reads as not-found (the no-oracle guarantee).
  - **Reversibility is unchanged** because no new op kind ships: a move/reorder is undone leg-by-leg through the existing `todo.move` / `project.move` (conditional — the pre-op container/area capture) and `reorder` (conditional — the pre-rank capture) inverse patterns. `things undo --last N` reverses the legs newest-first.
  - Docs: `things help move` (new topic), `skills/things-cli/SKILL.md` (move-vs-reorder made explicit), `docs/capability-matrix.md`, `docs/up-next.md`. (`src/write/move.ts` (new), `operations.ts`, `commands.ts`, `scope-guard.ts`, `vectors/simulator.ts`, `src/client.ts`, `src/index.ts`, `src/cli/commands/writes.ts`, `src/cli/move-hint.ts`, `src/cli/help.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`; tests in `test/engine/write-move.test.ts`, and updated `write-p19`/`write-simulator`/`scope`/`help-contract`/`move-hint`/`write-cli`/`e2e`/`mcp` suites.)

- **BREAKING: heading demotion — the `things heading …` namespace is REMOVED; the heading operations respell as project-scoped verbs (spec `docs/design/heading-demotion-and-move.md` §2).** A heading exists only inside a project, and the grammar now says so.
  - **Namespace removed.** The top-level `things heading` command group is gone. Its verbs move under `things project` and take a `<project-ref>` then a heading selector: `project add-heading`, `project rename-heading … --to`, `project archive-heading`, `project unarchive-heading`, `project promote-heading` (was `heading convert-to-project`), and the new `project move-heading` (variadic; replaces `reorder --scope headings`). Removed spellings fail with the standard unknown-command error — no aliases.
  - **Op-kind renames (wire break — agents pass op kinds directly to `run_operation`/`batch`, and they appear in JSON results):** `heading.add` → `project.add-heading`, `heading.rename` → `project.rename-heading`, `heading.archive` → `project.archive-heading`, `heading.unarchive` → `project.unarchive-heading`, `heading.convert-to-project` → `project.promote-heading`; plus the new `project.move-heading`. The `run_operation`/`capabilities` catalog grows from 49 to 50 op kinds.
  - **`reorder --scope headings` is REMOVED** (MCP `reorder` `scope` enum drops `headings`, and the CLI/MCP help point at `project move-heading` instead). Its replacement ships in this change; the other reorder scopes are untouched.
  - **Heading selector.** Every heading-consuming surface now shares ONE resolver (exported `resolveHeadingUuid`, also behind `todo add/move --heading`): an exact title OR a uuid, empty-string title a legal literal query, duplicates fail closed with uuid-bearing candidates (the H-DUPLICATE-TAG precedent), and NO ordinal/index form (an index silently re-targets a different heading after any reorder). `project add-heading` and `project move-heading` gain the placement flags `--first | --last | --before-heading <sel> | --after-heading <sel>`; heading reorder rides the same experimental surface as `reorder` (`things config set allow-experimental true`).
  - **MCP.** The `heading` multiplexer tool respells its actions to `add_heading | rename_heading | archive_heading | unarchive_heading | promote_heading | move_heading` (project param now required; heading selector semantics identical). To-do promotion moves to a dedicated `convert_to_project` tool (the heading tool's `promote_heading` is heading-only).
  - **Audit trail: NO alias map.** Pre-rename records carrying `heading.*` op kinds become unrecognized and non-undoable — the accepted, intended consequence of the ALPHA-CONTRACT doctrine (delete the old shape outright; no legacy reader).
  - **ui-vector certification carries over.** `project.promote-heading` keeps the ui-vector gating and its HEADCERT1 certification identity (the manifest key in `src/write/vectors/ui-certification.ts` is renamed; the evidence is unchanged).
  - Touched: `src/write/operations.ts`, `commands.ts`, `pre-state.ts`, `guards.ts`, `pipeline.ts`, `reorder.ts`, `heading.ts`, `undo.ts`, `batch.ts`, `scope-guard.ts`, `reversibility.ts`, `capabilities.ts`, `vectors/{applescript,shortcuts,ui-recipes,ui-certification,simulator}.ts`, `src/read/queries.ts`, `src/client.ts`, `src/index.ts`, `src/cli/commands/writes.ts`, `src/cli/help.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`; docs (`capability-matrix.md`, `reference/suite-audit.md`), `skills/things-cli/SKILL.md`, and the test suites.
- **`things todo add` takes MANY titles — a quick titles-only skeleton path, compiled onto the batch machinery.** `things todo add "T1" "T2" "T3" [shared flags]` creates several to-dos in one call; every shared flag (`--project`/`--area`/`--heading`/`--when`/`--reminder`/`--deadline`/`--tags`/`--checklist-item`/`--acknowledge-project-reopen`/`--create-tags`) is applied to EACH title, and the titles land in argument order. Two companion flags: `--stdin` reads newline-delimited titles from stdin (blank lines skipped — mutually exclusive with positional titles; fails closed on zero titles), and `--id-only` prints exactly one uuid per line in creation order and nothing else (TTY chrome suppressed; mutually exclusive with `--json`), for shell chaining. Under the hood a multi-title add is ONE batch of `todo.add` legs: per-item results stream (JSONL under `--json`, one human `ok todo.add …` line otherwise) followed by a summary carrying a single `undoToken` that removes the whole skeleton (`things undo --txn <token>`). **A single-title `todo add` is byte-identical to before** — it still returns the one single mutation-result envelope (or, with `--id-only`, just the new uuid). The documented new-project-with-children skeleton path is `things project add "<title>" --todo "T1" --todo "T2" …` (`--todo` repeatable, already shipped). MCP is unchanged: bulk creation goes through the existing `batch` tool (N `todo.add` ops → one `undoToken`), which already covers it cleanly — adding a `titles` array to `add_todo` would fork that tool's return shape between a mutation-result and a batch stream, so it was left to `batch`. Batch per-op `options` additionally accept `createTags` so a bulk add honors `--create-tags`. (`src/cli/commands/writes.ts`, `src/write/batch.ts`; tests in `test/cli/bulk-add-cli.test.ts`, `test/cli/write-cli.test.ts`, `test/engine/write-batch-temp-ids.test.ts`, `test/cli/help-contract.test.ts`.)

- **BREAKING: the `heading.create` operation is renamed `heading.add`.** Verb harmonization — `add` is the canonical create-verb across the operation catalog (`todo.add`/`project.add`/`area.add`/`tag.add`), so the odd-one-out `create` spellings are retired. What changes: the op-kind STRING `heading.create` → `heading.add` (it appears in `run_operation`/`batch` op fields and in JSON results — agents pass op kinds directly, so this IS a wire break); the MCP `heading` tool's `action` value `create` → `add`. What does NOT change: the CLI verb `things heading add <project> <title>` was already `add`. No alias verbs (the `config show` precedent): the old op-kind and action spellings now fail with the standard unknown-command / usage error. Pre-rename audit records are NOT normalized (per the alpha-contract doctrine — see the dedicated entry below): a `heading.create` record written before the rename carries an op-kind the current code no longer recognizes, so it becomes non-undoable/ignored — the accepted, intended consequence. (`src/write/operations.ts`, `src/write/commands.ts`, `src/write/undo.ts`, `src/write/reversibility.ts`, `src/write/vectors/shortcuts.ts`, `src/write/vectors/simulator.ts`, `src/client.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`; tests in `test/unit/undo-plan.test.ts`, `test/unit/write-compile.test.ts`, `test/unit/write-guards.test.ts`, `test/unit/reversibility-matrix.test.ts`, `test/engine/write-shortcuts.test.ts`, `test/mcp/server.test.ts`.)
- **BREAKING: the `project.create-repeating` operation is renamed `project.add-repeating`.** The same `add` harmonization (`make-`/`convert-` stay distinct as transform verbs). What changes: the op-kind STRING `project.create-repeating` → `project.add-repeating` (a wire break in `run_operation`/`batch` op fields and JSON results); the CLI command `things project create-repeating <title>` → `things project add-repeating <title>`; the MCP `repeat` tool's `action` value `create` → `add` (scope `project`, keeping the multiplexer enum consistent). No alias verbs — the old spellings fail with the standard unknown-command / usage error. Pre-rename audit records are NOT normalized either (alpha-contract doctrine, entry below): an old `project.create-repeating` record is ignored by undo — moot in practice, as the op is irreversible under either name. (`src/write/operations.ts`, `src/write/commands.ts`, `src/write/make-repeating-project.ts`, `src/write/undo.ts`, `src/write/scope-guard.ts`, `src/write/pipeline.ts`, `src/write/reversibility.ts`, `src/write/vectors/ui-certification.ts`, `src/write/vectors/simulator.ts`, `src/client.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`, `src/cli/commands/writes.ts`, `src/cli/help.ts`; tests in `test/engine/write-project-make-repeating.test.ts`, `test/engine/write-simulator.test.ts`, `test/unit/reversibility-matrix.test.ts`, `test/mcp/server.test.ts`.)
- **Removed the legacy op-kind alias map — pre-rename audit records are no longer normalized (alpha-contract doctrine).** The verb-harmonization rename above shipped with a read-time normalizer (`LEGACY_OP_ALIASES` / `normalizeOpKind` in `readAuditRecords`) that mapped old `heading.create` / `project.create-repeating` op-kinds to their new names so pre-rename audit records stayed undoable. Under the newly-recorded **ALPHA-CONTRACT** doctrine (the whole contract is alpha until v1.0, with exactly one consumer and NO compatibility shims — see `AGENTS.md` Conventions and `docs/design/architecture.md` §Alpha contract), that machinery is removed: an audit record written before the rename carries an op-kind the current code no longer recognizes, so it becomes non-undoable/ignored — the accepted, intended consequence. (`src/write/undo.ts`; tests dropped from `test/unit/undo-plan.test.ts`.)
- **`batch` gains temp-id chaining, per-line idempotency ids, and a batch-level undo token — "create a project, then file three to-dos into it" now works in one submission.** Three additive features ride the JSONL/MCP `batch` surface (existing per-line and summary shapes untouched):
  - **`tempId` chaining.** A line that CREATES a uuid may declare a `tempId` handle (`[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,32}`, unique per batch); a LATER line references that new uuid as `"$name"` in any ref-accepting param (`uuid`, `uuids[]`, `target`, `before`/`after`, `heading`, and the `uuid` of a `project`/`area`/`container` ref). Dotted access reaches an identity-replacement op's other discovered uuids: `"$name.instance"` (the spawned occurrence of a make-repeating) and `"$name.replaced"` (the destroyed source). The handle binds to the leg's primary result uuid once — and only once — the leg VERIFIES OK; a failed leg (including a rule-mismatch that surfaces discovery uuids in its error) binds nothing. `tempId` is valid only on a uuid-minting op (`todo.add`, `todo.add-logged`, `project.add`, `project.add-repeating`, `area.add`, `heading.add`, `todo.duplicate`, `project.duplicate`, `todo.make-repeating`, `project.make-repeating`, `todo.convert-to-project`, `heading.convert-to-project`) — a usage error on `tag.add` (tags have no uuid — reference a tag by title) and on any non-minting op. Declaration errors (bad charset, duplicate, wrong op) are pre-flight: they reject the WHOLE batch before any leg runs. `$` resolution is STRICT and fail-closed — a `$` value naming no declared tempId (`unresolved-temp-ref`), a forward reference, or a reference to a leg that bound nothing fails just that line before dispatch; independent later legs still run. (A real value beginning with `$` therefore cannot be used in a ref-accepting param — address that item by uuid.)
  - **`opId` idempotency.** A line may carry a client `opId` (`[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}`), recorded on the audit record. Before dispatch it is matched against the recent change history (last 1000 records AND last 7 days, whichever is smaller) for an `ok` record with the same id; on a hit the leg is SKIPPED and reported `already-applied` with the original uuid — which is bound to the line's `tempId` so later `$refs` still resolve. Resubmitting an ambiguously-failed batch is now safe against double-creates. (`already-applied` counts as success for `--fail-fast`/exit codes.)
  - **Batch undo token.** Every leg records under one shared transaction; the batch writes a summary audit record (`op: "batch"`) whose undo token undoes the WHOLE submission as one unit — `things undo --txn <token>` (MCP `undo` `txn`) replays each ok leg's inverse in REVERSE leg order through the existing compound-summary machinery (irreversible legs skipped with a note). A batch leg is not an independent undo target — the summary is the single undoable unit, as for every compound op.
  - **Result additions.** Each per-line outcome echoes `tempId`/`boundUuid`/`opId` when present; the CLI summary line gains `tempIdMapping` (`{name: uuid}`) and `undoToken`; the MCP `batch` result gains a second content block `{tempIdMapping, undoToken}` (the first block, the per-op array, is unchanged). `--dry-run` mints nothing, so a temp-ref line previews as `skipped` and no token is emitted. Batch options additionally accept `dangerouslyDriveGui` (MCP `dangerously_drive_gui`) so the uuid-minting ui-drive ops (make-repeating, convert-to-project) can pass their per-leg two-key gate inside a batch. See [docs/design/contracts.md](docs/design/contracts.md) (Batch chaining, idempotency, and undo). (`src/write/batch.ts`, `src/write/undo.ts`, `src/write/pipeline.ts`, `src/audit/schema.ts`, `src/client.ts`, `src/index.ts`, `src/cli/commands/writes.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`; tests in `test/engine/write-batch-temp-ids.test.ts`, `test/engine/write-batch.test.ts`, `test/mcp/server.test.ts`.)
- **BREAKING (behavior): a batch is now undone as ONE unit — its legs are no longer individually undoable and no longer carry a per-line `undoToken`.** Previously each batch op recorded as a standalone mutation: its per-line result carried its own `undoToken`, and `things undo --last N` / `--by` selected batch legs individually. Now every leg records under one shared batch transaction, so — like the legs of every other compound op — a batch leg is excluded from direct undo targeting (its per-line result no longer includes `undoToken`), and `undo --last`/`--by` never selects a leg. The batch's summary record is the single undoable unit: undo the whole submission with `things undo --txn <undoToken>` (the token from the batch summary line), which replays the legs' inverses in reverse. To reverse just part of a batch, undo the whole batch and resubmit without the unwanted lines. Pre-1.0, so no deprecation window. (`src/write/batch.ts`, `src/write/undo.ts`.)
- **A make-repeating rule-mismatch (or ambiguous discovery) now hands back the created template's uuid for cleanup instead of leaving an orphan.** When the make-repeating create-probe finds the new template but its rule does not match the request (the interval-race guard), the source item is already destroyed — so the failure verdict now derives the full discovery (template uuid, spawned instance via the template FK, and the source fate) exactly as the success path does, and surfaces it on the CLI/MCP error envelope's `detail.observed` as `repeating.templateUuid`, `repeating.instanceUuid`, and `repeating.replacedUuid` (alongside the landed `repeating.rule.*` fields); the message adds that the template WAS created and its uuid is included for cleanup (reschedule-repeat it to the intended rule, or delete it). The ambiguous-discovery refusal (multiple same-title templates, fingerprint can't disambiguate) likewise now includes `repeating.candidateTemplateUuids` in `detail.observed`. `reschedule-repeat` mismatches need none of this — reschedule is identity-preserved, so the caller already holds the uuid (noted in a code comment). (`src/write/verify/delta.ts`; tests in `test/unit/repeat-discovery.test.ts`.)
- **Container-scoped sandbox: hard-limit a whole process's Things access to ONE container (an area or a project), with a no-oracle guarantee.** `things mcp --scope <ref>` jails an MCP server to one container for its whole life; `THINGS_API_SCOPE` and a stored `scope` config key do the same for a CLI process (precedence: the MCP flag > env > stored config — the flag deliberately outranks env, since the launcher's boundary must not be agent-overridable). Under a scope: every read auto-filters to in-scope rows (`today`/`anytime`/`someday`/`upcoming`/`logbook`/`trash`/`search`/`changes`/`projects`/`areas`/`show`/`byUuid` — items outside the container are invisible, the Inbox is empty, `snapshot` refuses); every write is refused or redirected outside it (a bare `todo add` lands IN the container; moving out, detaching, restoring to the Inbox, emptying the Trash, mutating an area, renaming/deleting a tag, and — under a project scope — creating a sibling project or converting/making-repeating are refused; `tag.add` and in-container edits stay allowed). The crux is the **no-oracle guarantee**: an out-of-scope uuid/name is byte-indistinguishable from a nonexistent one — target resolution, `byUuid`, `show`, ref candidates, search, error copy, AND the undo trail all answer "not found" identically for hidden vs nonexistent, so the scope is never an existence oracle. (`things undo` filters the trail to in-scope records before any listing/selection: an out-of-scope record never surfaces, and a `--txn` token naming one fails byte-identically to an unknown token — no already-undone leak.) Enforcement lives once in the library (a scope predicate in `src/read/scope.ts`, the universal gate `evaluateScope` in the pipeline), so the CLI and MCP inherit it. Scoped reads carry an additive `meta.scope = { kind, uuid, title, source }` and a one-line "scoped to …" banner on a TTY; the MCP server instructions list only in-scope containers. A stored `scope` config key jails EVERY process on the host including the owner's terminal — `things config set scope` warns loudly and points at the env var / flag for per-process scoping, and `things doctor` reports the active ambient scope. See [docs/design/container-scope.md](docs/design/container-scope.md). (`src/read/scope.ts`, `src/write/scope-guard.ts`, `src/config.ts`, `src/client.ts`, `src/read/queries.ts`, `src/read/show-target.ts`, `src/write/pipeline.ts`, `src/write/undo.ts`, `src/contracts.ts`, `src/diagnose.ts`, `src/index.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`, `src/cli/commands/mcp.ts`, `src/cli/commands/writes.ts`, `src/cli/commands/doctor.ts`, `src/cli/read-driver.ts`, `src/cli/help.ts`; tests in `test/engine/scope.test.ts`, `test/engine/scope-undo.test.ts`, `test/mcp/scope-instructions.test.ts`, `test/cli/scope-cli.test.ts`, `test/cli/config-get-cli.test.ts`.)
- **`make-repeating` can no longer silently report success with the wrong interval; the interval field is driven closed-loop.** A follow-up to the reschedule cluster: the after-UIC7 lab run found that `make-repeating --interval N` (N>1, fixed) landed `fa=1` — the interval numeric field, when it is the first field after a frequency/type switch, races the dialog's group re-layout and reverts to 1 — yet the op reported `ok`, because its create-probe verified only that a template was born (`isTemplate`), never the landed rule. Two layers close it: (1) **verify** — the make-repeating create-probe now carries the requested `type`/`unit`/`interval` and, once the template is discovered, asserts its DECODED rule matches; a mismatch is a `verify-failed:mismatch`, never a silent `ok` (skipped only when the rule is undecodable, i.e. a future Things rule format — discovery still succeeds). Covers `todo.make-repeating`, `project.make-repeating`, and the `project.add-repeating` composite's promote leg. (2) **driver** — the numeric `set-value` primitive (`axSetValueScript`) now types, Tab-commits, then READS THE FIELD BACK and re-types on a bounded retry if it did not hold, failing closed if it never does (closed-loop, per the determinism doctrine) — so the interval lands rather than merely being caught after the fact. (`src/write/verify/delta.ts`, `src/write/commands.ts`, `src/write/vectors/ui.ts`; tests in `test/unit/repeat-discovery.test.ts`, `test/unit/ui-scripts.test.ts`.)

- **`reschedule-repeat` GUI-drive cluster fixed — a real fixed→after-completion conversion no longer reports failure while the change actually lands, and a botched conversion is now genuinely detectable.** A field report (2026-07-23) hit six defects converting a fixed biweekly repeater to after-completion through `things todo reschedule-repeat --after-completion --frequency weekly --interval 2 --dangerously-drive-gui`: the change landed, yet the CLI reported failure on both attempts and a retry with a *different* rule was stopped only by a preflight refusal — the failure mode actively invited clobbering. Fixes:
  - **(c) the after-completion cadence-unit pop-up is now plural-safe.** The drive died on `menu item "week" not found`: the app pluralizes the unit by interval (`week` at 1, `weeks` at >1), and a reschedule opens the dialog pre-populated with the item's *current* interval, so a biweekly template's unit pop-up already read `weeks`. The unit step now offers a candidate label list (singular AND plural for every unit) and clicks whichever exists — order-independent and plural-safe (`axSelectPopupCandidatesScript`, new `UiStep.valueCandidates`).
