(`src/write/vectors/ui.ts`, `src/write/vectors/ui-recipes.ts`, `src/write/vectors/types.ts`.)
  - **(a) false-negative verify → idempotency + post-abort recovery.** The reschedule delta now asserts the rule **type** (`fixed` vs `after-completion`), not just unit+interval — in the incident both rules were weekly/interval-2, so only the type flipped and verify could neither confirm the conversion nor detect a no-op. On a nonzero transport exit (a mid-drive abort) the pipeline now RE-VERIFIES with bounded backoff and, if the target rule landed, returns `ok` with a loud "the change DID land — no retry needed" warning instead of a false `verify-failed`. And before driving, an already-satisfied target state (a reschedule to the rule the template already carries) short-circuits to an `ok` no-op with no GUI drive at all. (`src/write/pipeline.ts`, `src/write/commands.ts`.)
  - **(d) sheet-cleanup honesty.** A mid-drive abort no longer claims "sheet dismissed (Escape)" without proof: after Escape the driver ASSERTS via AX that the sheet/popover is gone, retries Escape once, and if it is still present the report says so ("a sheet or popover may still be open … dismiss it manually") rather than claiming a dismissal it could not confirm. (`src/write/vectors/ui.ts` — `axSheetOpenScript`, `verifiedAbort`.)
  - **(e) preflight open-sheet diagnosis.** When the `Items ▸ Repeat` menu path fails to resolve, the driver now checks whether a modal sheet is attached to the Things window (an earlier aborted drive may have left one open, which disables the menu bar) and names THAT as the first likely cause with remediation, instead of the generic Things-update / Accessibility / non-English guesses. (`src/write/vectors/ui.ts`.)
  - Tests: `test/engine/write-ui-vector.test.ts` (idempotency no-op, post-abort recovery, type-conversion verifiability, verified-abort honesty, open-sheet diagnosis), `test/unit/repeat-recipe.test.ts` (plural-safe unit candidates), `test/unit/ui-scripts.test.ts` (candidate/sheet-probe script shapes).

- **`things config get [key]` reads configuration back — one key's effective value, or all keys with their stored-vs-default provenance.** Until now the only config verbs were `config show` (a flat dump of the effective config) and `config set`; there was no way to read a single key or to see which keys are still at a built-in default. `things config get <key>` prints one key's effective value (`profile`, `maxDisruption`, `actor`, `auditEnabled`, `accepted-fingerprint`, `allow-experimental`, `ui-enabled` — the same spellings `config set` accepts); a bare `things config get` lists every effective value — the settable keys plus derived read-only ones (`host`, and the profile-derived `maxDisruption` default) — each marked with its provenance. Human output marks a defaulted key with a dim `(default)`, an env-overridden key with a dim `(env)`, and a derived read-only key with a dim `(derived)` (stored keys render bare); `--json` emits a versioned `config` envelope — `{ key, value, source }` for one key, an array of those for all keys, where `source` is `"stored" | "env" | "default" | "derived"`. An unknown key is a usage error (exit 2), matching `config set`. (`src/config.ts`, `src/index.ts`, `src/cli/commands/writes.ts`, `src/cli/help.ts`; tests in `test/cli/config-get-cli.test.ts`.)
- **BREAKING: `things config show` is removed; use `things config get` (no key) instead.** `config show` was a flat dump of the effective config; a bare `things config get` is now a strict superset — it lists every effective value, including the derived read-only ones `show` printed, each carrying a provenance marker. Pre-1.0, so no deprecation window. (`src/cli/commands/writes.ts`, `src/cli/resolve-invocation.ts`, `src/cli/help.ts`.)
- **BREAKING (behavior): boolean environment overrides are now bidirectional and precedence is documented as `env > stored > default`.** Previously `THINGS_API_ALLOW_EXPERIMENTAL` and `THINGS_API_UI_ENABLED` only honored `"true"` and `THINGS_API_AUDIT` only honored `"off"`, so an env value could turn a vector ON but never force it back OFF against a stored `true`. Now a recognized env value always wins over stored config: `THINGS_API_ALLOW_EXPERIMENTAL` and `THINGS_API_UI_ENABLED` accept `true`/`false`; `THINGS_API_AUDIT` accepts `on`/`off` (legacy `off` still works); `THINGS_API_PROFILE` now accepts `workstation` as well as `dedicated-server`. An unset or unrecognized value falls through to stored config, then the built-in default, exactly as before. `things config get` provenance labels reflect the layer that actually supplied the effective value under these rules. (`src/config.ts`; tests in `test/unit/config.test.ts`, `test/cli/config-get-cli.test.ts`.)
- **Single-item write mutations now echo the mutated item's `title` in their ok result (additive).** `things todo cancel <uuid> --json` and `things todo complete <uuid> --json` (and the whole single-item family — every op that resolves one pre-existing uuid target: complete/cancel/reopen, update, move, set-tags/add-tags, delete/restore, duplicate, clear-reminder, backdate, the checklist ops, reschedule-repeat/pause-repeat/resume-repeat, and the convert/make-repeating transforms) now carry `title` on the `ok` result — the item's PRE-write title, captured before the change. A batch log can confirm WHAT was mutated without a second read. Strictly additive: the envelope shape is otherwise unchanged, and create/reorder ops (which have no single pre-existing target) omit it. The MCP surface inherits it through its verbatim `data` passthrough. (`src/write/pipeline.ts`; tests in `test/engine/write-simulator.test.ts`.)
- **Regression coverage locks the `--json` contract on the verify-failed write path (including the GUI-drove-then-verify-failed case).** A field report (2026-07-23) described `things todo reschedule-repeat … --dangerously-drive-gui --json` printing a plain-text `VERIFY FAILED (silent-noop): …` banner to stdout and breaking `jq`. Investigation found the current output layer already correct — both the transport-refused and the drove-then-verify-failed paths return the same `verify-failed` result kind, and the CLI emits exactly one JSON error envelope on stdout under `--json` (the plain-text banner is the non-`--json` rendering and goes to stderr only). New tests pin that contract at the CLI boundary and confirm the exit-0 "drove cleanly but the app did nothing" path yields the structured `verify-failed:silent-noop` result the formatter JSON-encodes, so a future change cannot let the banner leak onto stdout. (Tests in `test/cli/verify-failed-json-cli.test.ts`, `test/engine/write-ui-vector.test.ts`.)
- **A new `--area <ref>` filter scopes the `today`, `anytime`, `someday`, and `upcoming` views to one area, and `logbook` gains the annotation to match (it already scoped by `--area`).** The ref is a uuid or a unique area name, resolved by the SAME resolver `things areas <ref>` uses; an unresolvable or ambiguous ref fails closed as a usage error (exit 2) carrying machine-readable candidates, exactly like every other ref argument. Semantics are a pure POST-FILTER on the already-computed view — membership and ordering are untouched — keeping a row when its own area is the target OR its containing project (including via a heading) belongs to the target area; project rows survive when the project's own area is the target. Everything else drops: other areas, area-less loose items, area-less projects and their children, Inbox rows, and repeating-template rows follow the same rule via their own area/project. `--limit` and the grouped per-block caps apply AFTER the filter. Under `--json` an active filter adds an ADDITIVE `meta.filter = { area: { uuid, title } }` annotation (the `meta.clock` precedent — present only when active; no existing field changes). The MCP `read_view` tool gains the matching optional `area` param on the same five views (rejected on inbox/trash), surfacing the same `meta.filter` block. The filtering lives once in the library layer (`src/read/area-filter.ts`), applied uniformly by the client across the flat, Today-split, and grouped-sidebar shapes — the CLI and MCP surfaces only pass the ref. (`src/read/area-filter.ts`, `src/client.ts`, `src/contracts.ts`, `src/surface-copy.ts`, `src/cli/commands/reads.ts`, `src/cli/read-driver.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`, `src/index.ts`; tests in `test/unit/area-filter.test.ts`, `test/cli/view-area-filter.test.ts`, `test/mcp/server.test.ts`.)
- **`install-skill` and the skill-drift ratchet now key off the RUNNING BINARY's version rather than the bundled `SKILL.md` placeholder, so the ratchet works in a dev checkout and never mis-stamps a copy.** The repo's `skills/things-cli/SKILL.md` deliberately stays `0.0.0-dev` (stamped only at publish, keeping builds git-quiet) — but comparing and copying that placeholder was meaningless in a source checkout, and a dev-installed copy carried `0.0.0-dev` forever, which would later trip the drift notice under any published binary. Now: (1) `things install-skill` stamps the copied `SKILL.md` with this binary's version at install time by staging a stamped temp copy and handing THAT to the installer (so every location the `skills` CLI materializes — including agent dirs we don't enumerate — inherits the stamp); the repo file is never modified. In a published install the outcome is identical to before (the tarball is already stamped to the same version the binary reports); in a dev checkout the installed copy now reads e.g. `0.11.0-dev` instead of `0.0.0-dev`. (2) `install-skill --check` compares installed stamps against this binary's version and labels the report line `binary version:` (was `bundled skill version:`); the `--json` envelope field is renamed `binaryVersion`. (3) The passive drift notice compares the installed stamp against the binary version too, strips any `-dev` suffix before the minor-version comparison (so a dev binary does not nag when versions match), and reads a pre-fix `0.0.0-dev` copy as legacy/unstamped — one `run 'things install-skill' to refresh` line — rather than feeding it to drift math that would call it eleven minor versions behind. (`src/cli/commands/install-skill.ts`, `src/cli/skill-check.ts`, `src/cli/skill.ts`, `src/cli/main.ts`; tests in `test/cli/install-skill.test.ts`, `test/cli/skill-drift.test.ts`.)
- **Lab (SIMFID): the clone leg ran for the first time, producing a fresh app-side capture of the six headless CRUD cases — and it caught a finding + a harness bug.** `bash lab/scripts/simfid.sh` (run `simfid-20260722-200229`) drove the `clone-manifest.json` CRUD cases through the guest CLI against the real Things app in a disposable Tart clone once the tart volume had ≥ 12 GB headroom; those six rows are now fresh `clone-drive` captures instead of banked `suite-evidence`, moving the standing table to 29 MATCH · 6 TOLERATED · 0 DIVERGENT (`docs/lab/simfid-results.md`). Five corroborate the model exactly; the sixth is a reported finding — `todo-reopen` moved MATCH → TOLERATED(wallclock-bucket) because the SIMFID sim-side fixture seeds a `completed` to-do with `stopDate=null` (nothing for reopen to clear) while the real app stamps `stopDate` on completion and clears it on reopen, a fixture-fidelity gap masked by an over-broad tolerance (not new app behaviour, not a proven `simulator.ts` applier bug; fixture/tolerance fixes deferred). Running the leg also required fixing `simfid.sh`: it shipped the host's homebrew `node`, which links ~20 dylibs under `/opt/homebrew/opt/*` that do not exist on the vanilla guest (every guest CLI call aborted with SIGABRT), so the script now guarantees a self-contained guest node — the on-PATH node if `otool` shows it links only system libs, else a pinned official `node v24.18.0 darwin-arm64` fetched into a gitignored `lab/cache/`. Lab-only — no consumer-facing surface changes. (`lab/scripts/simfid.sh`, `.gitignore`; `docs/lab/simfid-results.md`, `docs/lab/probe-backlog.md`.)
- **Lab (SIMFID): resolved the `todo-reopen` clone-leg finding — reopen fixture now seeds a real `stopDate` and `wallclock-bucket` is presence-aware.** The reopen case seeded a `completed` to-do with `stopDate=null` (nothing for the applier to clear), so the sim delta never exhibited the `stopDate → null` transition the real app always makes, and the over-broad `wallclock-bucket` tolerance absorbed the present-vs-`null` difference as TOLERATED. The fixture now seeds a completion `stopDate` on the pinned RSIM day, and `wallclock-bucket` tolerates a bucket difference only when BOTH sides carry a timestamp — a present-vs-absent stop/creation date now reads DIVERGENT. The `simulator.ts` reopen applier was already correct (it clears `stopDate → null`); the fixture simply never exercised that path. `todo-reopen` returns to an honest MATCH (host-side re-run 30 MATCH · 5 TOLERATED · 0 DIVERGENT); both tolerance directions unit-tested. Lab-only — no consumer-facing surface changes. (`lab/simfid/cases.ts`, `lab/simfid/tolerances.ts`; tests in `test/unit/simfid-normalize.test.ts`; `docs/lab/simfid-results.md`.)
- **AGENTBENCH (simulator fidelity): the fixed make-repeating source-fate law is now complete — deadline to-dos and all-terminal-children projects PRESERVE their source.** `applyMakeRepeatingFixed` gains the two probe-isolated preserve triggers: a fixed `todo make-repeating` whose source carries a `deadline` relinks the source in place as the instance and mints only the template (§RSIM-T — deadline is the sole trigger; bare/notes/tag/checklist still delete), and a fixed `project make-repeating` now preserves when its subtree has a nested repeater OR every child is terminal/no open child (§RSIM-U — the second trigger, joining the existing nested-repeater one; the shared preserve helper leaves the source's children riding along unchanged, `childrenReplaced=0`). The response layer derives the fate generically from the FK, so `repeating.replacedUuid` reads `null` on both new preserve branches with no response-code change. Two new SIMFID cases (`todo-make-repeating-deadline-preserve`, `project-make-repeating-terminal-children-preserve`) both MATCH (`docs/lab/simfid-results.md`, now 35 cases → 30 MATCH · 5 TOLERATED · 0 DIVERGENT). Bench/lab-only: no consumer-facing surface changes. (`src/write/vectors/simulator.ts`, `lab/simfid/cases.ts`; tests in `test/engine/write-simulator.test.ts`.)
- **AGENTBENCH: the refinement loop gains a `--mission <default|friction>` mode that fences a compression round off the ladder's success-first pull.** A `friction` round injects a MISSION FENCE into the refiner charter (state the mission = reduce friction + SKILL.md/context size; FORBID patches whose rationale is fixing a task-success failure — "success failures are context, not targets; the ladder protects success automatically"; require every edit net-negative/neutral unless it repositions an existing fact) and enforces the byte rule in CODE: on a friction round `runIteration` measures the arm's total surface bytes before/after apply (post-fmt) and rejects+reverts any candidate that grows the surface past `SKILL_BYTE_GROWTH_LIMIT` (+80 B) BEFORE spending re-bench tokens, alongside the existing fmt/check gate. Default rounds are unaffected. Motivated by the loop-skill-v2r lesson (a mission-scoped round must forbid success-chasing rather than let the success-first ranking pull toward additive patches). Bench-only: no consumer-facing surface changes. (`bench/loop.ts`, `bench/loop-core.ts`; unit tests in `test/unit/bench-loop.test.ts`.)
- **Lab (SIMFID): the simulator-fidelity replay suite lands, certifying `src/write/vectors/simulator.ts` against the real app op by op** (`docs/lab/simfid-results.md`, `lab/simfid/**`, `lab/scripts/simfid.sh`, `npm run lab:simfid`). For every simulator-covered op it compares the op replayed on the host through the full write pipeline with the simulator vector against the same op driven through the guest CLI against the real Things app in a disposable Tart clone, after NORMALIZING away non-fidelity differences (uuids → placeholders keyed by (kind, title, container-aware discovery order); wall-clock timestamps → date buckets; list indexes → ranks) and applying declared TOLERANCES for the probe-proven app nondeterminism (the nondeterministic per-child instance→template `rt1_repeatingTemplate` stamping — RSIM-R C2 / RSIM-S — and the junk instance next-date sentinel). Verdict per op is MATCH / TOLERATED(&lt;which&gt;) / DIVERGENT(&lt;detail&gt;); a divergence is reported (simulator bug OR newly-discovered app behaviour), never silently folded into the appliers. The normalizer + comparator are host-side and unit-tested (`test/unit/simfid-normalize.test.ts`); wired into the drift runbook step 5 (re-certify after every golden rebuild). First certification run over 33 cases across the 21 covered ops: 28 MATCH · 5 TOLERATED · 0 DIVERGENT (app-side ground truth from banked RSIM/suite probe evidence this run; the fresh-clone leg `lab/scripts/simfid.sh` is authored and ready, deferred on tart-volume headroom). Lab-only — no consumer-facing surface changes.

## 0.11.0 — 2026-07-21

- **The agent skill and the CLI now keep each other up to date — a passive version ratchet, no agent version math.** Published tarballs carry a real `version:` stamp in `skills/things-cli/SKILL.md` (written at publish time from `package.json` by `scripts/stamp-skill.mjs`, wired into `prepublishOnly` and an explicit release-workflow step); the repo copy stays `0.0.0-dev`, and `npm run build` never stamps, so `git status` is quiet after a build. `things install-skill` copies whatever stamp the bundled skill carries. On human/`--help` output paths only — never inside a `--json` envelope, never the `mcp` server — the binary reads the installed skill (canonical `~/.agents/skills/things-cli/` first, then `~/.claude/…`; symlinks resolve) and, when the installed skill is a MINOR version or more behind the skill this binary bundles, prints one stderr line: `note: installed agent skill vX predates bundled vY — run 'things install-skill' to update`. It runs at most once per process, is silent on any error/absence, ignores patch-level drift, and is disabled by `THINGS_API_NO_SKILL_CHECK=1` (the skill-directory read is disclosed in the README and `things help agent`). The skill's own preamble handles the reverse direction: if `things --version` reports below 0.11.0 (the release that ships these features) it tells the agent to prefer `npx -y things-api@latest`, which always pairs current commands with current help. (`src/cli/skill-check.ts`, `src/cli/skill.ts`, `src/cli/main.ts`, `src/cli/help.ts`, `scripts/stamp-skill.mjs`, `package.json`, `.github/workflows/release.yml`, `skills/things-cli/SKILL.md`; tests in `test/cli/skill-drift.test.ts`.)

- **New `things install-skill` installs the bundled agent skill (`skills/things-cli/`), and the skill now ships in the npm package.** An agent skill — the compact, version-tolerant orientation an agent loads once (data model, item references, the stable machine contracts, pointing at `things --help`/`things help <topic>` for mechanics) — is now shipped in the tarball (`skills/` added to the package `files` list) and installable with one command. `things install-skill` resolves the skill from the running package's own location (works under npm/pnpm/bun/npx with no PATH heuristics) and hands it to the `skills` CLI (`npx -y skills add … -g -y`), which keeps one canonical copy under `~/.agents/skills/` and materializes it into every detected agent directory; when `npx`/the network/the tool is unavailable it falls back to a built-in plain copy into `~/.agents` and `~/.claude` (the report says which path was taken). `--project` installs into the current project's `.agents` instead of globally; `--check` compares the installed version stamp against the bundled one per location and writes nothing (exit 0 up to date, 7 absent/behind). Re-running the command IS the update — the skill directory is replaced wholesale (verified: a re-add cleanly overwrites, no prior `remove` needed), so it supersedes a generic `skills update` for this skill. The top-level `things --help` gained a one-line footer suggesting it, and the README documents the promise (install the skill, the rest self-maintains) plus the zero-npm `npx skills add mikegreiling/things-api` alternate. (`src/cli/commands/install-skill.ts`, `src/cli/skill.ts`, `src/cli/main.ts`, `src/cli/help.ts`, `package.json`, `README.md`; tests in `test/cli/install-skill.test.ts`.)

- **A namespaced `move` carrying scheduling intent now fails with a hint pointing at the command that actually schedules an item, instead of a bare unknown-option / excess-argument usage error.** `things todo move <ref>` and `things project move <ref>` change an item's CONTAINER (`--area`/`--project`/`--heading`; to-do also `--inbox`/`--detach`) — they never schedule. An agent that means "move X to Someday" reaches for spellings `move` does not accept (`--to someday`, `--when today`, `--someday`/`--today`/`--evening`/`--anytime`, `--date`/`--schedule <d>`, or a bare `someday`/`today`/`evening`/`anytime`/`YYYY-MM-DD` positional) and used to land on a usage error that never named `update --when`. These are now recognized BEFORE commander parses and answered with the concrete working command: a scheduling term → `things <group> update <ref> --when <value>`; a bare `inbox` positional on `todo move` → `things todo move <ref> --inbox` (the real Inbox-return — `--when inbox` is not a thing). `--inbox` and `--detach` stay genuine `move` flags and are never trapped; a value flag whose value merely looks like a term (`--heading someday`, an area literally named `today`) is never trapped; a `project move … inbox` is left to commander (a project has no Inbox). What `move` accepts is unchanged; the exit class stays Usage (2), and under `--json` the suggestion rides `error.details.suggestions`. (`src/cli/move-hint.ts`, `src/cli/main.ts`; tests in `test/cli/move-hint.test.ts`.)
- **The agent skill (`skills/things-cli/`) is restructured as skill-v2, splitting slow-moving truth from fast-moving mechanics.** The skill now carries only what is stable across binary versions — the data model, how to refer to items, and the stable contracts (JSON envelope, exit codes, dry-run/undo, the disruptive gate, and the recurrence contract) — and defers verbs, flags, and per-operation details to `things <group> --help` and the `things help <topic>` guides. A new preamble tells the agent to invoke `things` when it is on PATH and otherwise substitute `npx -y things-api@latest`, so the skill stays useful when the binary is newer than it. The six reference files fold into three (`references/model.md`, `references/contracts.md`, `references/gui.md` unchanged); `data-model.md`, `reads.md`, `writes.md`, `recurrence.md`, and `safety-and-recovery.md` are removed. Frontmatter `name`/`description` are unchanged. No accepted-candidate content was dropped (the `.data`/`.uuid` envelope, the recurrence preconditions and multi-weekday-in-one-rule, and the outside-in add-then-convert chain all survive). (`skills/things-cli/**`.)

- **CLI help copy is tightened across the group and subcommand `--help` surfaces, with contract detail relocated to the `things help <topic>` guides.** Copy-only (no behavior, flag, or default changes): the shared tag-filter flags (`--tag`/`--untagged`, which repeat on every read view) drop their inline inheritance/descendant paragraph and point at `things help filters`, which already carries the full model; the repeating verbs (`todo`/`project make-repeating`, `reschedule-repeat`) drop the rule-shaping restatement that duplicated their own flag list and `things help repeating`, pointing there instead; `todo checklist` stops re-listing the granular flags it already documents below; `project show`/`area show` drop the tag-inheritance clause already carried by their after-help footer; `todo clear-reminder` and `area reorder` drop delivery-mechanism prose for behavior-only copy per `docs/design/surface-copy.md`; the four `open` commands, `--create-tags`, and `--dangerously-drive-gui` shed redundant wording. No accepted-candidate content was removed (the top-level `--json` envelope/`.uuid` signpost and the `things help repeating` contract are untouched). A typical read view (`things inbox --help`) drops ~19% of its bytes; the five write groups together shed ~1.4k chars (~360 tokens). (`src/cli/tag-filters.ts`, `src/cli/commands/writes.ts`, `src/cli/commands/todo.ts`, `src/cli/commands/project.ts`, `src/cli/commands/area.ts`, `src/cli/commands/show.ts`.)
- **`things --version` (and the version line in `things --help`) now prints `<version>-dev` when the CLI is running from a source checkout, and the plain `<version>` in published installs.** `bin/things.js` loads live TS source (`src/cli/main.ts`) in a dev checkout (npm link / `node bin/things.js` in the repo) and the built `dist/cli/main.js` in a published tarball, so inside the CLI modules `import.meta.url` ends in `.ts` for source and `.js` for a build — the signal a new intra-surface helper (`src/cli/version.ts`) uses to append the `-dev` marker. Published installs are entirely unaffected: the suffix only ever appears when live TS source is running. (`src/cli/version.ts`, `src/cli/main.ts`, `src/cli/help.ts`; test in `test/cli/version.test.ts`.)
- **`todo restore` now fails fast on a trashed child of a repeating-template project, instead of surfacing a raw AppleScript error.** Our restore is a move-to-Inbox, and the Things app refuses to move a template-side child out to a built-in list (error 301) — so restoring a trashed to-do whose container project is a repeating template previously reached the app and came back as an opaque `Cannot move to-do (301)` no-op. A new pre-write guard (`H-TEMPLATE-CHILD-RESTORE`) detects the template container (the target's `project`/`headingProject` ref carrying `rt1_recurrenceRule`/`repeater`) and blocks before any write with a clean refusal: the to-do belongs to a repeating template, the app does not support restoring it, and to get the content back you recreate it inside the template (adds and title/notes edits do land there). Ordinary trashed to-dos are unaffected. The CLI (`things todo restore`) and MCP inherit it from the op layer. Evidence: `docs/lab/rsim-results.md` §RSIM-S (S-R3), oddities §8n. (`src/write/guards.ts`, `src/write/commands.ts`; tests in `test/unit/write-guards.test.ts`.)
- **AGENTBENCH (simulator fidelity): a fixed source-DELETE conversion now hard-deletes already-trashed source children too, matching reality.** The subtree helpers behind `project.make-repeating` (`readProjectSubtree` / `deleteProjectSubtree`) previously filtered `trashed = 0`, so a child trashed before the conversion was left as a dangling row pointing at the now-deleted source project — but the app hard-deletes the WHOLE source subtree, trashed rows included (RSIM-S §S-R1). The read now includes trashed rows (tagged): DELETION destroys them along with the live subtree (no orphan, no dangling Trash row), while COPYING still excludes them (spawned/template copies never contain trashed children, RSIM-P2 A3). Bench-only: no consumer-facing surface changes. (`src/write/vectors/simulator.ts`; regression test in `test/engine/write-simulator.test.ts`.)
- **Read surfaces now mark a to-do/heading whose container PROJECT is a repeating template with the ↻ repeat glyph.** Everywhere the CLI renders the muted `(container-title)` next to a to-do or heading — list rows, search results, logbook/trash rows, every `formatItem` site — the container title is now prefixed `(↻ Title)` when that container project is a repeating template (its `rt1_recurrenceRule`/`repeater` is non-null; the children themselves are plain rows). `things show`/`todo show` detail likewise renders the ↻ in front of the project title on both the to-do card and the heading card. This is the disambiguator for the two identical-looking same-title copies a project conversion leaves behind — the template blueprint (all future occurrences) vs. this occurrence — since a template project is hidden from views but its plain children still surface in search and title-ref resolution. The `--json` read output carries the fact as `isRepeatingTemplate: true` on the container ref (`project` / `headingProject`) wherever that ref appears (search results, show detail), omitted when false. Templates only — there is no instance-side marker, and view membership/filtering is unchanged. Reuses the same ↻ as the `[↻]`/`(↻)` template checkboxes; documented in `things legend`. (`src/model/entities.ts`, `src/read/queries.ts`, `src/cli/glyphs.ts`, `src/cli/render.ts`, `src/cli/commands/todo.ts`; tests in `test/unit/views.test.ts` + `test/unit/render-styling.test.ts`.)
- **`todo add` and `todo move` now reject `--project` together with `--area`, and `--heading` without a `--project`, instead of silently preferring the project.** Passing both a project and an area was accepted with the project silently winning (and the post-write check then expected BOTH links); a `--heading` with no project was silently dropped. Both are now rejected up front with a clear usage error (`project and area are exclusive destinations` / `heading requires a project destination`). The guard lives in the shared write layer, so the CLI (`things todo add`/`things todo move`), the MCP tools (`add_todo`/`move_todo`), and `batch` (as a per-op param error) all inherit it. The MCP `move_todo` description no longer says "a project and/or area". (`src/write/commands.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`; tests in `test/engine/write-r9b.test.ts`.)
- **make-repeating conversions now return a `repeating` block with the new uuids, and post-write discovery is hardened.** `todo.make-repeating`, `project.make-repeating`, and `project.create-repeating` (via its promote leg) now enrich the ok result with `repeating: { templateUuid, instanceUuid, replacedUuid }` (projects add `childrenReplaced` = how many pre-read child uuids are now dead — the full subtree in the delete-remint fate, just the flattened nested-template row in the nested-repeater preserve fate). `instanceUuid` is the visible current occurrence — derived exactly via the `rt1_repeatingTemplate` FK, never a title/time heuristic; `templateUuid` is the recurring rule (also the result `uuid`, unchanged); `replacedUuid` is the original when the source was destroyed, and `null` when the source was preserved AND relinked as the instance (then `instanceUuid` === the original uuid) — covering both probed fates (RSIM-R: source is EITHER absent OR relinked-as-instance). The MCP surface inherits the block through its verbatim `data` passthrough. Discovery is hardened per the RSIM-R deliverable-3 constraints: the template `creationDate` time-bound is restored even when `excludeUuids` is present (a minted template always carries a fresh write-time creationDate, so it is safe and tightens the same-title gauntlet — the backdated `add-logged` path still drops the bound), and when more than one same-title template survives the exclusion + `isTemplate` filters, discovery disambiguates by the source fingerprint (notes / tags / container / checklist — NOT deadline, which is asymmetric between template and instance) and fails LOUDLY rather than guessing when still ambiguous. Because these ops are irreversible, the ok result no longer carries an `undoToken` (`undo --txn` could only refuse it). Help copy (`things help repeating`, the `things-cli` skill recurrence reference, the MCP `repeat` tool) now points at the returned `instanceUuid`/`templateUuid` instead of re-finding the item by title. (`src/write/verify/delta.ts`, `src/write/verify/poller.ts`, `src/write/pipeline.ts`, `src/write/commands.ts`, `src/write/pre-state.ts`; tests in `test/unit/repeat-discovery.test.ts` + `test/engine/write-repeat-response.test.ts`.)
- **AGENTBENCH (simulator fidelity): the project-recurrence appliers now follow the reconciled RSIM-R laws.** `applyMakeRepeatingFixed` deletes-by-default but PRESERVES the source project (relinking it as the current-occurrence instance and flattening the nested repeater in place — nested template hard-deleted, nested instance demoted to plain, only the template project minted) when its subtree contains a nested repeater; `applyMakeRepeatingAfterCompletionProject` drops the non-reproducible per-child instance→template links (instance-side children stay plain). The to-do fixed-preserve anomaly stays unmodeled. Bench-only: no consumer-facing surface changes. See `docs/lab/rsim-results.md` §RSIM-R. (`src/write/vectors/simulator.ts`; regression tests in `test/engine/write-simulator.test.ts`.)
- **Lab (RSIM-R): reconciled the two RSIM-P2 contradictions — fixed-mode source fate and after-completion child links** (`docs/lab/rsim-results.md` §RSIM-R, `lab/scripts/research-rsim-r.sh` + `research-rsim-r2.sh`). Both were REAL app-behavior differences (re-derived uuid-by-uuid from the prior campaigns' raw snapshots — not differ bugs; same Things 3.22.11 + byte-identical ui-recipe — not a build regression), each now resolved to one reproducible law. **Source fate:** RSIM-P2's proposed area/scheduling axis is **falsified** — every bare item deletes regardless of area or a today schedule. The real axis for a **project** is whether its subtree contains a **nested repeating to-do**: a fixed `project make-repeating` preserves the source (relinking it as the current-occurrence instance and flattening the nested repeater, minting only the template) IFF a nested repeater is present, else deletes the source and mints template + instance fresh (area/When irrelevant). A **to-do** deletes when bare or checklist-only; a content-rich to-do may preserve on a deadline-led, not-yet-isolated trigger. **After-completion child links:** RSIM-P P4's per-child instance→template links are a non-reproducible anomaly — the reproducible behavior leaves all instance-side children (to-dos and headings) plain. **Specifies two simulator corrections** (not applied here): `applyMakeRepeatingFixed` must delete-by-default and preserve-source only for a project-with-nested-repeater (subsuming the RSIM-P2 flatten divergence), and `applyMakeRepeatingAfterCompletionProject` must drop the per-child links. The discovery invariant "source absent OR relinked-as-instance" holds in every case and the per-template `creationDate` time-bound survives. In-place corrections annotated in §RSIM (identity asymmetry), §RSIM-P (P4), and §RSIM-P2; new `docs/things-app-oddities.md` §8g addendum. Docs/lab only — no consumer-facing surface changes.
- **Lab (RSIM-P2): characterized nested repeaters + uuid-discovery adversarial traps** (`docs/lab/rsim-results.md` §RSIM-P2, `lab/scripts/research-rsim-p2.sh`). Converting a project whose subtree contains a repeating to-do **flattens** the nested repeater (nested template hard-deleted, instance demoted to plain, both survive only as plain copies — no template-of-template); edge-state children transform (completed→copied-but-reopened, trashed→skipped, external-repeating-instance→copied-plain-with-FK-cleared); an after-completion project with a heading leaves ALL instance-side children (headings and to-dos) plain (only the project row carries the instance→template link). uuid-discovery binds the new template correctly through a same-title gauntlet, derives the instance robustly via the `rt1_repeatingTemplate` FK, and must NOT assume the source is deleted. **Two systematic contradictions of prior RSIM/RSIM-P law are flagged for SIMFID (not folded in): the source is PRESERVED-as-instance for fixed conversions, and after-completion instance-side children carry no per-child FK link.** New app-quirk notes in `docs/things-app-oddities.md` §8m. Docs/lab only — no consumer-facing surface changes.
- **Fixed (AGENTBENCH): the refinement loop treats a runner budget-cap (exit 8) as a clean abort instead of crashing.** When the loop sizes the runner's `--max-total-tokens` cap to its remaining token budget and that cap trips mid-sweep, the runner exits 8 by design — but `benchSplit` re-raised it as a plain `Error` ("bench subprocess failed … exit=8"), an unhandled throw that crashed the loop past its finalization (state, ledger append, checkpoint). Observed in batch `loop-skill-r3`, where the 33-task corpus outgrew the 12M default budget: the batch ended in a crash with no checkpoint and ledger entries had to be reconstructed from `loop-state.json`. A subprocess exit 8 now surfaces as the SAME clean, resumable token-budget `LoopAbort` (exit 8) the loop's own budget gate throws — reverting any unapplied patch, writing ledger entries for the completed candidates, and writing `checkpoint.md` with the holdout noted SKIPPED, then exiting 8 with the resume message. Other nonzero exits still raise as hard errors, and a provider-error exit still feeds the circuit breaker. In the same change the loop prints a startup cost projection after the baseline sweep — pricing the planned iterations at the observed per-run median (`task count × reps`, baseline + up to max-iterations × 2 sweeps) — and WARNS loudly when `--token-budget` is below 1.5× the projected 2-iteration cost. The default budget is deliberately left unchanged; the warning only advises raising it. (`bench/loop.ts`, `bench/loop-core.ts`; regression tests in `test/unit/bench-loop.test.ts`.)
- **AGENTBENCH: the fenced simulator write vector now covers the recurrence ops**, applying the RSIM-characterized row shapes (`docs/lab/rsim-results.md`) directly to the synthetic fixture DB so a repeat-heavy bench task family runs end-to-end with no Things app. Covered: `todo`/`project` make-repeating in BOTH modes (fixed replaces the source with a hidden template + exactly one instance; after-completion preserves the source relinked as the sole instance under a `tp=1` template), `todo`/`project` reschedule-repeat (in-place rule replacement, identity preserved), the orchestrated `project.create-repeating`, and completion-side scheduling (completing an after-completion instance stamps its template's reference + next-start dates without materializing the next instance). Rule blobs come from a shared, decoder-validated composer (`src/write/recurrence-rule-blob.ts`, also now used by the bench world profile). `pause`/`resume`/`stop-repeat` stay out of the simulator matrix — no RSIM-proven delta, and unsupported beats guessed. Bench-only: no consumer-facing surface changes. (`src/write/vectors/simulator.ts`, `src/write/planner.ts`.)
- **AGENTBENCH (simulator fidelity): project `make-repeating` now reproduces the probed child-subtree semantics (RSIM-P).** The fixed-mode project applier hard-deletes the source subtree and mints two independent plain copies (one under the hidden template, one under the instance) instead of orphaning the children; the after-completion project applier splits from the to-do path (RSIM2) to delete-and-duplicate like fixed mode, with each instance-side to-do child carrying a per-child `rt1_repeatingTemplate` link to its template sibling; and `project.complete` cascades completion to heading rows and promotes an instance project's own `start` 2→1. The to-do make-repeating appliers (RSIM1/RSIM2) are unchanged. See `docs/lab/rsim-results.md` §RSIM-P for the row-level evidence. (`src/write/vectors/simulator.ts`; regression tests in `test/engine/write-simulator.test.ts`.)
- **BREAKING (MCP): the tool catalog is consolidated into verb-parameterized tools** — usage-evidence-backed (Mike-approved 2026-07-18). A mining pass over 350 bench MCP runs (852 tool calls) found 30 of 48 tools were never invoked and 61% of the serialized catalog paid for unused surface; the recurrence cluster alone was 24% of the catalog at zero calls. Twenty-two singletons collapse into five discriminator-parameterized tools (`repeat`, `update`, `heading`, `delete`, `set_status`) plus the existing `reorder` absorbing the sidebar-area reorder, with **zero loss of functionality** — every operation reachable before stays reachable behind a `scope`/`kind`/`action` discriminator, whose per-variant requirements are validated at call time and return the same structured `{code:"usage"}` error naming what is missing. The never-called tier-3 escape hatches (`run_operation`, `batch`, `undo`, `changes_since`, `capabilities`, `doctor`) are deliberately unchanged, and every remaining tool keeps its name and shape. Measured static init cost (server instructions + serialized catalog, `chars/4`, same fixture and method as `bench/arms.ts`) drops from **16,680 → 12,594 tokens** (catalog **16,156 → 12,070**, −4,086 / −25%). Migration:

  | Removed tool(s) | New tool | Discriminator |
  |---|---|---|
  | `make_repeating`, `reschedule_repeat`, `set_repeat_state`, `make_project_repeating`, `reschedule_project_repeat`, `set_project_repeat_state`, `create_repeating_project` | `repeat` | `scope` (`todo`\|`project`) + `action` (`start`\|`reschedule`\|`pause`\|`resume`\|`create`) |
  | `update_todo`, `update_project`, `update_area`, `update_tag` | `update` | `kind` (`todo`\|`project`\|`area`\|`tag`) |
  | `create_heading`, `rename_heading`, `archive_heading`, `unarchive_heading`, `convert_to_project` | `heading` | `action` (`create`\|`rename`\|`archive`\|`unarchive`\|`convert_to_project`) |
  | `delete_item`, `delete_area`, `delete_tag` | `delete` | `kind` (`item`\|`area`\|`tag`) |
  | `set_todo_status`, `set_project_status` | `set_status` | `scope` (`todo`\|`project`) |
  | `reorder_area` | `reorder` | `scope=areas` |

  Argument notes: the area/tag write target is now the shared `uuid` arg (was `target`) on `update`/`delete`; `repeat`'s `create` action names the project's due date `project_deadline` (the repeat rule keeps its own boolean `deadline`); the old `set_repeat_state`/`set_project_repeat_state` `state` values fold into `repeat`'s `action` as `pause`/`resume`. The GUI-driven variants (`repeat`, `heading`'s `convert_to_project`, `reorder` `scope=areas`) still require `dangerously_drive_gui`. (`src/mcp/server.ts`.)
- **Bench: the sandbox now runs commands strictly one at a time, so parallel tool calls can't clobber each other.** One sandbox is one shell over one fixture DB, but when the model emits several tool calls in a single turn the agent driver dispatches them concurrently — and two overlapping `things` children then raced against the shared DB. For a checklist edit (Things has no item-level surface, so every change reads the whole list and rewrites it wholesale) that was a lost update: both invocations read the same pre-state, each rewrote from it, and the last writer silently clobbered the other while BOTH reported a verified success — the worst failure class, and purely a bench artifact (a real terminal never overlaps command lines). `createSandbox` now serializes every `exec` through a tail promise. Bench-caught (`longtail-checklist-edit`): a `--check` and an `--add` emitted in one turn dropped one edit about a third of the time. (`bench/sandbox.ts`.)
- **CLI: the bare-verb hint for `move` now suggests the container flag, not a positional destination.** `things move <ref> <dest>` used to be answered with `did you mean: things todo move <ref> <dest>` — but `todo move` takes its destination as `--area`/`--project`/`--heading`, so the suggested command was itself a usage error. The hint now rewrites a stray positional destination behind the flag matching its kind (`things todo move <ref> --area <dest>`, or `--project` when it resolves to a project), giving a command that actually runs; a `move` that already uses a container flag is echoed unchanged. Caught by the agent-ergonomics bench. (`src/cli/verb-hint.ts`.)
- **Bench: fixture builder now maintains each project's leaf-action counters, so a project's read agrees with the write guards.** The per-row seed builders inserted a project and its child to-dos independently and left `untrashedLeafActionsCount` / `openUntrashedLeafActionsCount` at 0 — values the real Things app keeps in sync with the children. A project seeded with open children therefore read back `openUntrashedLeafActionsCount: 0` from `things projects --json` while the complete-project guard (which counts children live) correctly saw them, an internal disagreement no real database exhibits. `buildBenchFixture` now recomputes both counters after the whole tree is seeded (a leaf action is an untrashed to-do filed directly under the project or under one of its headings, matching the guards' open-children query). Caught by the agent-ergonomics bench (`recovery-project-children-policy`). (`bench/fixture.ts`.)
- **MCP: passing `limit` and `all: true` together no longer errors — `all` wins and `limit` is ignored.** The flat read tools (`read_view` on its flat views, `search`, `changes_since`) previously rejected the pair with a usage error; the bench's MCP tool-usage analysis found this combination was the single largest source of observed tool errors (45 of 161), almost all on `search` and `read_view`. The contract now resolves deterministically in favor of `all` (an explicit "everything" request, which already lifts every cap on its own — so no new result-size surprise), and both parameters' descriptions state the precedence. (A zod schema refinement was evaluated and rejected: the SDK surfaces a refinement failure as an unstructured `"Input validation error…"` string rather than this surface's structured `{code, message}` tool-error envelope, and it would keep the pair an error rather than eliminating it.) (`src/mcp/server.ts`.)
- **Fixed: `todaySection` is no longer emitted for items that are not in the Today view.** The read decode reported `todaySection: "today"` for every undated `start=active` to-do (real Things stores `startBucket=0` on ALL undated Anytime items, and future-scheduled Upcoming items carry it too), wrongly implying Today membership and contradicting the documented model (architecture.md; the MCP server note that an open, unscheduled `start=active` item is in Anytime). The field is now emitted ONLY for items actually in Today under the evaluation clock — `start=active`, dated, and not future-scheduled (`"evening"` for `startBucket=1`, else `"today"`; overdue scheduled items stay in Today and keep it); undated (Anytime) and future-dated (Upcoming) items OMIT the field. The Today gate rides the injected clock threaded through every read surface (`show`/`snapshot` now honor a pinned `THINGS_NOW`/`THINGS_TZ` for it too). Caught by the agent-ergonomics bench: subjects keyed on the field and mis-answered an Anytime to-do's view as "today". (`src/model/mappers.ts`.)
- **Fixed: write verification now gates `todaySection` on the injected clock, not the wall clock.** A regression introduced by the `todaySection` fix above: the verified read-after-write (`createDbReader` in `src/write/verify/delta.ts`) resolved `packedToday` from `new Date()` while the write planner dated the item from the injected clock. Under a pinned `THINGS_NOW` in the future (a consumer-timezone view, or the bench fence), a correct `todo add --when evening` (or `--when today`) dated pinned-today looked future-dated to the real-clock verify reader, which omitted `todaySection` — so the create-delta's `{ todaySection: "evening" }` assertion failed as `verify-failed:mismatch` on a write that had actually succeeded (and an agent's retry then duplicated state). The verify reader and the pre-read capture now ride `deps.now`/`deps.zone`, so verification judges Today membership under the SAME clock the mutation was planned on. Caught by the bench refiner's tool-defect classification. (`src/write/verify/delta.ts`, `src/write/pipeline.ts`.)
- **AGENTBENCH: bench sweeps abort loudly on a missing/empty `runs.jsonl` instead of defaulting metrics to zeros.** The metrics reader (`parseSweepRuns`) now hard-errors (`SweepParseError`) when a sweep's `runs.jsonl` is absent or empty, and the loop aborts with a clear message. Empty metrics mean zero successes, and the validation non-inferiority gate (`after.successes >= before.successes`) is then satisfied vacuously — so a lost sweep would silently promote a regression. Reads come from EXACTLY the directory the runner's `--out` wrote to (a locked-in invariant). (Diagnosis note: the `loop-cli-1` checkpoint's `validation baseline: 0/6, med tokIn 0` was **correct data**, not a path bug — the bare-CLI subject failed all six baseline validation runs, and friction/tokIn are measured over successful runs only; the `6` confirmed the right file was read. The skill arm's nonzero baseline was subject performance, not a code path difference.) The loop's first live batch showed the frontier refiner (`gpt-5.6-sol`) cannot reliably produce valid diff hunk math — patches died at `git apply` with "corrupt patch". The refiner contract is now `edits: [{file, find, replace}]` (each `find` an exact substring that must occur exactly once; `replace` may be empty) plus optional `creates: [{file, content}]` for new allowlisted files. A validation pass (allowlist membership + find-uniqueness + create-not-exists) runs before anything is written and applies atomically (all or none); a validation failure triggers ONE feedback retry with the specific per-edit errors appended to the refiner prompt, and a second failure records an `apply-failed` ledger entry (hypothesis preserved) rather than silently dropping the attempt. A real diff is still computed (`git diff`) after applying so the ledger, checkpoint, commit, and debrief keep showing diffs. The runner now records `tokensIn` as the TOTAL input the model processed — cache reads and writes re-added — instead of the provider's cache-discounted `usage.input` (pi-ai's Responses backend subtracts `cached_tokens`/`cache_write_tokens` before reporting `input`), and adds a `tokensInCached` field (the cache-read portion); the scorecard shows `tok_in` (total) beside a new `cached` column. This closes the round-0 anomaly where a cache-friendly arm's `tokensIn` (2.3k) fell far below its measured static context (16.6k). A new `--max-total-tokens <n>` runner flag (default 0 = unlimited) caps a sweep by accumulated total tokens (in+out): once exceeded it stops launching runs, records the remainder as skipped placeholders (ignored by the scorecard, never scored as failures), and exits with code 8. Coverage adds: a new dev task `bench/tasks/reasoning-standalone.json` (does the agent understand a to-do can belong to no project/area and still be actionable) and a block of standalone (container-less) anytime/someday/scheduled to-dos in the evergreen world profile (`bench/world.ts`).
- **AGENTBENCH: the refinement-loop driver (`bench/loop.ts`, Phase 2)** — per arm (`cli`/`skill`/`mcp`), it drives the runner as a subprocess to bench the dev+validation splits, digests the dev failures (failed + high-friction runs, with a heuristic pre-classification and NO validation/holdout content), asks a frontier refiner (`--refiner-model`, single-turn via the codex OAuth store) for the smallest generalizable patch, enforces a per-arm file allowlist, gates on `npm run fmt` + `npm run check`, re-benches, and accepts or reverts strictly by the [constitution](bench/CONSTITUTION.md) metric ladder (safety → success → friction → tokens); accepted patches are committed one-per-iteration on the working branch, gui-semantic skill edits are parked as `needs-mike.patch` instead of auto-accepted, and it stops after `--max-iterations` or two consecutive no-accepts, then runs the holdout once and writes `checkpoint.md`. A usage fail-safe caps cumulative subject+refiner tokens (`--token-budget`, default 12M): a prominent warning at 60% and a clean, resumable abort at 100% (exit 8) or after 5 consecutive provider errors (exit 9), both finalizing state + checkpoint with the holdout skipped. The refiner sits behind an interface and every git/bench/gate seam is injected, so the allowlist, gate-revert, decision-math, gui, budget, and abort paths are unit-tested with fakes (`test/unit/bench-loop.test.ts`).
- **AGENTBENCH: post-hoc debriefs + a per-arm knowledge ledger.** After every re-bench (accepted and reverted alike) a second single-turn refiner call attributes the measured delta and distills one transferable lesson (never blocking — a parse/provider failure is recorded as `debrief-failed`); each candidate becomes an append-only entry (rejected and parked included) in its arm's ledger under [`bench/ledger/`](bench/ledger/README.md) (`cli.md` / `skill.md` / `mcp.md` — per-arm so parallel loops never conflict at integration), committed at batch end so the ledger rides the arm's PR. Each refiner charter is now seeded with that arm's most recent lessons (own-arm only, ~15 newest capped ~2k tokens; cross-arm transfer stays human-mediated at integration checkpoints). Holdout hygiene holds by construction — lessons derive from the dev digest and metric numbers, never task text.
- **AGENTBENCH: the runner can authenticate against a ChatGPT subscription** (`--provider openai-codex`, OpenAI's OAuth "openai-codex" provider) as an alternative to `--provider openai` + `OPENAI_API_KEY`. `npm run bench:login` runs the OAuth flow once and stores the token at `~/.config/things-api-bench/auth.json` (0600, outside the repo) via a file-backed pi-ai `CredentialStore`; the agent refreshes it per turn through `getApiKey`, and a run with no stored credential fails fast pointing back at the login command. See [bench/README.md](bench/README.md) for the flow plus the subscription-rate-cap and softer-model-pinning caveats.
- **Simulation fencing hardened to fail closed on the database itself, not just env vars**: a DB carrying the bench-fixture marker can never be paired with the real write transports (`defaultVectors` throws), the reveal `open` withholds when the database in use is a marked fixture (even via bare `--db`), the simulator fence additionally requires scratch `THINGS_API_STATE_DIR`/`THINGS_API_CONFIG_DIR` (so bench runs cannot touch the operator's audit trail/config) and pins the modeled schema generation (`SIMULATED_DATABASE_VERSION` — a Things schema bump refuses simulation until the modeling chain is re-verified; see the drift runbook's new lockstep step). The bench runner refuses to start any task until a zero-dispatch `--dry-run` canary proves the child CLI compiles writes to the simulator.
- **AGENTBENCH: evergreen world-profile fixtures** — bench runs now seed a deterministic, lived-in synthetic library (areas, mixed-state projects, ~3 years of logbook history, checklists, reminders, repeating templates incl. nth-weekday rules) under every task's own seeds, with all dates as offsets from the pinned clock so the corpus never goes stale; `--world-seed` rotates the inventory, `--no-world` disables (`bench/world.ts`).
- **AGENTBENCH: an agent-ergonomics bench + refinement loop now lives under [`bench/`](bench/README.md)** — synthetic-fixture task runs measuring how well zero-context agents use the CLI help system, the new agent skill ([`skills/things-cli/`](skills/things-cli/SKILL.md), draft v0), and the MCP surface, graded deterministically (metric ladder: safety → success → friction → context → calls; see [bench/CONSTITUTION.md](bench/CONSTITUTION.md)). Supporting it, a **fenced simulator write vector** (`src/write/vectors/simulator.ts`) applies planned mutations directly to a marked synthetic fixture DB so the full write pipeline (guards, verification, audit) runs end-to-end on a host with no Things app; it activates only behind `THINGS_SIM_WRITES=1` + an explicit `THINGS_DB` + the fixture marker, and is inert for every normal consumer.

- **Consumer-timezone / pinned-clock views.** Two environment knobs, read by both the CLI and the MCP server process, let a consumer evaluate every date boundary (today / evening / upcoming grouping + `--since`/`--until` clipping / `--overdue` / the logbook sweep / `changes --since` / inbox created-date bounds) for THEIR calendar instead of the host's: `THINGS_TZ` (an IANA zone, e.g. `Asia/Tokyo`) and `THINGS_NOW` (an ISO-8601 instant pinning "now", a determinism/testing knob). Over MCP the date-sensitive tools (`read_view`, `search`, `changes_since`, `get_project`, `get_area`, `list_collections`, and the write tools that accept `when`) also take a per-call `tz` that overrides `THINGS_TZ`. Precedence: per-call `tz` > `THINGS_TZ` > host zone; invalid values **fail closed** with a usage error (exit 2 / MCP `usage`), never a silent host fallback. **Additive JSON shape:** `--json` envelopes and MCP responses carry `meta.clock { timezone, today }` **only** when a consumer zone or pinned now is in effect — absent on the host clock, so the wire shape is unchanged for existing consumers. **Writes normalize before dispatch:** a consumer-zone `when: today` resolves to that zone's explicit `YYYY-MM-DD` (reminder appended as `<date>@<time>`) so verification agrees by construction (a consumer-today that is host-yesterday lands in Today as overdue-start — coherent), while `when: evening` — a bucket that exists only for the app machine's own current day — is refused fail-closed (`blocked:clock`, exit 4) when the consumer's day differs. Reminder times stay wall-clock and are never translated. Internal undo/reorder machinery deliberately stays on the host clock. Docs: [docs/design/contracts.md](docs/design/contracts.md) → "Consumer clock" (incl. the host-timezone-alignment deployment note).
- **`heading.convert-to-project` is now lab-certified** (HEADCERT1) — the last ui-vector op that shipped uncertified. It was blocked because `things:///show?id=<heading>` does not select a heading (the reveal URL selects to-dos only, so Convert to Project… stayed disabled); the recipe now reveals the heading's **parent project** and selects the heading as a content-table row via the pure-AX row `select` action, addressed by position among the project's headings (heading rows expose no stable AX title). The op is unchanged for callers — same command (`things heading convert-to-project <ref> --dangerously-drive-gui`, MCP `convert_to_project`), same identity-replacement/irreversible semantics — but `things capabilities` and `things doctor` now report it `lab-certified` and a successful drive no longer carries the "failed certification" note. **Every ui-vector op is now lab-certified** against Things 3.22.11. Verified end-to-end through the production CLI in a VM clone (both headings of a two-heading project converted, children reparented, gating enforced). See [docs/lab/headcert1-certification.md](docs/lab/headcert1-certification.md).

## 0.10.0 — 2026-07-16

- **BREAKING (JSON shape): `meta.grouped.blocks` is now identity-carrying and nested** (#188) — each block reports `{ kind, ref, title, shown, total, limit }` (the former `uuid` field is now `ref`), project blocks nest inside their area/loose block under `children` (anytime item-lists; someday's active-project child groups), and a fully-truncated block still appears with `shown: 0`. The split `today` view's `meta.truncation` gains a per-section breakdown (`sections: [{ key: "today"|"evening", shown, total }]`).
- **BREAKING (library): the bounded read shapes no longer expose a pre-truncation `full` view** (#188) — `BoundedTodayView`/`BoundedSectionsView`/`BoundedAreaView` are `{ view, truncation | grouped }`; the human renderers derive every hidden-count hint from the enriched metadata above.
- **Fixed: `area show` caps only the ACTIVE project rows** (#188) — future-scheduled and someday project rows always survive into the card's Upcoming/Someday sections (previously a raw slice could drop them from an area with more than 30 projects).

- **The audit trail now survives a crash mid-write** (#180): every mutation records an intent marker immediately before the app is touched and its outcome after read-after-write verification, so a process killed between the two leaves evidence instead of an applied-but-unrecorded change — `things doctor` reports any started-but-unrecorded changes for reconciliation. Audit appends are now flushed to disk as a single atomic write (previously unflushed, tear-prone under concurrent writers), and unreadable lines encountered when reading history are reported on STDERR instead of dropped silently. Intent markers are never undo targets. (Companion test sweep #181: all 48 MCP tools are now driven through the protocol layer in tests.)

- **`undo` now refuses an inverse that would overwrite a container, status, schedule, or trashed-state change made outside things-api since the original change** (#177) — previously only title/notes/deadline/reminder/tags were guarded, so a manual move/reopen/reschedule was silently clobbered by a later undo. Pass `--acknowledge-out-of-band-changes` (MCP `acknowledge_out_of_band_changes`) to overwrite anyway; `--dry-run` previews the would-block outcome. Axes with no captured after-state (cascade children, repeat state, backdated timestamps, reorder ranks) fall back to the inverse's own verified re-read, documented at the guard.
- **MCP writes are now attributed to the connecting client's own handshake identity** (#178): the audit actor is `mcp:<client-name>` derived from the MCP initialize `clientInfo` (lowercased/slugged, capped at 32 chars; bare `mcp` when absent) and is not settable by any tool argument, so concurrent client sessions are distinguishable in the audit trail and to `things undo --by`. **BREAKING:** the `undo` tool's default `by` follows it — each client undoes its own writes by default; audit records written by older versions carry actor `mcp` and no longer match the new default (pass `by: "mcp"` explicitly, or `"*"`, to reach them).
- **`things mcp --allow-disruptive` / `--allow-very-disruptive` set the daemon's disruption ceiling once at startup** (#178) — a hard limit for the process lifetime; there is deliberately no per-request escalation, no config tool, and no delivery-mechanism override over MCP (restart the daemon with different flags to change policy; doctrine recorded in docs/design/architecture.md).
- **BREAKING (MCP): all tool arguments are now snake_case** (#178). Repeat-rule args (`afterCompletion`→`after_completion`, `monthlyDay`→`monthly_day`, `monthlyWeekday`→`monthly_weekday`, `monthlyOrdinal`→`monthly_ordinal`, `yearlyMonth`→`yearly_month`, `endsAfter`→`ends_after`, `endsOn`→`ends_on`, `startDaysEarlier`→`start_days_earlier`) and `batch` per-op `options` (`acknowledgeChecklistReset`→`acknowledge_checklist_reset`, `acknowledgeProjectReopen`→`acknowledge_project_reopen`, `dangerouslyPermanent`→`dangerously_permanent`, `acknowledgeTagSubtree`→`acknowledge_tag_subtree`) renamed with no back-compat aliases.
- **Docs: README rewritten against 0.9.0 reality and pinned with contract tests** (#175) — corrected version, catalog size (28→49 ops), the `--help` description, and the MCP tool enumeration; documented the Accessibility GUI vector, its gates, and its certification model. `test/unit/readme-contract.test.ts` re-derives the version, op count, tool names, and relative links from source. Companion docs reconciliation in #176 (gaps.md un-contradicted + acknowledged-absences section, ui-vector.md corrected against the certification manifest, surface-copy policy scoped to descriptions/help).
- **Adversarial-review fixes (2026-07-16, PRs #167–#173).** A seven-agent review pass over the whole codebase, with the confirmed defects fixed the same day:
  - **Stealing a stale mutation lock is now atomic** (#169). Two concurrent invocations racing to steal a dead process's lock could previously both conclude they held it (unlink-then-recreate); the steal now goes through a `rename` exactly one racer can win, with a post-rename holder re-check that restores a live lock rather than clobbering it.
  - **`things batch` now exits 6 (Unsupported) when a batch's only failures are unsupported ops** (#170) — previously it fell through to 3 (VerifyFailed), violating the stable exit-code contract. Aggregate precedence is drift > blocked > unsupported > verify-failed, now built by one shared helper; the `blocked:*` / `verify-failed:*` error-code strings are likewise constructed in exactly one place (`contracts.ts`) across the CLI envelope, MCP errors, and the audit trail (emitted strings unchanged).
  - **Create verification can no longer bind to a pre-existing same-title item** (#172). All nine create-discovery ops (`todo.add`, `project.add`, duplicates, `heading.create`, the make-repeating and convert-to-project families) now exclude the rows that already carried the probe title before the write, so a silently-failed create cannot verify green against — or record the uuid of — an unrelated same-title item that appeared in the discovery window.
  - **`search` treats `%` and `_` literally** (#173) — a query like `my_task` no longer wildcard-matches `myXtask`, and `50%` no longer matches every `50…`; applies to title/notes search and the did-you-mean fallback.
  - **Every read view now honors the injected clock consistently** (#173). The logbook-sweep boundary (the `logged` flag, Logbook membership) previously fell back to wall-clock time in most views even when a caller pinned the clock; all views now thread one clock through both the SQL boundary and row materialization.
  - **An unscheduled to-do pulled into Today by a due or overdue deadline now stars (★) in Anytime** (#173), matching the app.
  - **Reads now surface a non-blocking warning when the Things database schema no longer matches the version this build was validated against** (or its `databaseVersion` is unrecognized) (#171): a one-line STDERR note in human output, `meta.warnings` in the `--json` envelope, and the same note in MCP read-tool results. Reads stay best-effort and never block; `things doctor` has the detail. (Writes were already hard-gated.)

- **Dev: the `no-await-in-loop` lint rule is disabled and all 54 suppressions removed.** The rule's parallelize-your-IO advice does not fit this codebase — nearly every awaited loop is intentionally sequential (ordered write-pipeline legs, per-hop DB asserts, VM/UI drive steps, CI/poll loops) — so it produced only noise and repeated suppression churn. `.oxlintrc.json` now sets `no-await-in-loop: "off"`, and the per-site `oxlint-disable-next-line` directives are gone (their sequencing rationale kept as plain comments). No runtime behavior changed; no `Promise.all` was rewritten back into a loop.
- **BREAKING (JSON shape): read output now omits empty optional fields.** In the `data` of every `--json` read and every MCP read tool, an entity drops any optional field that is empty — a to-do with no deadline has no `deadline` key, one with no tags has no `tags` key, an empty checklist/notes/reminder/area/project is simply absent, and a root tag has no `parent` key. **Read a missing key as unset/empty/default — absent and empty are now identical, and a consumer must not distinguish them** (`item.tags ?? []`, `item.deadline == null`). This is a breaking change for any consumer that previously tested for an empty-array or `null` presence. The identity keys (`uuid`, `type`, and the `title`) are always present, and a semantically meaningful `false` (e.g. `logged`, `trashed`) or `0` count (e.g. `checklistItemsCount`) is never omitted. View scaffolding is preserved: the `today`/`evening` split, the project/area card sections, and a sidebar section's `area: null` (the "loose block" marker) stay even when empty. **`inheritedTags` reverses its earlier always-present ruling** — it is now absent when empty on the detail reads and present only when non-empty (as a plain array of tag names; see the tag-policy entry below). The error envelope, mutation results, and diagnostic payloads (`doctor`/`capabilities`/`config`) are unaffected. Full contract: [docs/design/contracts.md](docs/design/contracts.md) → "Omit-empty".

- **New `--overdue` filter surfaces open items past their deadline.** A content scope (like `--tag`): `--overdue` keeps only OPEN items whose deadline is strictly before today — **due today is not overdue**, mirroring the app's Today badge, where an equal-to-today deadline reads as "due" and only an earlier one as "overdue". It composes as AND with `--tag`/`--untagged` and never lifts a `--limit`/`--since`/`--until` default. Offered on the current-work views `today`, `inbox`, `anytime`, `someday`, and `search` (on `search` it lists open items, so it is refused together with `--logged`/`--trashed`/`--all`). Deliberately not offered on `upcoming` (a forward-looking view — nothing in it is past its deadline) or `logbook`/`trash` (closed items). The MCP `read_view` and `search` tools gain a matching `overdue` argument with the same guards.
- **`--overdue` now also scopes the container/list views, with OWN-DEADLINE semantics.** On `things projects`, `project show`, and `area show` (and their `things <id>` sugar) `--overdue` keeps each listed entity iff its OWN deadline is overdue — no recursion into container contents. `things projects` keeps projects whose own deadline is past (projects carry a `deadline` like to-dos); `project show` filters the project's child to-dos to the overdue ones and collapses any heading left empty (the project header still renders); `area show` filters the area's loose to-dos AND its child projects by each row's own deadline, without descending into project contents (an overdue to-do inside a non-overdue project does not surface — that is `project show --overdue`). Empty results render the view's normal empty state. It stays a content scope: it never lifts a `--limit` or the per-section caps, and the container "no strict `--limit`" doctrine is unchanged. `things areas` (the area LIST) rejects `--overdue` fail-closed — an area is not a dated entity — though `things areas <ref>` (a synonym of `area show`) accepts it. MCP parity: `get_project`, `get_area`, and `list_collections` (projects only; `areas`/`tags` rejected) gain the matching `overdue` argument.
- **Tag filters gain multi-tag AND and now scope the container views (§9a resolved), with a single view-dependent `--tag`.** Two orthogonal axes decide how a tag reaches an item: CONTAINER inheritance (an item carries its project/area/heading tags) and DESCENDANT expansion (filtering a parent tag matches items on a child tag). `--exact-tag` drops descendant expansion; container inheritance is **view-dependent**. In the FLAT views (`today`, `inbox`, `anytime`, `someday`, `upcoming`, `search`, `logbook`, **and the `things projects` LIST**) `--tag X` is inheritance-inclusive — X directly, X inherited from a container, or a descendant of X — and `--untagged` means no tag at all. In the SINGLE-CONTAINER views (`project show`, `area show`, and their `things <id>` / `things projects <ref>` / `things areas <ref>` sugar) `--tag X` instead matches a displayed row carrying X **directly** (project show → its child to-dos; area show → its loose to-dos AND its child projects), with no recursion into a matched container, and `--untagged` means "no direct tag on the row" — because every child inherits the one container's tags, so an inheritance-inclusive filter there is vacuous. **`--tag` is repeatable and ANDs** — `--tag foo --tag bar` keeps items matching both. Each tag ref resolves by name/path (tag uuids are internal); an unknown ref fails closed per ref. `--untagged` is mutually exclusive with the tag-presence flags. The bare `things areas` LIST rejects the tag filters (it shows areas with their own direct tags). This resolves the parked **§9a** question: the projects list stays flat (projects sit in heterogeneous areas — inherited tags are useful there), while the two single-container views mirror the app's own in-project filter bar (direct-only). MCP parity: `tag` is an ARRAY (a breaking change for a caller that passed a single string), plus `exact_tag`/`untagged`, on `read_view`/`search`/`get_project`/`get_area`/`list_collections` (projects only; areas/tags reject them), with `get_project`/`get_area` applying the single-container (direct) semantics. Semantics: [docs/design/cli-grammar.md](docs/design/cli-grammar.md) → "The tag filters".
- **BREAKING: the `--direct-tag` / `--direct-untagged` flags (and MCP `direct_tag` / `direct_untagged`) are removed.** The direct-only behavior they provided is now folded into the view-dependent `--tag` / `--untagged` above (direct-on-the-row in `project show` / `area show`), so a separate flag is unnecessary. Passing `--direct-tag` / `--direct-untagged` now errors as an unknown option; the MCP inputs are gone from the schemas. `--tag`, `--exact-tag`, and `--untagged` are unchanged in name.
- **BREAKING (JSON shape): `inheritedTags` drops its provenance and is now a plain array of tag names.** On the detail reads (`todo show` / `project show` / `get_item` / `get_project`) each inherited tag was `{ tag: {title}, source: {type, uuid, title} }`; it is now just `{ title }` — the same `TagRef` shape as `tags`, a flat list parallel to it. The human `inherited:` line likewise renders plain dim names (`#home #important`) with no `‹project X›` / `‹area Y›` provenance chip. The two lists (direct `tags`, inherited `inheritedTags`) stay distinct and are never merged.
- **Behavior change: a to-do's `area` now reports the EFFECTIVE area.** Previously `area` echoed only the raw `t.area` column, so a to-do nested in a project (or under a heading) reported no area even though the app shows one. It now resolves the nearest area up the chain — the to-do's own area, else its project's area, else its heading's project's area — so a to-do in a project-in-an-area emits `area: <that area>`, a loose to-do in an area emits its direct area, and an inbox to-do (no project/area) still emits no `area`. A project's `area` is its own (areas are not inherited), unchanged. Whether the area is direct vs inherited stays derivable from whether `project`/`heading` is set. This restores useful area context that the omit-empty change above would otherwise hide for nested to-dos.
- **Every error and refusal now honors `--json`, and disambiguation candidates are machine-readable.** Previously some flag/argument errors and every reference-resolution failure printed prose to stderr and ignored `--json`; now they all emit the standard `{ok:false, error:{code, message, details?}}` envelope on stdout under `--json` (prose to stderr otherwise), through one shared emitter. An ambiguous or not-found write/read target — `things project update <ambiguous-name> --json`, a partial-uuid that matches several items — carries `error.code` `ambiguous`/`not-found` (exit 2) with `error.details.candidates` as data (`{uuid, title, context?}`), so an agent self-corrects without re-parsing the message; the same structured detail rides the MCP tool result. Flag-combination errors, bad `--limit`, unparseable date bounds, `--type`, empty `--db`, and the bare-verb hint (`error.details.suggestions`) are all `--json`-aware. MCP already inherited the name / partial-uuid write-target sugar via the shared pipeline; that is now covered by tests. The full path × code × details contract is documented in [docs/design/contracts.md](docs/design/contracts.md).
- **Tag uuids are now a fully internal implementation detail — surfaced nowhere and accepted nowhere.** Like checklist-item ids, tag uuids never appear in output (`things tags`, `things areas`, the `tags`/`inheritedTags` fields on any read, JSON and MCP alike) and are no longer accepted as tag references on writes. Tags are addressed by **name** or a **`parent/child` path** only (names are app-enforced-unique, TAGW1-c). Rationale: a Things uuid is alphanumeric and indistinguishable from a plausible tag name, and tags are applied by passing the NAME to the app's own write vector — so the app resolves the name exactly as its GUI does, and we never pick a uuid. A duplicate-name pair (a Cloud-sync-only pathological state) therefore needs no special handling: the name passes through and the app resolves it, matching the GUI — the previous fail-closed duplicate-tag refusal is removed. `--create-tags` / `create_tags` still creates missing names (a name that happens to look like a uuid is created verbatim — no shape heuristic). Post-write verification is unchanged: it already asserts the tag SET landed by title.
- **`things tags` now renders as an indented tree.** The taxonomy listing shows each tag's leaf name indented two spaces per nesting level (mirroring the Things sidebar), with no uuid column and no ancestor-path prefix — a deeply nested tag reads at its true depth, and each displayed leaf name is directly usable as a tag reference (names are globally unique). Canonical depth-first order is unchanged (children follow their parent, siblings by `TMTag."index"` then uuid). Over `--json`/MCP each tag object carries `parent` = the parent tag's NAME (null for a root) so a consumer can reconstruct the tree; no uuid field.
- **The MCP project-write tools now say they accept a project name, matching the CLI.** `update_project`, `set_project_status`, `move_project`, `make_project_repeating`, `reschedule_project_repeat`, and `set_project_repeat_state` describe their target as `uuid or unique name` (the same forms the CLI's `things project <verb>` took as of the previous change — the shared write pipeline already accepted a name, only the tool descriptions lagged). To-do and heading write tools keep their uuid-only targets. The two remaining hand-written `no record with uuid …` misses (the `get_item` lookup and the write target-not-found guard) now read `no <entity> matching uuid or partial-uuid "X"`, the same wording the resolvers use.
- **A tag can now be named by a `parent/child` path, not just its bare title — anywhere tags are accepted** (`things todo add --tags`, `things todo tags --set/--add`, `things project tags`, `things area add/update`, and the matching MCP tools). Each comma-separated tag value resolves as: an exact title (a title containing a literal `/`, like `sl/ash`, wins as-is — **literal beats path**); else a `parent/child` chain walked from the root to the leaf. The applied/stored tag is always the leaf's own title. Tags are addressed by name only — tag uuids are internal (see the tag-uuid entry above).
- **Unknown tags can be created in place with `--create-tags` (CLI) / `create_tags` (MCP), instead of stopping.** Without it, naming a tag that does not exist still fails closed (the app would silently drop it — a partial write). With it, every missing tag is created first through the normal tag-creation path and then applied; a `parent/child` path creates any missing parents too (mkdir-p, parents first), and re-running is a no-op (creating a tag that already exists coalesces to it). The unknown-tag error now names `--create-tags` as the fix.
- **A tag name that matches two different tags is passed through to the app, which resolves it — exactly as its GUI does.** Two tags cannot share a name through any Things write surface, so this only happens after a Things Cloud sync merge. Because tags are applied by passing the NAME to the app's own write vector, we never pick a uuid — the app resolves the name itself, so a duplicate name behaves identically to selecting that tag in the GUI. (An earlier iteration this cycle refused duplicate names and listed uuid candidates; that was removed along with all tag-uuid surfacing.)
- **`things area add --tags` now confirms the created area actually carries the tags** (the same post-write tag-set check the other tag-setting commands already do) — a silent drop of a tag on area creation is no longer reported as success.
